# Audit Log

Yuzu records every operator action as a structured audit event. The audit log
provides an access-controlled, retention-managed trail suitable for compliance reporting, incident
investigation, and forwarding to external SIEM platforms such as Splunk or
Elastic.

> **Fixed (#2579), and it affected upgrades only.** A retention pass that began
> with NO stored clock reading declined nothing on its own account, so a host
> whose clock was ALREADY skewed forward when it first ran the guard could delete
> expired rows unremarked. Such a pass now declines once, warns, and anchors the
> reading; the next pass proceeds normally. A correct clock was never at risk, and
> a fresh install with nothing expired does not decline. If you ran an affected
> version with a wrong clock, rows deleted then are not recoverable without a
> backup taken before it. Detail under [Limits](#the-retention-clock-guard).

> **Known issue in v0.9.0 (advisory YZA-2026-001):** audit rows for requests
> authenticated via `Authorization: Bearer` or `X-Yuzu-Token` (every MCP tool
> call and every REST automation using an API token) had an empty `principal`
> field. Cookie-authenticated dashboard activity was unaffected. Fixed forward
> in v0.10.0; pre-fix rows are not backfilled. Operators auditing a window that
> spans the v0.9.0 → v0.10.0 boundary should expect a bimodal `principal`
> distribution. See `CHANGELOG.md` for full remediation context.

## Storage

Audit events are persisted in the server's PostgreSQL substrate under the
dedicated schema `audit_store` (ADR-0006/0040) — the same database that backs the
rest of the server's stores, not a separate file. There is **no SQLite
fallback**: if the substrate is unreachable at boot the server fails closed and
refuses to start.

Because the audit log is the SOC 2 evidence chain, its write and read paths have
deliberately asymmetric failure postures:

- **Writes fail hard.** A persistence failure is never silently dropped — the
  write returns an error to the caller. Behavioural-PII REST routes turn that
  into `503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and serve **no** data, so evidence-less
  PII is never recorded as audited.
- **Reads degrade to DENY.** A store or connection-pool failure on a query
  returns `503`, never a false-empty `200`. A reviewer or SIEM can never mistake
  an infrastructure blip for "no audit activity."

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Retention period | 365 days | Events older than this are deleted by background cleanup |
| Cleanup interval | 1 hour | How often the sweeper prunes expired events (fleet-wide, single-sweeper — see [The retention clock guard](#the-retention-clock-guard)) |

## Event structure

Every audit event contains the following fields:

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `timestamp` | integer (Unix epoch) | When the event occurred |
| `principal` | string | Username of the operator who performed the action (`agent:<agent_id>` on a row the server writes from an agent's own report, such as `registry.write`). Empty (as are `principal_role` and `session_id`) on a row built from the request after a step-up-gated request changed principal mid-request, because the later resolution yields no session; `principal_class` still reflects the presented credential, and rows that name their principal explicitly keep it |
| `principal_role` | string | Role of the principal at the time of the action (`admin`, `user`; `agent` on `registry.write` rows) |
| `action` | string | Dot-notated action identifier (see table below) |
| `target_type` | string | Category of the affected object (`Tag`, `Agent`, `Session`, etc.) |
| `target_id` | string | Identifier of the affected object |
| `detail` | string | Human-readable description or the new value |
| `source_ip` | string | IP address of the client that initiated the request |
| `user_agent` | string | HTTP User-Agent header value |
| `session_id` | string | For dashboard requests, the SHA-256 hex digest (64 characters) of the `yuzu_session` cookie the request presented — the value the session store keys on (`token_hash`). Empty when the request carried no cookie (API-token, Bearer and `X-Yuzu-Token` calls normally have none); when the cookie did not resolve a session on the session-resolving path (an expired or unknown cookie; the `auth.logout` row, written after the session is invalidated); and on a few hand-built rows that do not stamp it (`api_token.create`/`api_token.revoke`, `management_group.create`/`management_group.delete`). Pre-session sign-in rows (login, MFA, OIDC/SAML callback) carry the digest of any cookie presented. If a token call also presents a cookie, the digest is of that cookie, which may not be the credential that authenticated the call; `principal` is authoritative. Agent-session rows (`session.*` actions written by the gRPC service) carry the agent session identifier when the request presented one, otherwise empty. Rows written by an earlier release, or by a server still on one during a rolling upgrade, hold the value recorded at the time; the two forms look alike and are separated by `timestamp` against each server's upgrade date. The audit readers do not return this column; read it from the database. |
| `result` | string | Outcome: `success`, `denied`, or `failure` (or `attempted`, the audit-first row an operation writes before it changes anything, or `no_change`, the outcome row of an idempotent request that found nothing to change). See **Result vocabulary** below the actions table for the precise semantics of each value. **On the behavioural-PII access-audit rows** (`dex.device.view`, `dex.signal.view`, `guardian.device.view`, and siblings — #4858), `result=success` does NOT uniformly mean "data was disclosed"; see that same section for the pre-read-vs-post-read split. |

## Logged actions

The following actions are recorded automatically. No operator configuration is
required.

> **MCP `mcp.query_responses` emits a pair of rows on a scope-filtered call.** When
> an agentic worker collects responses it is only partly entitled to (some agents
> outside its management groups), the tool writes a `result=denied` row (the
> access-boundary event — `detail` carries the distinct dropped-agent count) **and**
> a `result=success` row (the served, possibly-empty set), both under the same
> principal + correlation key. A SIEM rule must treat these as a pair for one call:
> the `denied` row is informational CC6.1 access-boundary evidence (an operator
> reached toward agents outside its scope and was filtered), **not** a failed call —
> do not alert on `mcp.query_responses` denials as errors on a scoped multi-operator
> deployment.

> **The seven `/crypto` MCP tools write a generic `mcp.<tool>` row on two more paths.**
> A rejected argument (an unknown key, a wrongly typed value, a bad cursor) writes
> `result=denied`, `detail` `invalid arguments: <reason>`; the `<reason>` is fixed text and
> never echoes the caller's input. A burst of these from one principal is a misbehaving
> client, not a gap in access control. A tool whose provider, caller identity or audit sink is
> not wired answers a non-retryable internal error (the response says only `service
> unavailable`, or a `... unavailable` data message) and, when an audit sink exists, writes
> `result=failure`: a `detail` ending `provider unwired` is a deployment fault that cannot
> occur on a correctly started server, while `caller identity unresolved` is usually a
> credential revoked or expired between authentication and the tool call, which can occur on a
> healthy server. With no audit sink there is no row to write, and the four tools that write a
> `crypto.*.view` row and `set_crypto_dashboard_layout` refuse the call instead of succeeding
> without one. A row that fails to persist on a working sink is different: those tools still
> answer, with `audit_persisted:false`. An `export_crypto_cbom` random-number failure answers a
> retryable internal error and writes no row.

> **Per-principal quota-cap rejections (PR 4.4, ADR-1005 class engine
> principals) are deliberately NOT audited.** When an **engine principal**
> (`principal_kind=="engine"`, username `engine:<slug>`) exceeds its
> per-principal concurrency or rate cap, the server's pre-routing chokepoint
> returns `429` (REST A4 envelope / MCP JSON-RPC `-32010`, both transports —
> see `docs/user-manual/rest-api.md` and `docs/user-manual/mcp.md`) and
> increments `yuzu_server_principal_quota_exhausted_total{side,limit}`
> (`docs/user-manual/metrics.md`) — it writes **no** audit row. This is a
> deliberate fail-visibility choice, not an oversight: a quota rejection is a
> high-frequency, pre-handler operational event — an engine principal running
> hot against its own cap, expected under normal steady-state load — not a
> forensically interesting action against a resource. Auditing every
> rejection would flood the audit store under a busy or misconfigured engine
> principal with no compliance benefit; the counter (SIEM-routable,
> `absent()`-safe pre-seeded series) is the intended observability surface.
> Compare with `yuzu_onbehalf_rejected_total` and `yuzu_auth_lockout_blocked_
> total` (see `docs/observability-conventions.md`), which follow the same
> metric-is-the-signal / no-per-rejection-audit-row pattern for the same
> anti-flood reason.

> **Agent OTA admission rejections (#913) are deliberately NOT audited, but
> OTA identity rejections ARE.** The two halves of the OTA gate are treated
> differently on purpose. When a peer exceeds its per-peer concurrency or rate
> bound, `DownloadUpdate` returns gRPC `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` and increments
> `yuzu_ota_download_admission_total{decision}`
> (`docs/user-manual/metrics.md`) with **no** audit row — the same
> fail-visibility reasoning as the per-principal quota block above: a peer
> running hot against its own bound is expected steady-state behaviour during
> a fleet-wide update, and auditing every rejection would flood the store
> during exactly the event it is least useful for. By contrast, a rejection
> from the **identity** half — no usable client certificate, a certificate
> from a foreign CA, or an `agent_id` that does not match the certificate —
> writes a `session.ota_identity_rejected` row, because that is an
> authentication failure against a specific enrolled identity and is
> forensically interesting exactly once. Metric is the signal; the audit row
> is the evidence — and the row is rate-limited per peer while the metric is
> not, so under a flood the counter is the complete record and the rows are a
> sample of it. See the row's entry in the table below.


| Action | Target type | When |
|---|---|---|
| `auth.login` | Session | Operator logs in via the dashboard or API |
| `role.elevation.granted` | User | A pre-authorized operator activated a JIT admin elevation via `POST /api/v1/elevate` (SOC 2 CC6.3/CC6.6). `principal`=the operator, `principal_role=admin` (the effective role for the window), `result=ok`, `detail=duration_secs=<N> mfa=<oidc_amr\|local_totp> expires_at=<RFC3339 UTC> justification=<sanitised, ≤1KiB>` — the machine-parsed `mfa=` and `expires_at=` tokens are placed **before** the free-text `justification=` field so a crafted justification can never forge either token a first-match grep reads. `duration_secs` is the TRUE post-clamp window, and `expires_at` reflects any clamp to the session's own absolute lifetime (follow-up B). The grant was MFA-step-up-gated; `mfa=oidc_amr` records that the second factor was an IdP-MFA assertion (OIDC `amr` claim) rather than local TOTP enrollment — an OIDC session's factor is NEVER a local namesake account's TOTP enrollment (the two identity sources are checked on disjoint code paths). |
| `role.elevation.denied` | User | `POST /api/v1/elevate` was refused. `result=denied`, `detail` names the reason, with **distinct reasons per identity source and cause** (never a single generic message): "not eligible" / "eligibility read failed" (fail-closed; also the outcome for an OIDC identity with no `users` row at all); **`identity-source mismatch`** (the session's `auth_source` does not match the target row's `identity_source` — closes a cross-protocol collision where a crafted SAML NameID, or a legacy local row, shares a principal string with a real OIDC identity; #1852 governance round); "identity-source lookup failed" (fail-closed — the eligible row vanished between the eligibility read and the source-match lookup, e.g. a concurrent deprovisioning); for a **local** session, "no MFA enrolled" (no TOTP enrollment); for an **OIDC** session, "no MFA in SSO login (the IdP did not assert amr MFA)" (the current login carries no seeded `amr` proof) or "OIDC-amr elevation is disabled (--no-jit-oidc-amr-elevation)" (the toggle is off — OIDC sessions cannot elevate at all when disabled, they cannot fall back to a local TOTP step-up); "eligibility revoked during step-up" (won a TOCTOU race); "mfa_step_up_refused" (enrolled/proof present but stale). |
| `mfa.step_up.required` | Endpoint | One of the 37 step-up-gated REST and dashboard routes refused a request before the route ran (the central MFA step-up check, SOC 2 CC6.6). `target_id` is the route's action label. **`result=error` for every refusal, a policy denial included**: it is never `result=denied`, unlike a per-route handler denial. For a stale or absent proof the detail is `user=<name> age_secs=<N> window_secs=<N> cid=<id> stage=pre_authz` (`age_secs=-1` when the session has no proof at all); every other refusal is `user=<name> reason=<token> cid=<id> stage=pre_authz`. In every `mfa.step_up.required` row, whether written by the central check or from inside a handler (including the approver gate's own challenge), the `user=` value is neutralised before it is embedded: a space, `=`, `,`, DEL or control byte in the account name is written as `_`. An SSO account name can contain those characters, so a SIEM rule that reads `user=` must match the neutralised form, and a name cannot add a `reason=` token of its own. `cid` equals the response's `X-Correlation-Id` header and the body's `correlation_id`, so a reported refusal joins to its row, and `stage=pre_authz` marks a refusal made before the route's own authorization. For a cookie session the server-wide same-site gate runs first: a cross-site or header-less write is refused with a `csrf.denied` (central gate) row and writes no `mfa.step_up.required` row. A row from a JIT-elevation route's own in-handler check, or from the unwired-store refusal made inside a handler, has the same fields but carries neither `cid=` nor `stage=`. `reason` tokens: `engine_principal_denied` (an engine principal, `403`), `saml_session_no_mfa_this_release` (a SAML session, `403`), `mfa_status_unavailable` (the store could not be read, `503` or `401`), `auth_store_unwired` (no authentication store, `503`; a defensive state), `chokepoint_exception` (an unexpected failure inside the check, or a session that could not be fingerprinted, `503`, fails closed); `step_up_class_unsupported` is reserved and not written today. Written best effort: a failed audit write never turns a refusal into a pass. A pass never writes a row. While `--mfa-step-up-window-secs` is `0` or less no stale proof is refused (so none is recorded) and the SAML refusal and the store read are skipped too, but engine denials and unwired-store refusals still are recorded. The one refusal that writes no row is a request with no session at all (counted on the metric only; a defensive branch, since pre-routing answers anonymous requests first). On these 37 routes an engine denial is this row; the per-route handler row (`result=denied`, detail `engine_session_denied`) is no longer written there, so rules keyed on it must be updated. Approve and reject (`POST /api/approvals/{id}/approve` and `/reject`) are among the 37, so with the check active a stale proof, a SAML session or an engine principal on them is refused here and leaves no ticket-specific `approval.approve` / `approval.reject` row. Their own approver gate (#6079) is stricter (an enrolled factor and a proof no older than the approver window, `min(--mfa-step-up-window-secs, 300)` seconds, and 300 when that setting is `0` or negative) and runs inside the handler: when it refuses a stale proof, including a proof made before the account's current enrolment (` reason=proof_predates_enrolment` is appended), it writes this same row from the handler, with neither `cid=` nor `stage=`, next to the ticket-specific `denied` row with `detail=approver_assurance:mfa_proof_stale`. Counted on `yuzu_auth_mfa_step_up_gate_total` (see `docs/observability-conventions.md`). |
| `mfa.step_up.passed` / `mfa.step_up.failed` | User | `POST /login/mfa/stepup` refreshed the session's MFA proof (`passed`, `result=ok`, `detail=method=totp` or `method=recovery`) or refused (`failed`, `result=error`: a missing or rejected code, an unreadable store, a bearer, OIDC or SAML session that cannot step up locally, or a session that vanished during the step-up). These record the proof; the refusals that make a client call it are the `mfa.step_up.required` rows. A proof is also granted by a Settings enrolment confirm, which writes no `mfa.step_up.passed` row (and no event or `yuzu_auth_mfa_step_up_total` increment): its evidence is the `step_up_proof=stamped` detail of `mfa.enroll.verified`. |
| `role.elevation.revoked` | User | An operator stepped their elevation down early via `POST /api/v1/elevate/revoke`. `result=ok`, `detail=was_elevated=<bool>`. If the durable clear fails the call answers `503` and the row is written with `result=error` and a `durable_clear_failed=true` detail. |
| `role.elevation.expired` | User | A JIT admin elevation window LAPSED passively (no manual revoke). Emitted lazily — there is no background reaper — by `AuthManager::reap_expired_elevation` at the `AuthRoutes::resolve_session` cookie chokepoint, on the operator's first authenticated request *after* the window elapses. `result=expired`, `detail="JIT admin elevation window lapsed"`. Written once per window on a single server replica; with several replicas a rare duplicate is possible (effectively-once, ADR-2002). A manual `role.elevation.revoked` clears the same internal sentinel first, so a revoke never ALSO produces this row. **Boundary:** an operator who abandons the session (closes the browser, lets it idle) without another authenticated request never triggers this event — the window's end is still reconstructible from the `granted` row's `duration_secs`/`expires_at`. |
| `user.elevation_eligibility.set` | User | An admin set/cleared a user's JIT-elevation eligibility via `POST /api/v1/users/<name>/elevation-eligibility` (admin + MFA-step-up gated). `result=ok`, `detail=eligible=<bool>` (plus `elevations_cleared=<N>` when revoking dropped active windows); `result=denied`/`detail=self_grant_blocked` on a self-grant attempt, or `detail=elevated_session_eligibility_grant_blocked` when the caller is administrator only through a temporary elevation (HTTP 403); `result=error` on a store failure. |
| `user.password_change` | User | A local account changed its **own** password via `POST /api/v1/users/me/password` or Settings → User Management → **Change password** (#5342). `target_id` = the caller, and the principal is the caller on every row. `result=ok` with `detail=self_service sessions_revoked=<N> provisional_mfa_cleared=<true\|false>` on success (no separate `session.revoke_all` row). The success row is written **in the same database transaction** as the password change and the sign-out of the account's sessions, so it exists if and only if the change happened. Refusals are `result=denied` (`error` for the store and audit-failure tokens) with one `detail` token: `token_session` (API/MCP/engine/service-scoped token), `not_local` (OIDC/SAML session or SSO/SCIM account), `csrf` (cross-origin, or both `Origin` and `Referer` absent), `wrong_current`, `account_locked` (the wire answer is identical to `wrong_current`; only this row tells them apart), `sso_only_local_disabled` (`--auth-mode=sso-only` and not an armed break-glass account; no password was checked), `verify_transient` (the current password could not be verified — store read failure or a concurrent change; not counted toward lockout), `not_found`, `conflict` (the stored password changed concurrently), `store_unavailable`, `audit_unavailable` (the success row could not be written, so the change was not made — a best-effort record of the attempt). Every refusal leaves the password, sessions and lockout as they were, except that `wrong_current` counts a lockout strike (`account_locked` is refused before the counter is touched — `auth_routes.cpp:1685-1702` — and counts none) and a verified current password resets the counter before any later refusal. A malformed body, a new password outside 12–1024 bytes or a wrong `Content-Type` is not audited (nothing security-relevant was attempted). Never carries the password, its hash or its length. |
| `user.password_reset` | User | An administrator reset **another** local account's password via `POST /api/v1/users/{name}/password` or Settings → User Management → **Reset password** (#5342). `target_id` = the account reset; principal = the caller. `result=ok` with `detail=admin_reset target_role=<admin\|user> sessions_revoked=<N> provisional_mfa_cleared=<true\|false>` on success (no separate `session.revoke_all` row) and — only when the account had a lockout (`locked_until` set — even if the lock has since expired — or a non-zero failure count) — an `auth.lockout.cleared` row with `result=ok`, `detail=password_reset`. Both rows are written in the same database transaction as the reset itself, so they exist if and only if the reset happened; an account with no lockout gets no `auth.lockout.cleared` row. Refusal detail tokens: `token_session`, `csrf`, `durable_admin_required` (the caller is not a durable Administrator — a JIT elevation, IdP-group admin role, group-held grant or custom role does not qualify), `admin_gate_unavailable` (the stores could not confirm the caller's authority), `self_target` (use the self route), `break_glass_target` (the configured break-glass account cannot be reset), `not_found`, `not_local`, `store_unavailable`, `audit_unavailable`. A malformed username or body, a new password outside 12–1024 bytes or a wrong `Content-Type` is not audited. The target's API tokens are not revoked. |
| `user.delete` | User | A local account was deleted via `DELETE /api/settings/users/{username}`. The full list of `result`/`detail` values is in `docs/user-manual/rest-api.md` (audit action table). Added with the last-Administrator guard (#4966): `result=denied`, `detail=last_administrator_blocked` is an early refusal (nothing revoked); a late refusal is `result=partial`, `detail=last_administrator_blocked credentials_revoked_before_refusal=true account_remains_active=true <revoke summary>` (the linked credentials **were** revoked, the account remains active). `result=failure`, `detail=store_failure ...` is a store fault or an unwired guard (HTTP 503; the delete may not have completed); `result=partial`, `detail=account_deactivated session_wipe_failed=true ...` means the account is deactivated but a session may survive. Audit-write-failure posture: audit-first. An `attempted` row is written before anything changes; if that write fails the server answers `503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and changes nothing (no credential is revoked). If only the `success` outcome row is lost the `2xx` stands, `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` is set and the `attempted` row remains ("started, outcome unknown"). Failures are logged at warn and counted on the generic `yuzu_server_audit_emit_failed_total` (no per-action counter). |
| `user.role_change` | User | A local account's role was changed via `POST /api/settings/users/{username}/role`. Added with the last-Administrator guard (#4966): `result=denied`, `detail=last_administrator_blocked` (demotion of the last active local admin, HTTP 409, nothing changed) or `detail=inactive_promotion_refused` (HTTP 409; a store-level rule, reached at the route only when the account is deactivated between its lookup and the write, because the route answers `user_not_found` 404 for an inactive account); `result=failure`, `detail=store_failure` (HTTP 503; replaces the former `denied` / `db_failure`); `result=partial`, `detail=role_changed session_wipe_failed=true old_role=<role> new_role=<role>` (the role is changed, a session at the old role may survive). `result=denied`, `detail=elevated_session_role_change_blocked` (HTTP 403; the caller is administrator only through a temporary elevation; `principal_role` on the row shows the effective role `admin`, so `detail` is the discriminator). The remaining values are in `docs/user-manual/rest-api.md`. Audit-write-failure posture: audit-first. An `attempted` row is written before anything changes; if that write fails the server answers `503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and changes nothing. If only the `success` outcome row is lost the `2xx` stands, `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` is set and the `attempted` row remains ("started, outcome unknown"). Failures are logged at warn and counted on the generic `yuzu_server_audit_emit_failed_total` (no per-action counter). |
| `scim.user.last_administrator_refused` | User | The last-Administrator guard refused a SCIM deactivation, delete, `POST` with `active:false`, or group-derived demotion. `result=denied` for an early refusal and for a refused demotion, `result=partial` for a LATE refusal (the linked credentials were revoked before the guard refused); `target_id` is the SCIM resource id. `detail` carries `attempted_action=<scim action>`; on the Users routes also `early_refusal=true\|false`, `credentials_revoked_before_refusal=true\|false` (false: nothing was revoked; true: the linked credentials were revoked first, ADR-2001), `account_remains_active=true` and `live_api_tokens_slug=<n>` (the username's own live API tokens, `unknown` if the store could not answer) and `linked_principals_not_walked=true` (the linked OIDC/SAML principals' tokens are not counted, so a `0` does not mean nothing is live), followed on an early refusal by `sessions_not_revoked=true` and on a late refusal by `identity_links_unchanged=true` and the revoke summary; for a refused demotion `reason=group`, `old_role`, `intended_new_role`, `via_group` and `role_unchanged=true`. Written for every refusal, including the repeats an identity provider sends after a `409` (only the SCIM server log line is rate-limited). Counted on `yuzu_server_rbac_last_admin_guard_refused_total{transport="scim"}`. Every refusal, including a refused group-derived demotion (an IdP-demoted person keeps admin), also raises a critical analytics event. The `revoked_principals=` list inside the revoke summary is lossy: `,`, space, `=`, control bytes and bytes >= 0x80 become `_`, so two distinct principals can collapse into one token (the exact identity stays in `identity_links`). |
| `scim.group.recompute_incomplete` | Group | A Group request's role recompute did not apply every member as intended: a demotion was refused or failed, a member was undetermined (a read failed), or promotions were skipped after a failed guarded write. `result=partial`, `target_id` is the Group's SCIM id, `detail=members=<n>` followed, for each non-empty class, by `refused=<n> refused_users=<...>`, `failed=<n> failed_users=<...>`, `undetermined=<n> undetermined_users=<...>` or `skipped_promotions=<n> skipped_promotions_users=<...>` (up to 20 usernames per class, then `,+N more`, so the first 20 are named and the count is exact; an undetermined member whose username could not be read is `scim_id:<id>`), then `trigger="<group> op=<op>"`. One row per Group request. Counted on `yuzu_scim_group_recompute_skipped_total{cause="promotion"\|"undetermined"}`. The same request's `scim.group.created`/`updated`/`deleted` success row says `recompute=incomplete` (or `recompute=complete` when nothing was left undone). The Group request keeps its status. Nothing repairs the members automatically; see the SCIM provisioning guide, "Known limits of the Group role recompute". A refused demotion is also its own `scim.user.last_administrator_refused` row, and a failed one a `scim.user.role_changed` `failure` row plus a critical analytics event. |
| `scim.user.deactivated` / `scim.user.deleted` (store failure) | User | When the guarded deactivation hits a store failure (a lock wait that expired, an unavailable store, a lost commit acknowledgement) the request answers `503` and the row is `result=failure` with `detail=account_state=unknown credentials_revoked=true` followed by the revoke summary: the linked credentials were revoked, the account may or may not be deactivated. A failed durable session wipe after the deactivation is `result=failure` with `account deactivated; durable session wipe failed` (HTTP `500`). |
| `scim.user.reactivated` (failure) | User | `PATCH`/`PUT` `active:true` could not proceed: `tombstone role unreadable before reactivation` (`500`), `administrator grant check unavailable` (`503`), or an account/mirror write failure (`500`). An elevated tombstone or a name holding an `Administrator` grant is instead `scim.user.provenance_denied` (`404`). |
| `scim.user.compensation_failed` | User | A SCIM create or revive that failed part-way could not be undone: the guarded undo of the just-created account was refused (`outcome=guard_refused_last_administrator`) or failed (`store_failure`, `deactivated_but_session_wipe_failed`). `result=failure`, `target_id` is the username, `detail=reason=<step> outcome=<outcome>`. The request answered `500` and a critical analytics event is emitted; an operator has to check the account. It is left active only for `outcome=guard_refused_last_administrator`; after `deactivated_but_session_wipe_failed` it is deactivated but a session may survive, and after `store_failure` its state is indeterminate. Counted on `yuzu_scim_compensation_failed_total{outcome}`. |
| `audit.auth_sample.exported` | AuditLog | A caller pulled a pseudo-random authentication-surface evidence sample via `GET /api/v1/audit/auth-sample` or the **Draw sample** button on **Access > Audit and reviews** (SOC 2 CC7.2). `target_id=auth-sample`; `detail=from=<epoch-or-0> to=<epoch-or-0> limit=<N> returned=<N>` so the window + sample size are on the chain. `result=success`. Permission gate: `AuditLog:Read`. Store-unavailable is a 503 (not an audited failure). If the audit-store write itself fails, the response carries `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` (the export still returns). **Coverage note:** the sample is filtered to `action_prefixes = {"auth.", "mfa.", "session.", "engine_principal."}` — `engine_principal.*` rows (below) **are** included in this export, since an engine principal is itself a distinct authentication-surface principal class (ADR-1005 item 2b), not general application activity; they remain independently queryable as ordinary audit-log rows via `GET /api/v1/audit`/`query_audit_log` as well. |
| `mfa.disabled` | User | An administrator cleared their own second factor from Settings → Security and sign-in → Two-factor sign-in (`POST /api/settings/mfa/disable`). `principal` and `target_id` are the account; `principal_role` and the session are the acting session's, even though that session is deleted by the same request. **A disable leaves two rows, ordered by the audit `id` (`timestamp` is whole seconds).** `result=attempted` with `detail=MFA clear requested via Settings` is the intent row: it is written BEFORE the factor is touched and fails closed, so if the audit store does not accept it the route answers `503` (with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) and neither the factor nor any session is changed. The outcome row follows, written after the session delete, with the results below. `result=ok` with `detail=sessions_ended=<n>`: a verified factor was cleared and every session of the account was ended, the caller's included (API tokens are not ended). `<n>` is the number of durable session rows deleted for the account; it includes sessions that had already expired or idled out but whose row the reaper (absolute lifetime, runs about every 15 minutes) had not yet removed, so it can exceed the number of sessions the user could still have used and is not a count of live sessions. `result=ok` with `detail=no verified factor to clear`: an unfinished enrolment was cancelled (Cancel setup); no session was ended. `result=partial` with `detail=sessions_ended=failed`: the factor was cleared but the durable session delete failed, so the account's sessions may still be live; there is no retry control, and an administrator must end them (`DELETE /api/v1/sessions?username=<name>` or `yuzu-server --mfa-reset`). `result=error`: `detail=blocked: mfa_enforcement=<mode>` when the self-target guard refused the disable because `--mfa-enforcement` protects the caller (the only row for that request: nothing was attempted, so there is no `attempted` row); `detail=disable failed (sessions_ended=<n>)` or `disable failed (sessions_ended=failed)` when the clear itself failed (its sessions are still ended, because the clear may have committed with its acknowledgement lost; with `sessions_ended=failed` the page tells the user that the account's state is unknown and asks them to have an administrator check it and end the sessions). The outcome row is written after the changes and cannot undo them: if it cannot be written, the request still completes and the server logs an error (`mfa.disabled: MFA cleared for '<user>' but the outcome audit row failed to persist`). The factor clear and the session delete are separate database commits, so a process kill between them leaves the `attempted` row with no outcome row. **An `attempted` row with no outcome row marks a clear that was requested and whose outcome is unknown:** a process that dies after the clear leaves the factor cleared, and one that dies before the clear leaves it intact, so check the account's MFA state and end its sessions if the factor is cleared. `yuzu-server --mfa-reset` follows the same two-phase rule. The runbook has two read-only discovery queries for these rows, "Finding a clear whose outcome is unknown" and "Finding a clear that ended `error` or `partial`", in [Emergency MFA disable](../ops-runbooks/auth-db-recovery.md#emergency-mfa-disable-break-glass). |
| `mfa.enroll.required` | User | A sign-in under `--mfa-enforcement` that protects the account reached the enrolment step: the server issued the enrolment challenge during sign-in (`result=ok`, `detail=enforcement=<mode>`), or could not start one (`result=error`; the sign-in does not complete. `detail` is `auth_db unavailable (fail-closed)` or `mfa_init_enrollment: secret/store unavailable (fail-closed)` for a `503`, and `mfa_init_enrollment failed` for a `500`). `principal` and `target_id` are the account. It is the row to watch while an enrolment sign-in window is open, next to `mfa.enroll.verified`. See [Enrolling a non-admin approver](../ops-runbooks/auth-db-recovery.md#enrolling-a-non-admin-approver). |
| `mfa.enroll.failed` | User | A TOTP enrolment did not complete: Settings → Security and sign-in → Two-factor sign-in (`POST /api/settings/mfa/init` / `verify`) or the enforced login bootstrap (`POST /login`, `POST /login/mfa/enroll`). `target_id` = the enrolling account (empty when a `/login/mfa/enroll` pending token was unknown). `result=error`, with `detail=code rejected` (a wrong first code), a store-unavailable detail (fail-closed), or `detail=credential_changed` (#5342) — the account's password was changed or reset after the enrolment's credential was established, or while its write waited on the change; nothing was revealed, written or enrolled. `credential_changed` is the same token at every site; at `POST /login` it replaces `auth.login_failed` for that attempt and counts no lockout strike. Expect it next to a `user.password_change`/`user.password_reset` row for the same account. |
| `mfa.enroll.initiated` | User | Settings → Security and sign-in → Two-factor sign-in (`POST /api/settings/mfa/init`) asked for a TOTP secret. `target_id` = the enrolling account. `result=ok` with an empty detail when the secret was issued; `result=error` with the operator-facing reason as the detail (the already-enabled message (disable it first to re-enroll), the store-unavailable message, `Enrollment failed`, or `auth_db unavailable`) when it was not. A credential change that races the mint is audited as `mfa.enroll.failed` with `detail=credential_changed` instead. |
| `mfa.enroll.verified` | User | The first TOTP code was accepted and the enrolment is live: Settings → Security and sign-in → Two-factor sign-in (`POST /api/settings/mfa/verify`) or the enforced login bootstrap (`POST /login/mfa/enroll`, `detail=enforcement bootstrap`). `principal` and `target_id` are the account that enrolled, `result=ok`, and the row carries the request's `source_ip` and `user_agent`. A Settings confirm also stamps a step-up proof on the confirming session, and writes no `mfa.step_up.passed` row for it, so this detail is the only evidence of that proof: `detail=step_up_proof=stamped`, or `detail=step_up_proof=not_stamped` when the session could not be stamped (a bearer principal, an absent or mismatched cookie, or a store failure). The enrolment itself stands either way. For an approver this is the row to match to a person: it shows which account enrolled, when, and from where, not who held the device. See [Enrolling a non-admin approver](../ops-runbooks/auth-db-recovery.md#enrolling-a-non-admin-approver). |
| `mfa.recovery_codes.generated` | User | Ten recovery codes were issued: at an enrolment confirm (`detail=10 codes issued`, Settings, or the login bootstrap with no detail) or by **Regenerate recovery codes** (`POST /api/settings/mfa/recovery-codes`, `detail=10 codes issued (rotation)`). `target_id` = the account, `result=ok`. A failed regeneration is `result=error`, `detail=regeneration failed`. The code values are never in the row. |
| `mfa.enroll.race` | User | An enrolment confirm found the account already enrolled because a concurrent verify won the race (Settings or `POST /login/mfa/enroll`). `target_id` = the account, `result=ok`, `detail=already enrolled by a concurrent verify; no duplicate enrollment`. Audited apart from `mfa.enroll.failed` so it stays out of the bad-code population (#3777, CC7.2). The losing session is not stamped with a step-up proof. |
| `mfa.login.verified` | User | A local account's TOTP code was accepted at the login challenge (`POST /login/mfa`). `principal` and `target_id` are the account. `result=ok`. A recovery code instead writes `mfa.recovery_code.used` with `detail=method=recovery`. For an approval it is one of the adjacent rows that evidence the second factor behind a sign-in. |
| `mfa.reset.breakglass` | User | `yuzu-server --mfa-reset <username>` cleared an account's second factor and ended its sessions. `principal` is the OS account that ran the command (not an authenticated session), `principal_role` is `break-glass`, `target_id` is the cleared account. **A reset leaves two rows, ordered by the audit `id` (`timestamp` is whole seconds).** `result=attempted`, `detail=MFA clear requested via --mfa-reset CLI (os_identity=<account>)`, is the intent row: it is written BEFORE the factor is touched and fails closed, so if the audit store does not accept it the command prints `error: audit store did not accept the intent row; nothing was changed` and exits `1`. The outcome row follows: `result=success`, `detail=MFA enrollment cleared via --mfa-reset CLI (os_identity=<account>, sessions_ended=<n>)`, where `<n>` is the number of sessions deleted; `result=partial` with the same detail ending `sessions_ended=failed` when the factor was cleared but the session delete did not persist (the command exits non-zero and the sessions must be ended by hand with `DELETE /api/v1/sessions?username=<name>`); and `result=error`, `detail=MFA clear failed; state unknown, re-run after checking status (os_identity=<account>)`, when the clear itself reported a failure. In that last case the commit acknowledgement may have been lost, so the clear MAY have landed; no session is ended, the command exits `1` with `error: failed to clear MFA; the state is unknown, check the account's status and re-run`, and the operator checks the account before re-running. If the outcome row cannot be written after the clear, the command exits `1` with `error: MFA cleared but audit row failed to persist; record this reset manually`. An `attempted` row with no outcome row marks a reset that was requested but whose outcome is unknown (the process was killed or the host lost after the intent row: the factor may have been cleared or may be intact, so check `auth.users.mfa_enrolled_at`). The runbook has two read-only discovery queries for these rows, "Finding a clear whose outcome is unknown" and "Finding a clear that ended `error` or `partial`", in [Emergency MFA disable](../ops-runbooks/auth-db-recovery.md#emergency-mfa-disable-break-glass). |
| `audit.auth_sample.exported` | AuditLog | A caller pulled a pseudo-random authentication-surface evidence sample via `GET /api/v1/audit/auth-sample` (SOC 2 CC7.2). `target_id=auth-sample`; `detail=from=<epoch-or-0> to=<epoch-or-0> limit=<N> returned=<N>` so the window + sample size are on the chain. `result=success`. Permission gate: `AuditLog:Read`. Store-unavailable is a 503 (not an audited failure). If the audit-store write itself fails, the response carries `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` (the export still returns). **Coverage note:** the sample is filtered to `action_prefixes = {"auth.", "mfa.", "session.", "engine_principal."}` — `engine_principal.*` rows (below) **are** included in this export, since an engine principal is itself a distinct authentication-surface principal class (ADR-1005 item 2b), not general application activity; they remain independently queryable as ordinary audit-log rows via `GET /api/v1/audit`/`query_audit_log` as well. |
| `auth.logout` | Session | Operator logs out |
| `auth.login_failed` | Session | Failed login attempt |
| `auth.oidc_login` | User | An OIDC/Entra login completed via `GET /auth/callback`. Explicit-principal row (the request lands with no session cookie yet) — `principal`/`target_id` is the stable `oidc:<iss>#<sub>` principal (`oidc_principal_id`), never the mutable display name, `principal_role` is the RESOLVED session role (group-mapping may have promoted the login to admin), `result=ok`. `detail=auth_source=oidc;amr_mfa_asserted=<true\|false>;display=<sanitized display name>;email=<sanitized e-mail>` — the CC6.6 evidence of whether the IdP's `amr` claim attested MFA, and the human-readable name — with `;admin_group=<value>` appended when the login resolved to admin, naming the granting `--oidc-admin-group` value. |
| `auth.oidc_login_failed` | User | An OIDC login attempt at `/auth/callback` failed. `result=error` on early rejections (IdP returned an `error` param, missing `code`/`state`) with `detail` naming the reason; `result=failure` on the `handle_callback` failure branch (token exchange / claim validation) and on a refused browser-binding check. The generic failure branch carries no `detail`, and it is where a callback that could not get the discovery document or the signing keys lands (a discovery or JWKS outage is not told apart from a token-exchange or claim failure in the row); a failure to START the flow (`GET /auth/oidc/start`, for example when discovery fails) writes no row and increments no counter (#5704); a binding refusal carries `detail=reason=browser_binding_missing` (no binding cookie), `reason=browser_binding_mismatch` (a cookie that does not match the flow) or `reason=browser_binding_unavailable` (the server could not compute the digest; the flow stays pending). A sign-in that passed every check but whose session could not be stored (the durable session write failed) is also `result=failure` with `detail=reason=session_create_failed`: no session cookie is set and no `auth.oidc_login` row is written. A signature or key refusal (a token no usable key verifies, or one signed by a key skipped at parse for being an RSA key under 2048 bits or with a degenerate exponent) writes this same generic failure row with no `detail`; the refusal text is in the analytics event's `error` attribute, and the server's `failed to parse JWK` warning names the skipped key. A sign-in whose token exchange finished after a Settings save replaced the OIDC provider is refused before any group, role or session work: `result=failure`, `detail=reason=provider_replaced`; if the save landed after the session was minted, the session is deleted again, no cookie is set and the detail is `reason=provider_replaced;post_mint=true` (with `;db_persisted=false` if the durable delete failed). Both are counted in `yuzu_auth_oidc_login_total{result="error"}`. An IdP response of an unexpected shape (a wrong-typed field in the token response, the ID token header or its claims) is an ordinary failure on the generic branch, never an unhandled error: the provider catches an unexpected exception while handling the IdP's response, logs `OIDC handle_callback: unexpected <type> while processing the IdP response`, and the route then writes the usual no-detail failure row and counts it (the analytics event's `error` attribute is the fixed `OIDC callback processing failed`). |
| `auth.saml_login` | User | A SAML login completed via `POST /saml/acs`. Explicit-principal row (same rationale as `auth.oidc_login` above) — since ADR-2001 PR4a, `principal`/`target_id` is the **stable** `saml:<entity_id>#<NameID>` principal (`saml_principal_id`), not the raw NameID; `principal_role` is the RESOLVED session role, `result=ok`. `detail=auth_source=saml;name_id=<sanitized NameID>` (the raw NameID, run through `sanitize_detail_value` since it is untrusted IdP-supplied input carried in a `detail` field — same rationale as OIDC's `display`/`email` handling), with `;admin_group=<value>` appended when the login resolved to admin, naming the granting `--saml-admin-group` value. |
| `auth.saml_login_failed` | User | A SAML login attempt at `/saml/acs` failed — missing browser-binding cookie (forced-login/CSRF guard), oversized POST body, missing `SAMLResponse` field, signature/assertion validation failure, or an internal exception during validation/session creation. `result=error`, `detail` names the specific rejection reason. |
| `auth.sso_group_provision` | User | The OIDC `/auth/callback` handler reconciled the IdP-asserted `claims.groups` into the RBAC store (`RbacStore::reconcile_idp_memberships`, `source="entra"`) — #1832, hardened per the follow-up round below. `result=ok`: reconciliation committed AND actually changed something (`added+removed>0`); `detail=source=entra;added=<N>;removed=<M>`. A no-op login (the asserted set exactly matches what's already on record — the common steady-state case) writes **no** row for this action at all. `result=skipped`: the IdP omitted the `groups` claim, or replaced it with an Entra group-overage indirection pointer (`_claim_names`/`_claim_sources`, asserted once a user is in more groups than fit in the token) — reconciliation is **not run**, existing memberships are left untouched, and the **login still proceeds** (fail-open on membership, never on authentication); `detail=reason=groups_overage|groups_absent;source=entra`. `result=error`: the login was **denied** (no session minted) because either the asserted group count exceeded `RbacStore::kMaxIdpGroupsPerLogin` (`detail=reason=group_count_exceeded;count=<N>;source=entra`) or the reconcile transaction itself failed (`detail=reason=<store error>;source=entra`) — a provisioning failure never falls through to a session with stale/unreconciled roles (fail-closed); an `error` result also emits a paired `auth.oidc_login_failed` row + analytics event so a SIEM query on that action doesn't miss a provisioning-denied login. Groups are stored **namespaced** (`entra:<gid>`), which is also the confused-deputy fix: a locally-created group can never collide with a same-named IdP group, and the reconcile itself verifies a namespaced row's `source` before joining a membership to it (a pre-existing differently-sourced row is never joined). Paired with the metric `yuzu_auth_sso_group_provision_total{source,result}` (`result` ∈ `ok`/`skipped`/`error`). See `docs/auth-architecture.md` "RBAC group provisioning (#1832)". **`source="saml"`** (since #1832's SAML follow-up) writes the identical action via the same reconcile path, now driven by the `/saml/acs` handler reconciling `--saml-group-attribute`'s asserted values instead of the OIDC `groups` claim; `principal`/`target_id` is the stable `saml:<entity_id>#<NameID>` principal shape (`saml_principal_id`, same as `auth.saml_login` above), and the `result=ok|skipped|error` contract and `detail` shape are the same with `source=saml` substituted throughout (`detail=source=saml;added=<N>;removed=<M>` on `ok`, etc.) — except the `skipped` reason: SAML has no `groups_claim_present`/`groups_overage` equivalent, so `detail=reason=groups_absent;source=saml` covers both "attribute never asserted" and "attribute asserted with zero values" (SAML cannot distinguish the two), and the `error` cap-exceeded reason is `saml::kMaxGroupValues` (200) rather than `RbacStore::kMaxIdpGroupsPerLogin`. Groups are stored namespaced as `saml:<value>`, the same confused-deputy protection as `entra:<gid>`. See `docs/auth-architecture.md` "RBAC group provisioning (#1832)" (the "SAML now rides this same reconcile path" note) and `docs/user-manual/authentication.md` "SAML Fine-Grained RBAC". |
| `auth.breakglass.login` | User | The configured break-glass account (`--break-glass-user`) passed the password gate under `--auth-mode=sso-only` while **armed**. `result=ok` means the **password** was accepted — `detail` is explicit that no session is minted yet (the mandatory TOTP challenge still runs). Emitted at `Severity::kCritical`; paired with the metric `yuzu_auth_break_glass_login_total`. SOC 2 CC6.6. (Note: the sso-only denial of a *non*-break-glass local login is **not** audited per-attempt — it is the metric `yuzu_auth_local_disabled_total` only, to avoid audit-flood under credential spray; the boot-posture log is the activation evidence.) |
| `auth.breakglass.denied` | User | A break-glass login was **hard-denied** because the account has no MFA enrolled (cleared out-of-band after boot). `result=denied`, `Severity::kCritical`. Enrollment is deliberately **not** offered (it would defeat the second factor) — re-enroll it with the sequence in `docs/ops-runbooks/auth-db-recovery.md` (break-glass section). SOC 2 CC6.6. |
| `auth.breakglass.armed` | User | `yuzu-server --break-glass-arm` armed the break-glass account for the configured window. `principal` = the **kernel OS identity** that ran the CLI (not the forgeable `USER` env var), `principal_role=break-glass`, `result=success`, `detail` carries the `armed_until` timestamp + window. The audit store is verified reachable and migrated **before** the arm mutates, so the exemption is never granted without a record. SOC 2 CC6.6. |
| `rbac.bootstrap.first_admin` | User | A fresh-install boot (`auth.users` was genuinely empty) provisioned the config-file admin's account AND granted it a durable fleet-wide Administrator role, atomically, via `RbacStore::provision_first_admin`. `principal`/`principal_role`=`system` (no human/session actor exists this early in boot); `target_id`=the provisioned username; `result=success`; `detail=reason=fresh_install_bootstrap ...` plus the OS identity that ran the boot. This is a one-time, non-retriable event — `auth.users`'s `WHERE NOT EXISTS` gate means it can never fire again on that database. The audit store is verified writable **before** the mutation runs (same pre-flight posture as `--break-glass-arm` above); if the write itself then fails after the mutation already committed, the boot continues (the grant is durable regardless) and an ERROR-level log line is the operator's only signal — see `docs/adr/1008-rbac-management-groups-target-architecture.md` "Delivery note (fresh-install bootstrap)" for the narrow ack-loss residual this doesn't close. SOC 2 CC6.1/CC6.2/CC7.2. |
| `session.revoke_all` | User | An admin force-logged out every active cookie session for a named user via `DELETE /api/v1/sessions?username=<name>`. `target_id=<username>`; `detail=count=<N>` (in-memory cookies wiped). `result=partial` with `detail=count=<N> db_error=true` if the durable `SessionStore` delete failed (since HA WS-1/1a; operator should **retry** — the delete is idempotent — NOT restart: durable sessions survive a restart, so a failed revoke stays valid until its absolute expiry). API tokens belonging to the user are NOT revoked by this action. |
| `session.revoke_all.self` | User | A user wiped all their own sessions via `DELETE /api/v1/sessions/me`, OR an admin supplied their own username to the admin path. The `/me` flow also revokes API tokens; `detail=count=<N> api_tokens_revoked=<M>`. `result=denied` if a non-interactive credential (MCP token, service-scoped token) attempted self-revoke. |
| `session.peer_mismatch` | Session | A gRPC Subscribe whose agent peer IP did not match the IP recorded at Register time. **Two outcomes share this action.** *Rejected* (`result=denied`, #1059 — a stolen-session signal): `detail` carries `agent_id=<id> register_ip=<ip> subscribe_ip=<ip> gateway_mode=<bool>` (plus `raw_peer=<peer>` when the presented peer was malformed and `subscribe_ip` is empty); the SIEM signal is `yuzu_grpc_subscribe_peer_mismatch_total{event="security"}`. *Tolerated* (`result=ok`, #1128 — a NAT-aware relaxation downgraded it): `detail` carries `agent_id=<id> outcome=advisory reason=<mtls_identity_match\|trusted_nat_cidr> register_ip=<ip> subscribe_ip=<ip>`; the SIEM signal is `yuzu_grpc_subscribe_peer_advisory_total{event="security",reason=…}`. `principal=agent:<agent_id>`; `target_id` is the `session_id`. SOC 2 CC7.2: the *denied* form is a high-signal security event; the *advisory* form is expected operational telemetry in NAT-traversal deployments (a spike there alone is benign; correlated with a *denied* spike it warrants investigation — see `docs/observability-conventions.md`). Security events route to the SIEM via Prometheus, not by tailing the audit store; this row is the paired forensic evidence. If the audit-store write fails, the gRPC response carries trailing metadata `x-yuzu-audit-failed: true` (see **gRPC audit-failure signal** below). |
| `session.identity_mismatch` | Session | A gRPC Subscribe was rejected because the mTLS client identity (cert CN/SAN) did not overlap the identity bound at Register time (#1118 — a stolen-session signal, the mTLS sibling of `session.peer_mismatch`). `principal=agent:<agent_id>`; `target_id` is the `session_id`; `detail` carries `agent_id=<id> reason=mtls_identity_mismatch presented=[<cert CN/SANs at Subscribe>] bound=[<cert CN/SANs at Register>]` so an auditor can identify the cert used in the attempt. `result=denied`. SOC 2 CC7.2: high-signal security event. **The SIEM signal is the Prometheus counter** `yuzu_grpc_subscribe_identity_mismatch_total{event="security"}`; this audit row is the paired forensic evidence. On audit-store write failure the gRPC response carries `x-yuzu-audit-failed: true` (see **gRPC audit-failure signal** below). |
| `session.ota_identity_rejected` | AgentCertificate | An agent-initiated OTA RPC (`CheckForUpdate` or `DownloadUpdate`) was rejected because the peer presented no usable client-certificate identity, or the `agent_id` in the request body did not match the certificate's CN/SAN (#416). `principal=agent:<cert identity, or the claimed agent_id when no cert was presented>`; `target_id` is the cert identity; `detail` carries `reason=<foreign_ca\|agent_id_missing\|agent_id_mismatch> rpc=<check_for_update\|download_update>`. `result=denied`. Distinct from `session.identity_mismatch` above, which covers the Subscribe binding check and carries a different `detail` shape. Emitted **only** where the agent listener actually requires a client certificate — on a default-certificate deployment the OTA identity gate is inert so unenrolled agents can still bootstrap. **Not every identity rejection writes this row.** `reason=no_client_identity` — a peer that presented no certificate at all — is deliberately **metric-only**: it has no resolvable principal to attribute, it is the cheapest case to forge in volume, and this audit write is synchronous and sits ahead of the per-peer admission bound, so an unbounded stream of them would pin server threads on the audit path. That is the same split `reject_revoked_peer` already makes for `heartbeat` ("a flood must not hammer the WAL"). The remaining three reasons all name a certificate we recognise — but they are **not** bounded by the issued-certificate population, as an earlier version of this page claimed; they are bounded by the number of REQUESTS, since one enrolled agent holding a valid certificate can loop `CheckForUpdate` with a mismatched `agent_id` indefinitely. **So this row is RATE-LIMITED, at roughly two per second per (peer, RPC, reason)** — a peer exercising both OTA RPCs therefore has a bucket on each (keyed on the peer's certificate identity, or its source IP where it presented none — never on the claimed `agent_id`, which the caller controls and could vary to mint a fresh allowance per request). During a sustained flood you will therefore see far fewer rows than rejections. The metric below is NOT rate-limited and counts every one, so the gap between the counter and the row count is itself the signal that suppression is happening; treat the counter, never the row count, as the measure of volume. `agent_id_missing` returns `INVALID_ARGUMENT` rather than `UNAUTHENTICATED` — the request is malformed, not the certificate — and still writes this row. SOC 2 CC7.2: the SIEM signal is `yuzu_grpc_ota_identity_rejected_total{event="security",rpc,reason}`, which counts ALL four reasons including the unaudited one; this row is the paired forensic evidence for the three that have a principal — sampled, per the rate limit noted above, while the counter is complete. On audit-store write failure the gRPC response carries `x-yuzu-audit-failed: true`. |
| `session.gateway_peer_denied` | GatewayRpc | A call to the gateway-upstream service (`--gateway-upstream`) was refused because the caller was not an authorized gateway peer (SPKI public-key pin; see [server-admin.md](server-admin.md#gateway-upstream-peer-authorization)). `result=denied`. **Written only when the peer's key hash is known**, that is, for a TLS-authenticated peer whose certificate parsed: `principal` is `gateway-peer:<spki8>` (the first 8 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the peer certificate's public key, computed by the server) with `principal_role=unverified_peer` (the caller failed the pin, so the row does not claim it is a gateway; an agent-leaf probe produces the same label); `target_id` is the RPC name (`proxy_register`, `batch_heartbeat`, `proxy_inventory`, `notify_stream_status` or `forward_guardian_message`); `detail` is `reason=<reason> rpc=<rpc> spki=<16 hex>` (hex only: never a certificate, CN or serial), with `reason` from the closed set `no_server_auth_eku`, `not_pinned`, `outside_validity` or `internal_error` for these peers; `source_ip` is the transport peer. **Refusals with no key to attribute write no row**: `null_context`, `not_authenticated`, `no_cert`, `bad_cert`, and an `internal_error` raised before the certificate was read are counted and logged only. That is the same split the OTA `no_client_identity` rejection makes (`session.ota_identity_rejected` above): there is no resolvable principal, the audit write is synchronous on the request path, and for three of the four there is also an unbounded-volume concern: `null_context`, `not_authenticated` and `no_cert` are reached by unauthenticated or certificate-less callers, while `bad_cert` is reached only for a transport-authenticated peer whose certificate does not parse (there is no key to attribute a row to). The full reason set is `null_context`, `not_authenticated`, `no_cert`, `bad_cert`, `no_server_auth_eku`, `not_pinned`, `outside_validity`, `internal_error`. The action is in the `session.*` family, so it is inside the CC7.2 `auth-sample` export. **Rows are written under a bounded per-source budget** (keyed by reason and key prefix; 10 rows per key per 10-second window); beyond 64 distinct active keys, new keys are charged to one overflow bucket per audit-eligible reason (same 10-row limit; the worst case is 680 rows per 10-second window per replica); rows beyond the budget are not written and are counted in `yuzu_server_gateway_peer_denial_audit_suppressed_total`. The row is bounded evidence, not a count: **the SIEM signal and the complete count is the unsampled Prometheus counter** `yuzu_server_gateway_peer_denied_total{event="security"}` (see [metrics.md](metrics.md)). **Audit-write-failure posture: set-and-proceed.** The refusal is decided before the row is written, so a failed or budget-suppressed write never changes the refusal. Only a FAILED write logs `gateway peer guard: audit write failed for a denied call (rpc=<rpc>)` at warning level; a budget-suppressed row increments the suppression counter and appears only in the per-window summary warning (`gateway peer: N denial audit rows suppressed in the last window`, logged when the next denial arrives): the caller always receives the same gRPC `UNAUTHENTICATED`. The response does **not** carry the `x-yuzu-audit-failed` trailer (see "gRPC audit-failure signal" below): the denial counter is complete whether or not a row lands, so the evidence chain for the count is intact. Budget suppression is counted by `yuzu_server_gateway_peer_denial_audit_suppressed_total`; a **failed write has no counter of its own**, only the warning line, so there is no metric to alert on. The write is synchronous on the gRPC handler thread and shares the audit store's pool; the per-source budget above bounds that cost. Not written when `--insecure-gateway-peer` is set, since no call is refused. |
| `server.gateway_peer_authz_disabled` | GatewayUpstream | The server started with `--gateway-upstream` and `--insecure-gateway-peer`, so peer authorization on the gateway-upstream service is disabled on every port the service is reachable on. `principal` is `system`, `target_id` is `peer_authorization`, `result=success`, `detail` is `mode=<insecure_ack\|insecure_ack_tls> tls=<true\|false>`; one row per start (`insecure_ack_tls` always has `tls=true`). `insecure_ack_tls` means TLS and a client CA were present, a production-shaped configuration. Not written in enforce mode. If the row cannot be written (the audit store is not open, or the write fails) the server logs a warning, `gateway peer audit row 'server.gateway_peer_authz_disabled' was not written: <cause>`, and starts anyway. This row is not in the CC7.2 `auth-sample` export (its prefixes are `auth.`, `mfa.`, `session.` and `engine_principal.`); query it by action. |
| `gateway.cluster_affinity_violation` | Agent | A gateway-side `CONNECTED` notification claimed a `cluster_id` differing from the agent's durably-bound `home_cluster_id` affinity (#4669, multi-cluster gateway mode) — refused outright, nothing published to either the durable routing directory or the in-memory dispatch path. `principal=gateway_cluster:<claimed_cluster_id>` (gateway-asserted, NOT cryptographically bound to the presenting peer's actual identity — see `gateway_mgmt_stub_pool.hpp`'s TRUST BOUNDARY note); `principal_role=gateway`; `target_id` is the agent_id; `detail=session=<id> claimed_cluster_id=<id> detected_at=<pre_check\|announce_connected_write>` (the latter distinguishes which of the two detection sites caught it — the pre-check is the common case; `announce_connected_write` is the narrow race window where the pre-check itself degraded or missed it, and by that point the in-memory placement had already published — see ADR-2002 §7d). `result=failure` always. **Any non-zero rate is worth investigating** — unlike most gateway desync outcomes, this is not a benign background rate: it means either a `cluster_id` rename/misconfiguration, or a genuine rogue/compromised-gateway claim attempt. Paired Prometheus signal: `yuzu_server_gateway_route_desync_total{op="announce_connected",outcome="cluster_affinity_violation"}` (also `op="cluster_affinity_check"` on the pre-check's own read-degrade, a distinct, non-security desync). SOC 2 CC6.1/CC6.6. If the audit-store write itself fails, the gRPC response carries `x-yuzu-audit-failed: true` (see **gRPC audit-failure signal** below) — the refusal itself is never contingent on the audit write succeeding. |
| `gateway.session_held_by_other_agent` | Agent | A gateway-side `CONNECTED` notification presented a `session_id` that a DIFFERENT agent's directory row already holds, and the presenting agent has no directory row of its own, so the fallback insert was refused by the unique session index (#5683, HA WS-5) - refused outright: the in-memory placement is unpublished and the notification is not acknowledged. `principal=gateway_cluster:<cluster_id>` (gateway-asserted, NOT cryptographically bound to the presenting peer's actual identity, same as `gateway.cluster_affinity_violation`); `principal_role=gateway`; `target_id` is the presenting agent_id; `detail=detected_at=announce_connected_write`; `result=failure` always. The session id is deliberately NOT recorded: it may be another agent's live session. The `ProxyRegister` replay-adopt refusal of the same condition writes NO audit row (metric and WARN only). An agent that still has a directory row, including a tombstone, is not covered (its CONNECTED is a `session_mismatch`; ADR-2002 7f). The audit write is best-effort: if the write fails, the gRPC response carries `x-yuzu-audit-failed: true` (see **gRPC audit-failure signal** below); an unwired or closed audit store writes no row and sends no trailer, and the refusal itself never depends on the audit write succeeding. **Any non-zero rate is worth investigating** (after ruling out the one-time v5 migration dedup by the two-part rule in [gateway.md](gateway.md#forged-or-colliding-session-ids): at most N `proxy_register` refusals, none from `announce_connected`, AND all within that start's window of roughly 2 minutes when every gateway includes #5695, or roughly 6 minutes plus 20 ms per agent if any gateway is older; refusals inside the bound and window are still not proof of benignness): it means a forged or colliding presented id from a pinned gateway peer. Paired Prometheus signal: `yuzu_server_gateway_route_desync_total{op="announce_connected",outcome="session_held_by_other_agent"}`, alert `YuzuGatewaySessionHeldByOtherAgent`. SOC 2 CC6.1/CC6.6. |
| `ca.cert.revoked` | AgentCertificate\|CodeSigning | An internal-CA-issued certificate was revoked via `POST /api/v1/ca/revoke`, the `revoke_certificate` MCP tool, or the dashboard revoke wrapper (`POST /api/settings/ca/revoke`). `target_type` is derived from the certificate's own recorded purpose (`derive_cert_audit_target_type`, shared by REST/MCP so both classify identically); `target_id` is the serial hex. `result=success` on an actual revoke, with `detail=<reason>`; `result=denied` when the serial is unknown or already revoked (reject-without-state-change, matching every other idempotent-retry-safe destructive verb in this table); `result=failure` on a genuine store/lease error (distinct from `denied` — never conflate a database outage with a rejected revoke attempt). A successful revoke always attempts a CRL republish, recorded separately below as its own `ca.crl.published` row. |
| `ca.crl.published` | Security | **Every internal-CA CRL (re)publish now writes this row (HA WS-6, #4829)** — the single production publisher `CaStore::publish_next_crl` is reached from five distinct triggers, each with its own `reason=` token in `detail`: an operator revoke (`reason=revoke`, from the `ca.cert.revoked` row above), a subordinate-CA import on any of its three surfaces — REST `POST /api/v1/ca/import-chain`, MCP `import_ca_chain`, or the dashboard wrapper (`reason=import_chain`, `via=dashboard` appended on the dashboard surface) — the boot-time pre-publish (`reason=startup`), the leader-gated freshness re-publish when the served CRL nears its `nextUpdate` (`reason=freshness`), and the count-compare self-heal re-publish when the served CRL does not cover every current revocation (`reason=self_heal`). The two operator-triggered reasons (`revoke`, `import_chain`) are audited by the calling REST/MCP/dashboard handler under the caller's own request context, exactly like every other operator-initiated row in this table; the three system-initiated reasons (`startup`, `freshness`, `self_heal`) are audited by the publisher itself (`CrlPublisher::audit_publish`) under `principal=system principal_role=system`, since there is no live request to attribute them to. `target_id` differs by which handler wrote the row: the two operator-triggered rows use the SAME `target_id` as their sibling row above (`ca.cert.revoked`'s serial hex for `reason=revoke`; the literal `root` for `reason=import_chain`, matching `ca.subordinate.imported`); the three system-initiated rows (`CrlPublisher::audit_publish`) use the new CRL's version number on success, or the literal `crl` when no version was minted (a failed publish, or a known-degraded-store early-out). `result=success`/`failure` mirror whether the publish was durably recorded (`publish_crl()`/`CaStore::publish_next_crl` never reports success unless the transaction committed — #1240 B-1), so `result=failure` here always pairs with `crl_republished:false` on any REST/MCP response that surfaces it. A `self_heal` row with `result=success` is the resolution of an earlier `revoke`-triggered `result=failure` row — read the pair together, not the failure row in isolation, when reconstructing whether a given revocation ever reached the public CRL. See `docs/pki-architecture.md` "CRL numbering across replicas" and `docs/user-manual/metrics.md`'s `yuzu_server_ca_crl_publish_failure_reason_total{reason}` for the paired real-time signal. |
| `ca.subordinate.imported` | CaRoot | An enterprise-signed intermediate was imported to switch the internal CA from built-in to subordinate mode, via `POST /api/v1/ca/import-chain`, the `import_ca_chain` MCP tool, or the dashboard wrapper (`POST /api/settings/ca/import-chain`, CSRF-gated same-site before `Security:Write`). `target_id` is always the literal string `root`. `result=success` (`detail=mode=subordinate`, `via=dashboard` appended on that surface) on a validated import (own-key + CA:TRUE + chains-to-parent, all checked before any state change); `result=denied` for each business rejection (`detail=reason=no_root\|bad_intermediate\|not_ca\|key_mismatch\|chain_invalid`, `via=dashboard` appended where applicable) — reject-without-state-change, matching this table's other maker-checker-adjacent verbs; `result=failure` (`detail=reason=store_error`) on a genuine persistence fault, and also on an internal exception during validation (`detail=reason=exception[...]`) so a thrown C++ exception from the crypto/chain-validation path never silently skips the audit trail on this privileged trust-root switch. A successful import always attempts a CRL republish under the new issuing identity, recorded separately as its own `ca.crl.published` row with `reason=import_chain`. |
| `tag.set` | Tag | A tag is created or updated on an agent |
| `tag.delete` | Tag | A tag is removed from an agent |
| `instruction.execute` | Instruction | An instruction is dispatched to one or more agents, or `POST /api/instructions/{id}/execute` refuses the call (`target_id` is the definition id). A refusal for supplied `params` has `result=denied` and `detail` `reason=param_schema path=<path>` (HTTP 400; `<path>` is `/<name>`, `/<name>/<index>`, `/*` for an undeclared name or `(root)`, never a submitted value); a stored schema that cannot be used has `result=failure` and `detail` `reason=param_schema_invalid` (HTTP 500, fail closed; it is a server-side condition, not an operator denial). Both happen before any approval ticket, execution row or dispatch |
| `command.dispatch` | command | A `plugin.action` pair is dispatched through the shared classification/authorization chokepoint — written by `POST /api/command`, the dashboard exec console (`POST /api/dashboard/execute`) and the legacy forwarder. `result=success` records the dispatch (a dashboard send that targets a `scope_expression` first writes `result=attempted` and dispatches nothing, answering `503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed`, when that row is not stored; a send that reaches no agent then records `result=failure`); `result=denied` records a refusal, with `detail` carrying `reason=<r> <plugin>:<action>` (both caller-supplied fields sanitised and capped at 128 bytes). `<r>` is the union of three families: the `DispatchDenialReason` set from the shared chokepoint (`unclassified`, `ambiguous`, `anonymous_operator`, `forbidden`, `approval_required`, `kill_switched`), the targeting-shape and route-reject reasons (see `yuzu_server_dispatch_target_rejected_total` in `metrics.md`), and three emitted by the dashboard's Destructive gate: **`destructive_untargeted`** (a Destructive-classified pair dispatched without explicit, non-empty `agent_ids` — broadcast and scope fan-out are refused), **`scope_violation`** (explicit targets supplied, but confinement against the operator's visible agents left none reachable), and **`classifier_unavailable`** (the capability classifier is unwired, so *every* dispatch on that surface is refused, ReadOnly included — a misconfiguration, not a client error). `POST /api/command`'s own REST-side counterpart to `scope_violation` is **`destructive_no_visible_target`** (#2557): explicit, non-empty `agent_ids` WAS supplied, but confining it to the caller's visible agents left none reachable (the route answers `404` in this case — see `rest-api.md`). Before #2557 this REST arm emitted no metric increment, no server log line, and no audit row at all — a bare `404` with nothing to correlate it to; only the dashboard's analogous `scope_violation` case had any observability. **Caveat:** the refusal rows record who dispatched what, but not *which* agents were requested — `target_id` is empty and the requested target is not in the `detail` string. It is recorded in the **server log** instead, on both dashboard refusal arms: the untargeted arm logs the supplied `scope_expr`, and the confinement arm logs the requested agent ids it could not reach. So a SIEM rule keyed on this row alone cannot reconstruct the intended blast radius of a refused Destructive dispatch — that requires correlating the audit row with the server log by principal and timestamp. The same limitation applies to the REST twin. |
| `command.dispatch_withheld` | Command | A dispatch was withheld before send because the plugin is absent from every named target's reported inventory (#3511), or because the per-OS kill switch for a target's OS is off (#5294) — added in the PR #3939 review round, correcting an earlier claim that this had "nothing for an auditor to review": a command that was not delivered is exactly what `docs/observability-conventions.md`'s "Denied operations MUST emit an audit event" clause requires durable evidence of. `result=denied` always, `target_id="*"` — ONE row per dispatch (not per withheld id), mirroring `quarantine.dispatch_denied`'s fail-closed shape: this is a single withholding decision applied to a batch. `detail` carries `COMMAND_DISPATCH_WITHHELD command=<id> plugin=<name> reason=plugin_not_found agents=<N>`, or `reason=kill_switched_os agents=<N>` for the per-OS case (a separate row, counting only the ids withheld for that cause). A third variant, `COMMAND_DISPATCH_WITHHELD command=<id> plugin=<name> reason=os_gate_unreadable` (#5294, no `agents=` count and no paired rejected-counter), records a dispatch refused before targeting because agent presence could not be read while a per-OS kill switch was set; the scheduled path additionally counts it on `yuzu_server_command_outbox_deliver_retry_cause_total{cause="os_gate_unreadable"}`. The paired `yuzu_server_dispatch_target_rejected_total{reason="unknown_plugin"}` (or `reason="kill_switched_os"`) counter moves by the same count — use the counter to detect the rate, this row for identity (who dispatched, which plugin). Emitted on the same three routes as the quarantine emitters (`dispatch_closure`, `command`, `legacy`); MCP `execute_instruction` and the dashboard exec console reach the shared `dispatch_closure` chokepoint and are covered by that row. |
| `registry.write` | command | A mutating action of the Windows `registry` plugin (`set_value`, `delete_value`, `delete_key`, `set_user_value`, `delete_user_value`, `apply`) ran, was refused or failed on an endpoint, with the old and new values as the plugin renders them (hex of the raw bytes for every value type, at most 256 bytes each, then `~<total>`); a `delete_key` row carries counts and no values, a per-user row names `hive=HKU` and the profile's `sid=<SID>` (a `result=success` per-user row with `reason=hive_unload_failed` is a durable write whose offline hive may still be mounted: a `reg unload` is owed, see the plugin README's token table), and an `apply` writes one row per plan item it attempts with `op=apply.<op>`, as each item concludes (items skipped after a stop have none; the reply's `summary` row gives the totals) (a dry run, of `apply` or of `delete_key`, writes `result=success` rows with `dry_run=1`: the row records that a vet ran, not a change, so a reader filtering `result=success` must also read `dry_run`) (the field grammar is in the plugin README, "Audit line grammar and producer contract"). Written by the SERVER from the agent's output, not by an operator route: the agent streams `audit\|...` lines in its output frames and the server records one row per line. **Binding:** only a command whose `command_id` is exactly what the server mints for the `registry` plugin (`registry-` plus 16 or 32 lowercase hex digits; the plugin name keeps the operator's spelling and is matched ASCII-case-insensitively) whose response the server's own dispatch record labels `registry`, and whose agent-stamped action is one of the six mutating actions produces rows: a read action never does, and an agent that predates the action stamp stamps none and produces none (the write still happens; there is no row, so during a mixed-fleet rollout only upgraded agents are audited). The plugin is not taken from anything the endpoint sends: the gate takes it from the server's dispatch record for the `command_id` (never from the id's text), and the server also checks the shape of the `command_id` and the stamped action. What the endpoint still supplies is the content of the lines, and an endpoint can replay a registry id the server issued to it (the record proves the server issued the id, not who sent the frame).
