| `deployment.delete` | SoftwareDeployment | An operator deleted a deployment via `POST /fragments/auto/deploy/delete?dep=<id>`. `target_id` is the `deployment_id`. `result=success` when an owned row was removed, `noop` when the id was unknown or not owner-visible (prevents cross-owner enumeration — same posture as `preflight.run.delete`). **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty) before `delete_deployment` is ever called — same confinement-gap reason as the two rows above: a token sharing its creating principal's username could otherwise delete evidence for a fleet-wide deployment outside its own service. Permission gate: `SoftwareDeployment:Execute`; owner-scoped at the store seam. |
| `deployment.list.view` | SoftwareDeployment | A service-scoped API token was denied outright (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty) on the read-only deployments list, `GET /api/v1/deployments` or `GET /fragments/auto/deployments`. The reads are owner-scoped by username, which a service-scoped token shares with its creating human, so they cannot be confined to the token's service. A successful list is not audited. |
| `deployment.detail.view` | SoftwareDeployment | The same denial for the single-deployment read, `GET /api/v1/deployments/{id}` or `GET /fragments/auto/deployments/detail`. Unlike `deployment.advance` this read never ticks the engine and writes no row per poll. |
| `directory.users.view` | Directory | An operator (or integration) read the AD/Entra-synced directory user list — email/UPN/group-membership PII — via `GET /api/directory/users` (legacy), the Integrations page fragment `GET /fragments/settings/directory-sync/users` (audited before the read; the list is withheld if the row cannot be persisted, and the read is capped at 201 rows) or its REST v1 twin `GET /api/v1/directory/users` / MCP tool `list_directory_users` (#4031). `target_id` is the `group_id` query-param filter when supplied, empty otherwise. **Behavioural-PII tier**, but the surfaces do NOT share one posture (the Integrations fragment fails closed like REST v1, answering a 200 note that withholds the list rather than a 503): the REST v1 route fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, no data served) if the audit row cannot persist; the MCP tool is genuinely set-and-proceed, surfacing a dropped row as `audit_persisted:false` in its response payload (MCP has no response-header channel); the **legacy route cannot detect or signal a dropped row at all** — it reads through `DiscoveryRoutes::AuditFn`, which now returns `bool` for the Integrations writes but whose read route still discards the result, so the read always proceeds regardless. This is a known architectural inconsistency the legacy route inherits from `DiscoveryRoutes`'s older audit contract, not a per-route decision — tracked as #950. Closes a real pre-existing gap — before #4031 this route returned the same PII with **no audit call at all**. Permission gate: `Directory:Read`. |
| `directory.sync` | Directory | An operator ran an Entra directory sync via `POST /api/directory/sync` (the Integrations page uses this route). `target_id` is the provider (`entra`; empty for `invalid_json`). Once the request is valid, a `result=attempted` row (`target_id=entra`, empty `detail`) is written BEFORE the sync starts; if it cannot be written the sync is refused (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) and no upstream call is made. A cross-site cookie write is refused `403` (`csrf.denied`). `result=success` on a completed sync; `result=denied` with `detail` the in-progress refusal (409) when another sync is running; `result=failure` with a fixed `detail` of `missing_credentials` (400, a tenant, client or secret field was empty), `invalid_json` (400, unparseable body) or `sync_failed` (500). The detail never carries the tenant id, client id, secret or the upstream error text; the upstream text is in the status row's last error. Permission gate: `Directory:Write`. |
| `directory.group_mapping.update` | Directory | An operator saved group-to-role mappings via `PUT /api/directory/group-mappings`. `target_id` is `group-mappings`. The whole request is validated first (every entry an object, `group_id` and `role_name` strings when present): a malformed request is refused `400` with nothing stored and no row. A valid one writes a `result=attempted` row BEFORE any pair is stored, whose `detail` is `<N> mappings requested` (`<N>` is the total asked for; `(first listed)` follows when the list does not fit one row) and the pairs that fit its first 1000 bytes; if that row cannot be written the save is refused (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) and nothing is stored. The rows below follow the read-back. `detail` is `<N> mappings updated: <group>=<role>, ...` (a removal reads `(removed)`; control bytes, `,` and `=` are replaced (a group id cannot add pairs) and each value is clipped at 256 bytes plus `...`). Every pair is recorded: a long list is split across several rows of at most 1000 bytes each, `<N>` counting the pairs in that row, so a large request writes several rows. The route reads the stored set back after writing (a group named twice in one request counts as its last entry, which is what the store ends with): the pairs that are stored get the `result=success` row, and the pairs the store did not take get a separate `result=failure` row whose `detail` is `<N> mappings not confirmed by read-back: ...`, so a mixed request writes both rows. If the read-back itself fails nothing is confirmed, and every requested pair, removals included, is in the failure row. Mappings are recorded for reference only; nothing reads them at sign-in. Permission gate: `Directory:Write`. |
| `nvd.sync.request` | NvdSync | An operator asked for an NVD CVE-feed sync via `POST /api/nvd/sync`. `target_id` is `nvd`. `result=success` is written **before** the request is made, and if it cannot be persisted the request is refused with `503` (an A4 error with `retry_after_ms: 5000`) and `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, and no sync starts; `result=disabled` when sync is off on this server (the call answers `503`). |
| `enrollment.auto_approve.rule_add` | AutoApproveRule | An auto-approve rule is added (`POST /api/settings/auto-approve`). Every successful write leaves two rows: `result=attempt`, written before the rule is saved (if it cannot be stored the write is refused with `503`, `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, and nothing changes), then `result=success` with the same detail. `detail` carries `type=`, `value=`, `label=`, `by=<operator>`; caller-supplied text is tokenised (spaces, `=`, `,` and control bytes become `_`) so it cannot forge an adjacent field. A rule with an unknown type, an empty value, a value over 253 or label over 128 characters, a `:`/CR/LF/control character in value or label, a malformed `ip_subnet` (IPv4 address, optional `/0-32`), a `trusted_ca` that is not 64 hex characters, or a match-everything rule (hostname glob of only `*`, `ip_subnet` `/0`) is refused with `400`, nothing is persisted, no `attempt` row is written, and the row is `result=denied` with `rejected=<reason>`. If the policy file cannot be written the row is `result=error`, `detail=persist_failed by=...` (`500`, nothing changed). A request whose `Origin`/`Referer` names another site is refused with `403` and a `csrf.denied` row. |
| `enrollment.auto_approve.rule_remove` | AutoApproveRule | A rule is deleted (`DELETE /api/settings/auto-approve/{index}`). Same `attempt` then outcome pair, `503`/`500`/`403` behaviour as `rule_add`. `target_id` is the list index; `detail` carries the removed rule's `type=`, `value=`, `label=` (the rule actually removed, read under one lock), `by=`. `result=denied`, `detail=rule_not_found by=<operator>` when the index does not exist. If the request states the rule it was drawn for (`type`, `value` query parameters, which the dashboard always sends), nothing is removed when that rule is no longer at that index: the answer is `409`, with `detail=stale_rule_view by=<operator>` when the index now holds a different rule (or a stated `type` is not a rule type) and `detail=rule_not_found by=<operator>` when the index no longer exists. A request that states neither keeps the by-index behaviour: an unknown index is a `200` no-op with the same `rule_not_found` row. |
| `enrollment.auto_approve.rule_toggle` | AutoApproveRule | A rule is enabled or disabled (`POST /api/settings/auto-approve/{index}/toggle`). Same `attempt` then outcome pair and failure behaviour as `rule_add`. `detail` carries `type=` and `value=` (the rule actually toggled, read under one lock), `enabled=0|1` (the new state) and `by=`. `result=denied`, `detail=rule_not_found by=<operator>` for an unknown index. If the request states the rule it was drawn for (`type`, `value` and `enabled` form fields, which the dashboard always sends), nothing is toggled when that rule is no longer at that index: the answer is `409`, with `detail=stale_rule_view by=<operator>` when the index now holds a different rule (or a stated `type` or `enabled` is malformed) and `detail=rule_not_found by=<operator>` when the index no longer exists. A request that states none of them keeps the by-index behaviour: an unknown index is a `200` no-op with the same `rule_not_found` row. |
| `enrollment.auto_approve.mode` | AutoApproveRule | The match mode is changed (`POST /api/settings/auto-approve/mode`). Same `attempt` then outcome pair and failure behaviour as `rule_add`. `detail` carries `mode=any|all` and `by=`. Any other mode value is refused with `400` and audited `result=denied`. |
| `enrollment.auto_approve.view` | Enrollment | An operator read the configured auto-approve enrollment rules (agent-registration bypass criteria) via REST v1 `GET /api/v1/enrollment/auto-approve-rules` (#4031) — no MCP twin, REST-only per #520. `target_id=` empty. Operational, set-and-proceed (a dropped row is logged but never blocks the read — the rule set is fleet config, not per-person data). Permission gate: `Enrollment:Read` (Administrator only). The dashboard fragment this route twins (`GET /fragments/settings/auto-approve`) remains unaudited, matching its pre-existing posture. |
| `enrollment.pending_agents.view` | Enrollment | An operator read the pending/denied agent-enrollment queue (device-identity fingerprints: agent_id, hostname, OS/arch, agent_version) via REST v1 `GET /api/v1/enrollment/pending-agents` (#4031) — no MCP twin, REST-only per #520; do not conflate with the unrelated `Approval` securable's `list_pending_approvals` MCP tool. `target_id=` empty. Operational, set-and-proceed — a lighter version of the `device_ci` GDPR-personal-data-adjacent class, so recorded but not fail-closed. Permission gate: `Enrollment:Read` (Administrator only). The dashboard fragment this route twins (`GET /fragments/settings/pending`) remains unaudited. |
| `oidc.configure` | OidcConfig | An operator saved the OIDC sign-in configuration via `POST /api/settings/oidc`. `target_id` is the issuer; `result=success`, or `result=failure` with `detail=persist failed: <keys>` when the running provider was swapped but the store refused part of the write. `result=denied` with `detail=elevated_session_oidc_change_blocked` (HTTP 403, `target_id` empty) when the caller is administrator only through a temporary elevation (`principal_role` on the row shows the effective role `admin`, so `detail` is the discriminator); nothing was changed. |
| `settings.oidc.view` | OidcConfig | An operator read the OIDC SSO configuration (issuer, client ID, redirect URI, admin group, skip-TLS-verify — **never** the client secret, which is masked to a boolean) via REST v1 `GET /api/v1/settings/oidc` (#4031) — no MCP twin, REST-only per #520. `target_id=` empty. Operational, set-and-proceed — IdP/admin-group configuration carries reconnaissance value but is not per-person data. Permission gate: `OidcConfig:Read` (Administrator only) — deliberately **not** `Directory:Read`, despite this route twinning the legacy `/fragments/settings/directory` path: that path is pure OIDC config, unrelated to the AD/Entra directory-sync feature `Directory` gates. The dashboard fragment this route twins remains unaudited. |
| `discovery.scan` | Discovery | An agent reported network-discovery scan results via `POST /api/discovery/scan` (`discovery_store`, ADR-0044). `target_id` is the scanned `subnet`; `detail=<stored>/<attempted> devices stored` (an entry skipped for an empty `ip_address` is not counted as attempted). `result=success` when every attempted device persisted, `partial` when some but not all did (the same "some succeeded, some failed" outcome `session.revoke_all`/`api_token.rotate` use `partial` for — never folded into `failure`, which would misreport devices that DID persist), `failure` when every attempted device failed to persist (the request also returns `503`). |
| `patch.deploy` | Patch | An operator created a patch deployment via `POST /api/patches/deploy` (`PatchManager`, ADR-0062). `result=success`: `target_id` is the new `deployment_id`; `detail="Deployed <kb_id> to <N> agents"`. `result=denied`: emitted on any `PatchManager::deploy_patch` validation rejection — invalid KB ID format, the `agent_ids` batch exceeding `kMaxDeployTargets` (5000, post-de-duplication), or a failed transaction; `target_id` is empty, `detail` is the validation error string verbatim (e.g. `"too many target agents (max 5000)"`). **Known gaps, tracked in #3705**: the DB-transaction-failure and patch-manager-unavailable causes are server-fault, not operator denial, and should audit `failure` rather than sharing `denied` with the client-caused rejections; `detail` should carry a stable `reason=<token>` rather than the raw error string; the route's five pre-`deploy_patch` validation branches (malformed body, missing `kb_id`/`agent_ids`, past `reboot_at`, 503 unavailable) remain unaudited entirely. Permission gate: `Patch:Write`. |
| `approval.approve` / `approval.reject` | approval | An operator approved or rejected a pending approval request via `POST /api/approvals/{id}/approve` or `/reject`, or via the dashboard Approvals page, which posts to the same routes. `target_id` is the approval id; a path id that is empty, longer than 128 characters or holds anything other than letters, digits, `_` and `-` is recorded as the fixed placeholder `invalid-id` so that caller-supplied bytes never reach an audit row; such a request is answered `400` `approval not found` with a single `result=denied` row (`detail` `invalid_approval_id`) and no `attempted` row, because the store is never asked. From #6079 the `attempted` and `success` rows carry `detail` `approver_assurance:enrolled_fresh_mfa`; rows without it were written before the control existed. **A decision leaves two rows** (a replica that predates #6061 leaves only the outcome row, with an empty `detail`). `result=attempted` is written BEFORE the decision is applied and fails closed (#5823): if it cannot be written the route returns `503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and the request stays pending. The outcome row follows: `result=success`, or `result=denied` with the store's reason in `detail` when the store refuses the decision (400), for example the self-approval (reviewer = submitter) block. **Only the `attempted` row is fail-closed.** The outcome rows are best effort: if one cannot be written, the response is unchanged and carries no `Sec-Audit-Failed` header, and `yuzu_server_audit_emit_failed_total` is the alert signal for the loss (on a connection-pool timeout or an INSERT error the server log also carries an `AuditStore::log` error line naming the action; a closed audit store logs nothing there). For a lost `success` row the decision has been applied and the response is still `200`; for a lost `denied` row the store refused the decision, so this request applied nothing and the response is still `400`; the ticket keeps its prior state, which is usually still pending but may already be decided (the store also refuses an already-reviewed or unknown ticket, and an update failure cannot prove the update did not commit), so check its status. An `attempted` row with no outcome row means "started, outcome unknown": the decision may or may not have been applied, so check the approval's status. A ticket can hold several `attempted` rows (a refused attempt writes `attempted` then `denied`, and the real decision writes its own pair), so a surviving `denied` row can hide a lost `success` row: reconcile by COUNT, not by presence. For each `target_id`, count the `attempted` rows and the outcome rows (`success`, plus `denied` rows whose `detail` does not start with `approver_assurance:`, which are written with no `attempted` row), and treat `attempted` greater than outcome as suspect (an example query follows the table, under "Reconciling approval decisions"). **Cutover marker (#6079):** the `attempted` row and the `success` row both carry `detail=approver_assurance:enrolled_fresh_mfa`, which records that the decision passed the enrolled-factor and fresh-proof check. An `attempted` or `success` row without it was written before that control, or by a replica that does not have it (see the mixed-version warning in [Upgrading](upgrading.md)). The token states that the check passed; it does not record the factor type or the proof's age. It is a pass marker on `attempted` and `success` rows: the `NOT LIKE 'approver_assurance:%'` filter in the reconciliation query below applies to `denied` rows only and must not be widened to exclude these. For those, correlate by principal and time with the adjacent `mfa.login.verified`, `mfa.step_up.passed` and `mfa.enroll.verified` rows (local accounts) or the `auth.oidc_login` row's `amr_mfa_asserted` detail (OIDC), and do the same for rows that predate the token. **Class refusal:** in the handler, after the central step-up check, an API-token, MCP-token or other non-interactive session is refused `403 approver_assurance_required:<reason>` and leaves ONE row, `result=denied` with `detail=approver_assurance:<reason>` (`non_interactive_session` or `non_human_principal`; `engine_principal` is not written on these rows, because over REST an engine principal is refused earlier, by the central check described below); no `attempted` row is written, because nothing was attempted. The refusal does not depend on that row: if it cannot be written the `403` stands and the response carries `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`. **MFA refusals (#6079)** come from the approver gate and follow the same shape, one `result=denied` row with no `attempted` row and `detail=approver_assurance:<reason>`: `mfa_not_enrolled` (`403`), `mfa_proof_missing` (`403`), `saml_mfa_unsupported` (`403`), `account_unavailable` (`403`; a local session whose account row is deleted, deactivated or has an invalid username; this typed row is written only with `--mfa-step-up-window-secs` at `0` or when the account is removed in the middle of the request, because with step-up on the central check normally answers such an account first with a `401`), and `mfa_proof_stale` (the step-up `401`, which also leaves the gate's own `mfa.step_up.required` row; when a local proof predates the account's current enrolment that row's detail carries `reason=proof_predates_enrolment`). An unreadable MFA store (`503`) leaves one row with `result=error`, `detail=approver_assurance:mfa_store_unavailable` and no `attempted` row. An OIDC approver whose stored MFA proof is dated after the database clock (`503`, the clock-skew refusal) leaves one row with `result=error`, `detail=approver_assurance:mfa_proof_clock_skew` and no `attempted` row; it is a clock fault (the replica that took the sign-in ran ahead of the database clock; the identity provider's token time is clamped to that replica's own clock), not a store fault, and `yuzu_auth_secret_unavailable_total{route="approval"}` does not count it (see "OIDC approvers refused with a clock-skew 503" in `docs/ops-runbooks/auth-db-recovery.md`). The same rule holds for all of them: the refusal stands whether or not its row persists, and a row that cannot be written adds `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` to the `403`, `401` or `503`. The one exception is the gate's own `mfa.step_up.required` row on the `401`: it is best effort, so a failed write is logged on the server and never surfaced in the response, while the ticket-specific `mfa_proof_stale` row above it does set the header. None of these counts as an outcome row in the reconciliation below. The `invalid_approval_id` row is different: its `detail` does not start with `approver_assurance:`, so the query counts it as an outcome row under `target_id` `invalid-id`, which has no `attempted` rows and is therefore never listed. **Central refusals leave a different row, and no `approval.*` row.** Both approval routes are among the 37 routes the central step-up check covers, and it runs before the handler. Over REST it refuses an engine principal with `403` whatever the window: that attempt is recorded as `mfa.step_up.required` with `reason=engine_principal_denied` in the `detail` and the route label (`POST /api/approvals/{id}/approve` or `/reject`) as `target_id`. With `--mfa-step-up-window-secs` above `0` it also refuses a SAML session (`reason=saml_session_no_mfa_this_release`, `403`) and a proof older than the configured window (`age_secs=<n> window_secs=<n>`, `401`), with `stage=pre_authz` in the `detail` and no ticket-specific row. The typed `approver_assurance_required:<reason>` refusals above are therefore what an account with no enrolled factor, a bearer-class credential, a proof older than 300 seconds but inside a longer configured window, a proof made before the current enrolment, or (with the window at `0`) a SAML session sees. SIEM guidance: count approvals with `result=success`; check every `attempted` row that has no outcome row, by comparing counts per `target_id` as above (its outcome row was lost, or the server stopped before the decision; read the ticket's status); alert on `result=denied` rows whose `detail` is `approver_assurance:non_interactive_session` or `approver_assurance:non_human_principal` as evidence that a bearer credential reached the decision surface (the MFA reasons `mfa_not_enrolled`, `mfa_proof_missing`, `saml_mfa_unsupported`, `account_unavailable` and `mfa_proof_stale` are interactive approvers being turned away, not a credential misuse signal), and on `mfa.step_up.required` rows with `reason=engine_principal_denied` and an approval route label as evidence that an engine credential tried to (an engine attempt over MCP is instead recorded on `mcp.approve_request` / `mcp.reject_request` rows whose `detail` contains `approver_assurance:engine_principal`, never on `approval.*` rows, so a SIEM rule for engine attempts should cover those rows too); do not alert on every `approval.approve` row, since the `attempted` row doubles the count. `--mfa-step-up-window-secs=0` no longer weakens approvals (the approver window is then 300 seconds and an enrolled factor is still required), but a `success` row written before the #6079 upgrade carries only the weaker assurance (and, with the setting at `0`, only an interactive session), so review those, and check every `attempted` row that has no outcome row (compare counts per `target_id`). |
| `csrf.denied` (approvals) | Endpoint | This route's own copy of the same-site check, on `POST /api/approvals/{id}/approve` or `/reject` with no `Origin`/`Referer`, or one naming another site: refused `403` before the class check, step-up or the `attempted` row. It is written only where the server-wide check does not run (a handler called directly); a cookie-session caller in production gets the `csrf.denied` (central gate) row instead. `target_id` is `approval`; `detail=cross-site or header-less browser write refused`. Token-authenticated callers are not gated by this check (they are refused by the class check above). An `Origin` or `Referer` matching a `--csrf-trusted-origin` entry is accepted. |
| `approval.precondition_denied` | approval | A redemption of an approved ticket was refused by its pre-consume precondition (#6062): the state the approved effect depends on had changed since the ticket was minted, or the ticket's tool and arguments no longer matched the recall. Written by the approval store for every redeemer that supplies a precondition; today that is the MCP recall only (every approval-gated tool supplies a request match, `confirm_engine_rotation` adds a rotation-state check), so every row in this release carries `origin=mcp` (the other origins appear once their producers exist: Guardian deploy approvals are not mintable yet, #6063). `result=denied`, `principal` is who tried to redeem it, `target_id` is the approval id, `detail` is `origin=<surface> reason=<the precondition's own server-authored message, cut at 256 bytes> (ticket not consumed)`. The ticket is NOT consumed and stays redeemable. An MCP recall that is refused this way also writes its own `mcp.*` denied row, so one MCP event leaves two rows; count `approval.precondition_denied` for redemptions across all surfaces. Best effort: if the row cannot be written the refusal is unchanged and `yuzu_server_audit_emit_failed_total` is the alert signal. The matching counter is `yuzu_approval_precondition_denied_total{origin}`. |
| `mcp.approve_request` / `mcp.reject_request` | mcp_tool | The MCP tool `approve_request` or `reject_request` was called (#6061). Both are always refused after the `Approval:Approve` permission check: `result=denied`, `target_id` is the tool name, `detail=approval_id=<id> approver_assurance:<reason>` with `<reason>` as above (`non_interactive_session` unless the session is an engine or non-human principal). No decision is recorded and no `attempted` row is written. These rows have a different shape from the REST refusal, which is an `approval.approve` / `approval.reject` row. A replica that predates #6061 does not refuse the call: its MCP decisions leave `result=success` rows for these same actions, which is why the mixed-version query in [Upgrading](upgrading.md) lists them. |
| `schedule.create` | schedule | An operator created a recurring instruction schedule via `POST /api/schedules`. `target_id` is the new schedule id; `detail` is the schedule name. Permission gates: `Schedule:Write` + `Execution:Execute` (H-01, #1806 — a created schedule fires unattended through `ScheduleRunner` with no per-fire permission check, so create-time is the only gate). **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** even holding both permissions — an `ITServiceOwner`-shaped grant passes both gates, and a created schedule dispatches fleet-wide with NO per-fire confinement (`exec_visible=std::nullopt`), which is worse than a one-shot mutating read. This denial has been produced by `require_permission`'s own service-scope default-deny since guardian-confinement-2298 PR 3 ("the flip") — audited as `auth.permission_required`/`result=denied` (empty target), **not** a `schedule.create`/`result=denied` row. The route's own interim deny that used to emit the latter was already unreachable dead code by then, and was formally retired as such in #3290 Phase 2 bucket 1a (2026-08-21) — this row's correction reflects that, not a new behavior change on that date. Proper fix (persist the minting token's scope on the row, derive `ScheduleRunner`'s `exec_visible` from it) is tracked follow-up work. |
| `schedule.enable` / `schedule.disable` | schedule | An operator (re)enabled or disabled a schedule via `POST /api/schedules/{id}/enable`. `target_id` is the schedule id. Owner-scoped at the store seam (`set_enabled(id, enabled, user)` — a `Schedule:Write` grant only affects schedules the caller created). Re-enabling additionally gates `Execution:Execute` (H-01 — arms unattended fleet dispatch, same concern as create); disabling gates `Schedule:Write` alone, deliberately, so an operator can always kill a runaway schedule. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright, both enable and disable** — `Schedule:Write` is checked unconditionally before `enabled` is even parsed, so a service-scoped token never reaches either branch (this was true before #3290 Phase 2 bucket 1a too; the route's own interim `enable=true`-only deny was already provably dead — a service-scoped token was always caught by the `Schedule:Write` gate first — and was retired as such). **The kill-switch guarantee this route's design intends** (disable stays reachable for a service-scoped token) **does not currently hold**; tracked as #3378. This has been audited as `auth.permission_required`/`result=denied` (empty target) via `require_permission`'s own service-scope default-deny since the flip — **not** a `schedule.enable`/`result=denied` row; bucket 1a (2026-08-21) only formally retired the already-dead code that used to emit the latter. |
| `schedule.delete` | schedule | An operator deleted a schedule via `DELETE /api/schedules/{id}`. `target_id` is the schedule id on success. `result=success` reported via the response body's `deleted` boolean (no distinct `noop` result value — unlike the preflight/deployment delete rows above). Owner-scoped at the store seam (`delete_schedule(id, user)` — a `Schedule:Delete` grant only affects schedules the caller created). **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** before `delete_schedule` is ever called — a token sharing its creating principal's username could otherwise delete a fleet-wide schedule outside its own service. This has been audited as `auth.permission_required`/`result=denied` (empty target) via `require_permission`'s own service-scope default-deny since guardian-confinement-2298 PR 3 ("the flip") — **not** a `schedule.delete`/`result=denied` row; the route's own interim deny that used to emit the latter was already unreachable dead code by then, formally retired as such in #3290 Phase 2 bucket 1a (2026-08-21). |
| `schedule.list` | schedule | The MCP `list_schedules` tool denies a service-scoped API token outright via its own STILL-LIVE pre-gate deny (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty), audited as `schedule.list` — a separate, worse-than-username-scoping gap found in a guardian-confinement-2298 hardening sweep: `ITServiceOwner` grants full CRUD on `Schedule`, and `ScheduleEngine::query_schedules` has no owner/service filter of any kind, so a bare `Schedule:Read` gate alone let a service-scoped token enumerate every schedule from every other service. **The REST twin, `GET /api/schedules`, no longer emits this verb on denial** — its own interim deny was retired as provably dead code in #3290 Phase 2 bucket 1a (a service-scoped token was already caught by the route's `Schedule:Read` gate first); its denial now audits as `auth.permission_required` instead. This is no longer "one shared verb across REST and MCP" — only MCP's `list_schedules` still emits it. No success-path emission on either transport (neither previously audited the list read at all). |
| `schedule.list.access_denied` | schedule | The dashboard-fragment twin of `schedule.list` above: a service-scoped API token was denied on `GET /fragments/schedules`, the Instructions page's Schedules tab. Distinct verb from `schedule.list` (mirrors `guaranteed_state.fragment.access_denied`'s relationship to its own REST/MCP siblings) since a dashboard fragment is a separate surface. `target_id=` empty; `result=denied`. This fragment ALSO previously had **no RBAC gate of any kind** (any authenticated session could read it) — now gated `Schedule:Read` in addition to this deny; the ordinary `Schedule:Read` denial has no dedicated audit row (matches every other bare `perm_fn` rejection in this codebase — RBAC denials are not separately audited unless the route says otherwise). |
| `auth.fleet_read_required` | (empty) | The shared fleet-read gate (`require_fleet_read`) refused a request on a fleet-wide read route. `result=denied` for a refusal, and `result=failure` when a read inside the check failed (`detail` `fleet read blocked: authorization read failed for <permission>`, `engine principal: authorization read failed for <permission>`, or `engine principal: RBAC enforcement state unconfirmed for <permission>` (no grant read was attempted: the enforcement state could not be confirmed); the caller is answered a retryable `503` with `retry_after_ms: 5000`, never a `403`, and the body names no permission); `target_type` and `target_id` are empty; the reason is in `detail`. Every denial path of the gate is audited (except on the Dashboard panel fragments, which use the non-auditing variant: a refused interactive sign-in leaves no `auth.fleet_read_required` or `auth.permission_required` row there, nor does a store outage met by an interactive sign-in on those panels (the `503` and the degrade counter remain), while a token or engine caller still leaves its row; the header bell and the approvals badge keep the auditing gate and write one `auth.permission_required` row per page view for a refused account, because each stops polling after the first `403`), and the refusal does not depend on the audit write succeeding. Two variants apply only to a **service-scoped API token** and check that token against the `ITServiceOwner` role, which is the authority ceiling for service tokens regardless of what the minting user holds: `detail=fleet read blocked: service-scoped token lacks ITServiceOwner permission <Securable:Operation>` (HTTP 403; the role does not hold the pair, whether the grant was never seeded or an operator revoked it) and `detail=fleet read blocked: RBAC read degraded resolving the ITServiceOwner ceiling for <Securable:Operation>` (HTTP 503 with `retry_after_ms`; the role's grants could not be read, so the request is refused rather than admitted, and `yuzu_server_rbac_read_degrade_total` is incremented; `require_scoped_permission` answers the same failed read with the same `503` and counter and records it under `auth.scoped_permission_required` with `detail=service-scoped token blocked: RBAC read degraded resolving the ITServiceOwner ceiling`; `require_permission` does not answer `503` (its empty service-scope allow-list refuses every pair anyway), it records its default-deny row under `auth.permission_required` with `detail=service-scoped token blocked: default-deny (<Securable:Operation> not on the service-scope global-safe allow-list)`, and a failed read of the role's grants appends `; ceiling read degraded` to that detail (the HTTP response and metrics are the same as for a healthy default-deny); the definitive deny of both keeps `detail=service-scoped token blocked: lacks ITServiceOwner permission` and `403`). The gate's other denials use the same verb with their own `detail` text: an engine principal or MCP token tier refused, RBAC store, management-group store or tag store unavailable, a service-scoped token while RBAC enforcement is off, the topology floor, no management-group grant. |
| `upload_grant.list.access_denied` | UploadGrant | A service-scoped API token was denied outright on `GET /api/v1/upload-grants`, before the grant list is read. `result=denied`; `target_id=` empty; `detail` is `service-scoped tokens may not list upload grants (path=/api/v1/upload-grants)`. The list route resolves the caller's `UploadGrant:Read` view from the minting user's grants, so without this deny a service token would have seen whatever its minter could (the whole fleet for a minter with a global grant). The MCP twin refuses the same caller through the shared permission gate. Same shape as `schedule.list.access_denied` above. |
| `upload_grant.revoke` | UploadGrant | An operator revoked an upload grant via `DELETE /api/v1/upload-grants/{grant_id}` or the MCP tool `revoke_upload_grant`. Both write `result=attempted` **before** revoking, then `success`, or `failure` with the store error or `not found or not revocable` in `detail` (a grant already redeemed, revoked or unknown). If the `attempted` row cannot be stored, nothing is revoked: REST answers `503` and MCP a retryable `-32603` (#6169). `target_id` is the grant id. An `attempted` row with no matching outcome row for the same `target_id` means the outcome is unknown (a crash, or a lost outcome write), not an unaudited revoke: check the grant's state with `GET /api/v1/upload-grants` or `list_upload_grants`. |
| `cert.reload` | TlsCertificate | Certificate hot-reload attempted; detail contains outcome (success or failure reason) |
| `guaranteed_state.rule.create` | GuaranteedState | A Guaranteed State rule is created. On 400 (missing required fields), 409 (rule_id/name conflict), or RBAC rejection, emits `result=denied` with the store error in `detail`. **A `rule_id` that fails the `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`/256-byte check also emits `result=denied`** (both REST and MCP, #4665) — `target_id` is the rejected `rule_id` (REST replaces control bytes, space, `=` and `,` with `_` in it, on this row and on the `denied` rows below that are written before the store call; MCP records it unmodified) and `detail` is the fixed string `"invalid rule_id, missing name, or missing yaml_source"`, not a store error. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty) on both `POST /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules` (REST — found by external review, PR #3156) and the dashboard fragment twin `POST /fragments/guardian/guards` — same confinement-gap class as the read fragments in this file's `guaranteed_state.fragment.access_denied` row, but on the create path: `perm_fn`'s service-token branch checks only the `ITServiceOwner` role, never the token's own service-tag scope. **A `version` outside 1 to 2147483647 on `POST /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules` also emits `result=denied`** (`target_id` is the `rule_id` from the body, neutralised as above, `detail="version out of range"`) alongside the `400`; the MCP `create_guardian_rule` twin answers `-32602` for the same value without a `guaranteed_state.rule.*` row. |
| `guaranteed_state.rule.update` | GuaranteedState | A Guaranteed State rule is updated (version is incremented on every successful update). **An update of a rule whose stored version is already 2147483647 or above emits `result=denied`** (`target_id=<rule_id>`, `detail="rule version counter exhausted"`) with a `409` on `PUT /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules/{id}`; the MCP `update_guardian_rule` twin answers `-32602` for it without a `guaranteed_state.rule.*` row. On 404 (rule not found), 400 (invalid JSON body — `detail` is the literal string `"invalid JSON body"`), 409 (name conflict), or RBAC rejection, emits `result=denied`. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`result=denied`, `target_id=<rule_id>`) on both `PUT /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules/{id}` (REST — PR #3156) and the dashboard fragment twins `POST /fragments/guardian/guard/{id}/enabled` and `POST /fragments/guardian/guard/{id}/update` — same confinement-gap class as `.rule.create` above. **The dashboard twins write a `success` row before the change and refuse it if that row cannot be written (`503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` for edit and delete; the Enable/Disable toggle answers `200` with a banner and the same header); a store rejection after the row (409, 400, 404 on edit and delete) adds a `result=failure` row, so one request can produce a `success` and a `failure` row.** A cross-origin dashboard post is `result=denied`, `detail=csrf_denied`. |
| `guaranteed_state.rule.delete` | GuaranteedState | A Guaranteed State rule is deleted. On 404 or RBAC rejection, emits `result=denied`. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`result=denied`, `target_id=<rule_id>`) on `DELETE /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules/{id}` — found by external review, PR #3156; this REST route had no confinement check of any kind before this fix. The dashboard twin `POST /fragments/guardian/guard/{id}/delete` has the same denial, writes its `success` row before the delete (`503`, `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` if it cannot be written), adds `result=failure` if the store then rejects it, and records `result=denied`, `detail=csrf_denied` for a cross-origin post. |
| `guaranteed_state.rule.read` | GuaranteedState | A service-scoped API token was denied outright (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty for the list, or the rule id for a single-rule read) on `GET /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules` or `GET /api/v1/guaranteed-state/rules/{id}` — found by external review, PR #3156, the same confinement-gap class as `.rule.create`/`.update`/`.delete` above, but neither REST route previously had any audit trail at all. This verb currently records the service-scoped DENY only — an ordinary successful read is not audited (matches the read-tier posture of `.rule.create`'s sibling reads elsewhere in this table; the mutating verbs above audit both outcomes). |
| `guaranteed_state.push` | GuaranteedState | A push of the active rule set to scoped agents is accepted. `target_id` is empty (pushes are fleet-level, not per-entity); `detail` carries `rules=<N> full_sync=<bool> scope="<sanitised-expr>" agents=<N>`, where `agents` is the number of in-scope agents the push was queued for. The scope value in `detail` is **sanitised before embedding**: `"` and `\` are backslash-escaped, C0 control bytes (0x00–0x1F, DEL) are dropped. SIEM parsers reconstructing the original scope expression must account for this normalisation — a raw operator input of `env="prod"` appears in the audit as `scope="env=\"prod\""`. Delivery is asynchronous: a `success` row means the push was queued, not that every agent applied it. If a store read the push depends on is degraded (the policy generation, the rule store, the Baseline deployed set, the tag or custom-property store, or a `group:` scope's membership), nothing is sent and the row is `result=denied` with detail `store degraded scope="<sanitised-expr>"` for every cause (REST answers `503`, MCP a JSON-RPC internal error with a retry hint); an unparseable scope is `result=denied` with detail `invalid scope="<sanitised-expr>"`. Non-object JSON bodies are rejected with `result=denied` and `detail="invalid JSON body"`. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty) on `POST /api/v1/guaranteed-state/push` — found by external review, PR #3156, and the single most severe instance of the guardian-confinement-2298 class found on this branch: unlike every other fix in this table, this route's fleet-wide `full_sync` push directly mutates what every OTHER service's agents enforce, and (before this fix) had no confinement check of any kind — not even the dashboard-analogue check `guaranteed_state.baseline.deploy` below already had. |
| `guaranteed_state.reconcile` | GuaranteedState | A **system-initiated** re-push of the deployed Guard set to ONE agent, fired from the heartbeat path when the agent reports a policy generation behind the server's (an agent that was offline when a push fired, has just reconnected, or is holding its generation, see [Guaranteed State](guaranteed-state.md)). `principal=system`, `target_id` is the agent id, and at most one row is written per agent per 25 s: an agent is admitted at most once per 25 s, and a claim younger than 25 s is kept even when the agent reports the current generation in between, so alternating reports cannot buy extra pushes (the limit is kept in memory by each server process, so it applies per server replica, not across a cluster, and a restart empties it). A reconcile that this replica **sheds** (#5513: its admission budget is exhausted, its per-second ceiling is reached, or its in-flight cap of 256 is reached; see `--guardian-reconcile-budget-ms-per-s`) writes **no row** and does not consume the 25 s slot: the agent re-reports on its next heartbeat, and only `yuzu_server_guardian_reconciles_total{result="shed"}` records it, so a row is always one admitted push (the converse does not hold: an admitted push that ends without sending and without aborting writes no row and keeps its claim, so it is still limited to one admission per 25 s; that covers `result="no_session"`, a fresh generation that is not ahead of the agent, and a failed queue write. `no_session` fires only when the agent's session was removed (reaped, revoked or torn down) while a pending-map or gateway-held session id still resolved, on a deregistration race, or when the agent re-registered while the push was being prepared (the push was built for the earlier session, so it is withheld). An agent that has Registered but not Subscribed already has a session: it takes the full push path and a failed send shows only as `yuzu_server_system_reserved_push_total{result="undelivered"}`). Two shapes, distinguished by `result`. **(1)** `result=success`, `detail=heartbeat reconcile re-push (generation <agent> -> <server>)`: the push was **dispatched** to the agent's session. **`success` means the push was sent, NOT that the agent applied it or converged**: an agent whose generation stays behind keeps producing a fresh row about once per heartbeat at the default 30 s heartbeat (the interval is the smallest whole number of heartbeats that is at least 25 s), so repeated rows for one `agent_id` mean that agent is not converging (the agent can also be deliberately holding its generation while an arm is still unresolved), not that the server is healthy. A push that could not be sent writes no row. A row reading `generation 0 -> <N>` is either an agent that has never had a push acknowledged or one that reports 0 because a start-up re-arm failure is unresolved (#5513); today the agent log (`N cached rule(s) failed to re-arm at boot`) is the operator-visible discriminator, and the agent's `yuzu.guardian_boot_rearm_unresolved` heartbeat tag is a wire diagnostic with no reader (#5558): the health store overwrites tags each heartbeat and the audit row does not record it. Repeated `generation 0 -> <N>` rows for one `agent_id` mean that agent keeps reporting 0, for example because a boot re-arm failure persists; a persistent failure is re-pushed with an agent-side back-off, one row per push: when the agent's persisted generation equals the server's current generation, at the default 30 s heartbeat about 60 s, 90 s, 150 s and 270 s apart, then about every 330 s (5.5 minutes) in steady state, because after each failed catch-up that reaches the end of `apply_rules` the agent reports its real generation for the next 1, 2, 4, 8 and then 10 heartbeats before reporting 0 again (the server sees no generation gap on those heartbeats and does not push). An agent whose persisted generation is behind the server's (or is 0), or whose push returns before the end of `apply_rules` (a persistent KV write failure, a `begin_application` throw, the invalid-rule-id reject), earns no holdoff and keeps producing a row about once per heartbeat; a generic server-side per-agent back-off for that held-generation retry is a follow-up. **(2)** `result=degraded`, `detail` begins `heartbeat reconcile ABORTED`, then names the stage that failed and ends `agent rules did not converge (generation <agent>)`. The stage is one of `rule store degraded (<error>)`, `baseline store degraded (<error>)`, `rule bundle load failed (<error>)`, `policy generation unreadable (<error>)` (the fresh read taken at admission), since #5513 `rule bundle invalidated during load (a rule or policy change landed while the re-push was being prepared; retried on a later heartbeat)` (a rule create, update or delete, a Baseline update, delete or deployed create, a policy-generation raise that increased the stored value, or a Guardian push fanned out by this replica landed while the reconcile was being prepared, so the rule set it held may be older than that change; nothing was sent, and the claim is kept, so the agent is reconciled from a fresh load at its first heartbeat after the 25 s slot; this is a benign, edit-driven abort rather than a failing store: it is counted by `yuzu_server_guardian_reconcile_invalidated_total` and NOT by `yuzu_server_guardian_reconciles_total{result="degraded"}`, and the row itself carries `result=degraded`, exactly like the store-failure abort (they share one audit helper), so a SIEM filter on `result=degraded` mixes both; tell them apart by the stage text `rule bundle invalidated during load`) or, since #5513, `scope store degraded (db_degraded)`, where the parenthesis is `db_degraded`, optionally followed by `: <detail>` (any other untrusted-read kind the scope evaluator reports, such as `owner_check_failed` or `presence_degraded`, is written as the kind name alone, never with its detail): nothing was pushed, deliberately, because an unreadable store is never fanned out as an empty rule set. The scope literal is written when the tag or property store cannot be read while evaluating a rule's scope for the agent; its detail does not say whether tags or properties failed (read `yuzu_server_tag_store_read_degrade_total` and `yuzu_server_custom_properties_read_degrade_total` for that): previously that case was treated as "agent not in scope" and pushed a narrowed rule set; now nothing is pushed, and the agent retries on its first heartbeat after its 25 s slot (an abort keeps the claim, so a retry inside the slot is rate limited). Count the rows per `agent_id` to find agents being re-pushed repeatedly; the fleet counters `yuzu_server_guardian_reconciles_total` and `yuzu_server_guardian_pushes_dispatched_total{reason="reconcile"}` track the same events without the per-agent axis. |
| `guaranteed_state.policy_generation.raise` | GuaranteedState | The Guardian policy generation was raised via `POST /api/v1/guaranteed-state/policy-generation/raise` or the MCP twin `raise_guardian_policy_generation` (administrator-only, see "Restoring the policy generation" in [Guaranteed State](guaranteed-state.md)). `target_id` is the fixed literal `policy_generation` on every row of this verb, including the denials of a service-scoped token and an MCP-tier bearer token on REST. It is a fail-closed write, so it produces TWO rows: `result=attempted` is written FIRST, before the counter is touched (if it cannot be written the change is not made), then one outcome row. A store fault after the `attempted` row leaves `attempted` followed by `failure`; an `attempted` row with no outcome row means "started, outcome unknown". A request refused before the `attempted` row leaves a single `denied` row (or none: see the list of cases below), and no `attempted` row. A store fault after the `attempted` row records `failure` with a detail ending `the outcome is unconfirmed`: the value may or may not have moved, and repeating the request is safe because it is idempotent (a repeat of a raise that took effect returns `raised=false`). `result=success` carries `detail=target=<N>; previous=<P>; resulting=<R>; raised=<true\|false>; rules=<X>; deployed_baselines=<Y>`: `raised=false` marks the idempotent no-op (a target at or below the stored value; nothing changed), and `rules` and `deployed_baselines` are the counts read just before the raise. `result=denied` covers a caller who is not a durable Administrator (`caller is not a durable RBAC administrator`), a caller whose authority the re-check under the current RBAC regime refuses (`caller does not hold authority under the regime that is durably true right now`; a re-check that cannot be answered is also `denied`, with a `503`), a service-scoped token, an MCP-tier bearer token on REST, an empty MCP tier (`empty mcp_tier, denied outright (auth_source=<...>)`), an unreadable rule or Baseline count (`target=<N>; store degraded before the change, nothing was written` on MCP, `store degraded: rule or deployed Baseline count unreadable; target=<N>` on REST), a target the store itself rejects as out of range, and, on both transports, an empty rule set (no rules, or no deployed Baseline) raised without `acknowledge_empty_rule_set: true` (`detail` is `empty rule set not acknowledged; rules=<X>; deployed_baselines=<Y>; target=<N>`; REST answers `400`, MCP `-32602`; nothing is changed and no `attempted` row is written). On REST a request body that is not valid JSON or fails validation (bad target, missing acknowledgement) also leaves one `denied` row, with `detail` `invalid request: <fixed reason>`; the MCP tool writes no row of this verb for an argument its input schema rejects and answers `-32602` (the empty-rule-set refusal above is the exception on MCP and does write its `denied` row). A store fault leaves `result=failure`. **Cases that write no row of this verb:** a missing session (`401`); a step-up refusal on REST, which is recorded under `mfa.step_up.required` instead (a SAML session's `403` and an engine session's `403` at the same central check are recorded there too, the latter with `reason=engine_principal_denied` and the route label as `target_id`); a cross-site or header-less cookie-session write on REST, which is refused with `403` and recorded as `csrf.denied` (`target_type` `Endpoint`, `target_id` `policy_generation`) rather than under this verb; a REST request that finds the RBAC store missing (`503`) or the Guardian state store or Baseline store missing or not open (`503`, counted in `yuzu_server_guardian_policy_generation_raise_total` only, after the administrator gate); on MCP, an argument rejected by the input schema (`-32602`), an RBAC store that is not available, a call whose Guardian stores were never wired (an internal error, counted only), and a tier or approval refusal. If the success row cannot be persisted after the request reached the store, REST answers `503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` stating that the audit record was not persisted, and MCP returns an error with `audit_persisted:false` stating the change took effect; the `attempted` row is then the only record. |
| `guaranteed_state.policy_generation.read` | GuaranteedState | A service-scoped API token was refused on `GET /api/v1/guaranteed-state/policy-generation`: the generation is a fleet-wide scalar, so such a token is denied outright (`403`). This verb is **REST only**. `result=denied` is the only result it carries: a permitted REST read writes no audit row. `target_id` is the fixed literal `policy_generation`, and `detail` names the denial and ends with a correlation id (`cid=`). A caller who merely lacks `GuaranteedState:Read` is refused by the permission gate and writes no row of this verb. The MCP twin `get_guardian_policy_generation` has no service-scope class, so a service-scoped token is refused earlier, by the generic MCP service-scope default-deny gate, and the refusal is recorded as `mcp.get_guardian_policy_generation` with `result=denied`, `target_type=mcp_tool`, `target_id=get_guardian_policy_generation` and `detail=service-scoped token blocked: default-deny (C8, #2298)`, with `yuzu_auth_service_scope_default_denied_total` incremented; no row of this verb is written for it. A permitted MCP call is recorded by the same `mcp.get_guardian_policy_generation` row with `result=success`. |
| `guaranteed_state.baseline.create` | GuaranteedState | A Guardian Baseline (the deployable collection of Guards) is created from the dashboard. `detail` carries `<name> (members=<N>, draft)`. RBAC rejection (`GuaranteedState:Write`) is audited centrally under `auth.permission_required`. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`target_id=` empty) before the form is parsed — same confinement-gap class as `.rule.create` above. |
| `guaranteed_state.baseline.update` | GuaranteedState | A Baseline is renamed or its member set edited (`GuaranteedState:Write`). `detail` carries `<name> (members=<N>)`. For a *deployed* Baseline the change is staged — it reaches agents only on re-deploy. **A service-scoped API token is denied outright** (`target_id=<baseline_id>`) before any edit is applied. |
| `guaranteed_state.baseline.deploy` | GuaranteedState | A Baseline is deployed or re-deployed (`GuaranteedState:Push`): its member set is snapshotted and the fleet converges to the union of deployed Baselines' enabled members. `detail` carries `<name> deployed fleet-wide (agents=<N>, members=<M>)`. If the fan-out was abandoned because a store read it depends on degraded (the rule store, the Baseline deployed set, the tag store, the custom-property store or a management-group membership), the row is `result=degraded` and the detail reads `(a store read degraded - fan-out deferred to reconcile, members=<M>)`: the Baseline is saved and a generation bump was attempted, the dashboard shows a warning toast, and the heartbeat reconcile re-sends the rule set once the store recovers, but only if the bump succeeded (it is best effort). `(push not wired/failed, members=<M>)` is recorded, with `result=success`, if no push function is wired, which a production server never does; the dashboard then shows a warning toast ("Baseline saved; fleet fan-out not sent - push not wired or failed") instead of the "Baseline deployed" toast. Store-unavailable / no-such-baseline precondition failures emit `result=denied`. **A service-scoped API token is now ALSO denied outright** (`target_id=<baseline_id>`) before the deploy even reads the Baseline: this route's fleet-wide `full_sync` push meant a service-scoped token could change what every OTHER service's agents enforce, not merely read it — a severe instance of the guardian-confinement-2298 class, though `guaranteed_state.push` above (found later in the same branch) is the more severe of the two: this route is at least gated on `GuaranteedState:Push`, where the REST push endpoint had no confinement check of any kind. |
| `guaranteed_state.baseline.delete` | GuaranteedState | A Baseline is deleted (`GuaranteedState:Delete`); if it was deployed the fleet re-converges to remove its Guards where no other deployed Baseline still delivers them. `detail` carries one of three outcomes: `<name> (was deployed; reconciled agents=<N>)`; `<name> (was deployed; a store read degraded - fan-out deferred to reconcile)` with `result=degraded` when the fan-out was abandoned because a store read it depends on degraded (the rule store, the Baseline deployed set, the tag store, the custom-property store or a management-group membership; the Baseline is deleted and a generation bump was attempted, and the heartbeat reconcile converges the fleet once the store heals, but only if the bump succeeded); or `<name> (draft)` for a Baseline that was never deployed. If no push function is wired, which a production server never does, the row reads `<name> (was deployed; push not wired or failed; fleet not re-converged)` with `result=success` and a warning toast, because the delete itself succeeded. Store-unavailable / no-such-baseline emit `result=denied`. **A service-scoped API token is now ALSO denied outright** (`target_id=<baseline_id>`) before the delete is applied. |
| `dex.device.view` | Agent | An operator opened a **single device's DEX signal history** — the behavioral view that reveals which applications a person runs. Emitted by the dashboard per-device drill-down (`GET /fragments/dex/device?id=<agent_id>`), by the **shared device page's DEX lens** (`GET /fragments/device/dex?id=<agent_id>`), **and** by the agent-scoped REST query (`GET /api/v1/guaranteed-state/events?agent_id=<agent_id>`, which returns that device's `detail_json`). `target_id` is the `agent_id`; permission gate is per-device-scoped `GuaranteedState:Read` (management-group aware; a service-scoped API token is confined to its own service's agents). **A bulk events query with no `agent_id` filter (`GET /api/v1/guaranteed-state/events`, no `agent_id` param) is ALSO audited under this same verb** — every reporting agent's row is identity-linked, so this is not a de-identified aggregate. That fleet-wide shape has no single agent: `target_type=GuaranteedState`, `target_id=` (empty), and it requires global `GuaranteedState:Read` **and** denies a service-scoped token outright (`result=denied`) — the bare role-only service-token check does not confine a fleet-wide read. Both shapes emit `result=success` for the access attempt (access-transparency of the open) and fail closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, no data served) if the audit row cannot persist. |
| `app_usage.agent.view` | Agent | An operator opened **one device's per-executable app-usage projection** (Wave 7 PR7.2) via `GET /api/v1/forensics/agents/{agent_id}/app-usage` — `exe_key`, `first_seen`/`last_seen` (within TAR's retained usage window, 31 days default — never a full run-history value), `run_count_30d`, `total_seconds_30d`. `target_id` is the `agent_id`; permission gate is per-device-scoped `Forensics:Read` (management-group aware; `Forensics:Read` is held by default only by Administrator and the read-only `ForensicsAnalyst` role, and is absent from the seeded Viewer read-list). Behavioral data revealing which applications a person ran, so this uses the same fail-closed audit tier as `dex.device.view`/`sle.agent.view`: `result=success` for the access attempt, and the request is refused (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, no data served) if the audit row cannot persist. A store degrade is separately audited `result=failure`. |
| `dex.observation.view` | Agent | An operator clicked a **single signal-history row** on a DEX device drill-down, loading the **per-event observation detail panel** (every captured projection field for that one event: subject / reason / symbolic / component / metric / platform / exact timestamp / event ID). Emitted by `GET /fragments/dex/observation?agent_id=<agent_id>&event_id=<event_id>`; the `event_id` is bound to the scoped `agent_id`, so a guessed or foreign event ID returns an **opaque 200 placeholder** (byte-identical to "no such event" — the route returns 200 not 404 so the dashboard's htmx swap:false-on-4xx still renders it) and reveals nothing. `target_id` is the `agent_id`; permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`. The `detail` records the opened event's **obs_type and bound event_id**, so usage-class opens (e.g. `process.crashed` / `process.hung` — what a person ran) stay **separately countable** from machine-class opens for works-council access-audit purposes (same rationale as `dex.device.procperf.query`). Behavioral PII — reveals which specific application event a person's device generated at a given moment. As an HTML dashboard fragment it is **set-and-proceed** (not fail-closed like the REST per-device endpoints): if the audit row cannot persist the response carries `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and the panel **still renders** (#1647 — the emit routes through the shared `emit_behavioral_audit` chokepoint, which also catches a throwing audit sink). |
| `dex.signal.view` | ObsType | An operator (or integration) opened a **DEX signal drill-down** for one `obs_type` — top subjects, per-OS split, per-day trend, and a **most-affected devices** list naming `agent_id`s exhibiting the signal (behavioral, individual-identifying). `target_id` is the `obs_type`; permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`, and a service-scoped API token is denied outright (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty — the route has no per-device shape to confine a per-target check against; the empty `target_id` on denial is deliberate, not a bug: the deny fires before `obs_type` charset validation, so the raw route match is not yet safe to embed in an audit detail string). **Three emitters:** the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/dex/signals/{obs_type}`) fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, no data served) if the audit row cannot persist; the dashboard fragment (`GET /fragments/dex/catalogue/signal?type=<obs_type>`) and the MCP tool `get_dex_signal_detail` are both **set-and-proceed** (a dropped row flags `Sec-Audit-Failed`/`audit_persisted:false` but still renders/serves). |
| `guardian.device.view` | Agent | An operator opened the **Guardian lens** on the shared device page (`GET /fragments/device/guardian?id=<agent_id>`) — per-guard compliance state for that device. `target_id` is the `agent_id`; permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`. |
| `guaranteed_state.rule.view` | GuaranteedState | An operator opened the **per-Guard fleet-wide drilldown** on the Guardian dashboard (`GET /fragments/guardian/guard/{id}/page`) — every reporting agent's identity, hostname, and compliance state for ONE rule, fleet-wide. `target_id` is the guard/rule id. Deliberately a DIFFERENT verb from `guardian.device.view` above: that verb's contract is per-DEVICE (`target_type=Agent`); this drilldown is per-RULE, fleet-wide agents, so reusing `guardian.device.view` would silently corrupt a SIEM/works-council query filtering that verb by `target_type=Agent`. Permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`; a service-scoped API token is blanket-denied before reaching this fragment (see `guaranteed_state.fragment.access_denied` below). Set-and-proceed (HTML dashboard fragment, not fail-closed) — a transient audit hiccup must not blank the operator's lens. |
| `guaranteed_state.fragment.access_denied` | GuaranteedState | A service-scoped API token was denied on a fleet-wide/identity-bearing Guardian dashboard fragment — the data-bearing set (`/fragments/guardian/status`, `/guards`, `/events`, `/guard/{id}/page`, `/baselines`, `/baseline/{id}/page`) plus the create/edit form fragments (`/guard-form`, `/baseline-form`, `/baseline/{id}/edit`, PR #3156) — one shared verb across every fragment this gate covers, since the gate itself is a blanket deny (these fragments have no single agent to scope a per-target check against). `target_id=` empty; `result=denied`. A rejected probe still leaves a trace — mirrors the REST fleet-wide deny's own denial audit (`dex.device.view`/`dex.perf.device.view`/`network.device.view`, `result=denied`). |
| `notification.mark_read` | notification | The header bell marked one notification read (`POST /api/notifications/{id}/read`). Written FIRST with result `attempted`, then `success` or `failure` (`detail` says `no such notification` or `store error`); if the `attempted` row cannot be persisted the change is refused with `503` and `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`. `target_id` is the notification id. `target_id` is the canonical decimal id (`0007` is recorded as `7`). A digit string that does not fit a 64-bit id answers `404` and writes no row at all; the `success`/`failure` outcome rows are best-effort (only the `attempted` row gates the change). |
| `notification.dismiss` | notification | The header bell dismissed one notification (`POST /api/notifications/{id}/dismiss`). Same attempted, then success or failure order and the same fail-closed `503` as `notification.mark_read`. |
| `csrf.denied` (notification bell) | Endpoint | A browser (session-cookie) `POST /api/notifications/{id}/read` or `/dismiss` that carried no `Origin`/`Referer`, or one naming another site, was refused with `403` before anything was written. Token-authenticated callers are not subject to it. `target_id` is `notification`. An `Origin` or `Referer` matching a `--csrf-trusted-origin` entry is accepted. For a cookie-session caller the `csrf.denied` (central gate) row is written instead. |
| `dashboard.fragment.access_denied` | dashboard | A service-scoped API token was denied on a Dashboard panel fragment (`/fragments/dashboard/summary`, `/policies`, `/activity`, `/health`). The panels answer `403` (summary) or a `200` note (the others) and the page loads no data for the caller. |
| `dex.perf.device.view` | GuaranteedState | An operator (or integration) read the **fleet-wide DEX performance device list** — the worst-by-metric, not-reporting, or cohort-member device list behind every Performance drill, each row an `agent_id` + its perf metrics. `target_id=` empty (fleet-wide, no single entity); permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`, and a service-scoped API token is denied outright (`result=denied`) for the same reason as `dex.signal.view` above — no per-device shape to confine against. **Three emitters:** the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/dex/perf/devices`) fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) if the audit row cannot persist; the dashboard fragment (`GET /fragments/dex/perf/devices`) and the MCP tool `list_dex_perf_devices` are both **set-and-proceed**. |
| `network.device.view` | GuaranteedState | An operator (or integration) read the **fleet-wide network device list** — the worst-by-metric, not-reporting, co-occurrence-band, or cohort-member device list behind every `/network` drill, each row an `agent_id` + its network perf/correlation facts. `target_id=` empty (fleet-wide); permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`, service-scoped token denied outright (`result=denied`), same shape and rationale as `dex.perf.device.view` above. **Three emitters:** the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/network/devices`) fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) if the audit row cannot persist; the dashboard fragment (`GET /fragments/network/devices`) and the MCP tool `list_network_devices` are both **set-and-proceed**. |
| `network.discovered.view` | Infrastructure | An operator (or integration) read the **fleet-wide discovered-hosts list** — hosts found by network discovery scans, each row an address, MAC and agent-reported hostname. `target_id=` empty (fleet-wide); permission gate `Infrastructure:Read`, service-scoped token denied outright (`result=denied` on the REST endpoint and the fragment; an MCP denial is recorded at the MCP gate as `mcp.list_discovered_devices`, `result=denied`). **Three emitters:** the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/network/discovered-devices`) fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) if the audit row cannot persist; the dashboard fragment (`GET /fragments/network/discovered`) and the MCP tool `list_discovered_devices` are both **set-and-proceed**. |
| `dex.overview.view` | GuaranteedState | On the **dashboard fragment** (`GET /fragments/dex/overview`) a service-scoped API token was denied outright — the default `/dex` landing view's top-devices list, `agent_id`s fleet-wide; `target_id=` empty, `result=denied` only on this surface (the overview fragment is the default fragment every `/dex` page load fetches, so no per-open success audit was added there). **On the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/dex/overview`) and the MCP tool `get_dex_overview`** (#4035): every call additionally emits this SAME verb with `result=success` before serving (`target_id=` empty) — deliberately MORE rigor than the fragment's own denial-only posture. A service-scoped API token is denied outright on both surfaces too (`result=denied`, `target_id=` empty), via each surface's own `deny_fleet_wide_service_scoped` call. **Fail-closed on the REST endpoint:** if the `result=success` audit row cannot persist, returns `503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and serves no data; the MCP tool is set-and-proceed (`audit_persisted:false` in the result on a dropped row). Permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`. |
| `dex.app.view` | GuaranteedState | On the **dashboard fragment** (`GET /fragments/dex/app?name=<app>`) a service-scoped API token was denied outright — the app-centric affected-devices list, `agent_id`s fleet-wide; `target_id=` empty, `result=denied` only on this surface (same deferral as `dex.overview.view` above). **On the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/dex/app`) and the MCP tool `get_dex_app`** (#4035): every call additionally emits this SAME verb with `result=success` before serving — deliberately MORE rigor than the fragment's own denial-only posture, same shape and fail-closed/set-and-proceed split as `dex.overview.view` above. Permission gate `GuaranteedState:Read`. |
| `device.list.view` | Infrastructure | A service-scoped API token was denied on the **fleet device roster** (`GET /fragments/devices/list`) — hostname/agent_id/OS/online-state for every visible device. `target_id=` empty; `result=denied` only (this route's own success path has no dedicated audit — a pre-existing gap, not introduced here). Permission gate `Infrastructure:Read`. Same confinement-gap class as `dex.overview.view`: the route's `DevicesFn` narrowing is username-keyed and does not confine a service-scoped token. |
| `tar.device_picker.view` | Infrastructure | A service-scoped API token was denied on a **TAR frame's device picker** (`GET /fragments/tar/process-tree` or `GET /fragments/tar/capture-sources`) — one shared verb across both, disambiguated by `path=<url>` in `detail`. `target_id=` empty; `result=denied` only (no success-path emission — same deferral as `dex.overview.view`). Permission gate `Infrastructure:Read`. Same confinement-gap class as `device.list.view` above. |
| `device.live.uptime` | Agent | An operator clicked **Get live info** on a device page and the uptime panel dispatched a live `os_info/uptime` instruction to the device. Execute-gated (`Execution:Execute`, beyond `GuaranteedState:Read`); `target_id` is the `agent_id`, `detail` records the agent count reached and the `command_id`. Machine-health telemetry. **Two emitters, by design:** the dashboard panel audits **post-dispatch** (`result=dispatched`/`no_agents`); the REST endpoint (`POST /api/v1/dex/devices/{id}/live?kind=uptime`) audits **pre-dispatch** (`result=requested`, `detail` carries `cid=<correlation_id>`) and fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed`, no dispatch) if the row cannot persist. The dashboard live + lens sites also set `Sec-Audit-Failed` on a persist failure (#1585). Aligning the dashboard emitter to pre-dispatch is a tracked follow-up. **Known gap (Stream B):** the `device.live.*` REST incarnation (`POST /api/v1/dex/devices/{id}/live` in `rest_api_v1.cpp`) is the last behavioural-PII audit site not yet migrated to the shared `emit_behavioral_audit` chokepoint — it fails closed before dispatch on a `false` return (no PII without audit), but an audit sink that **throws** escapes to an httplib 500 instead of the documented `503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed`. Also note the throw path emits no `yuzu_server_audit_emit_failed_total` metric (catch-arm `spdlog::warn` only) — for set-and-proceed routes alert on the `Sec-Audit-Failed` header/log, not the counter (#1703). |
| `device.live.processes` | Agent | An operator clicked **Get live info** and the running-process panel dispatched a live `processes/list_hashed` instruction (PID, name, and the SHA-256 of each on-disk executable image). **Usage-class behavioral telemetry** — reveals which programs a person is running. Deliberately a **separate verb** from `device.live.uptime` so usage-class reads stay separately countable for works-council access-audit purposes (same rationale as `dex.device.procperf.query`). Execute-gated; `target_id` is the `agent_id`, `detail` records the agent count and `command_id`. No hash/process data leaves the device until an operator explicitly dispatches this panel. **Persistence:** the dispatched result (process names, paths, SHA-256) is stored in the response store (PostgreSQL schema `response_store`) for `response_retention_days` like any command result, so it is also a DSAR/data-inventory source and is retrievable via the standard response-store API under `Execution:Read` — the works-council "separately countable" guarantee covers the *dispatch* (this verb), not a later read-back of the persisted row. **Note:** the dashboard's Get-live-info Processes card now dispatches `process_tree` (parent PID + SHA-256 + connection join) instead; `device.live.processes` (flat `processes/list_hashed`) remains valid for REST/scripted callers dispatching that action directly. **SIEM / works-council migration:** a query previously keyed on `device.live.processes` to count dashboard process reads should add `device.live.process_tree`, or dashboard process reads will be silently undercounted from this release onward. **Two emitters** (same as `device.live.uptime`): the dashboard panel audits post-dispatch; the REST endpoint audits pre-dispatch (`result=requested`) and fails closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed`) on a persist failure. |
| `device.live.process_tree` | Agent | **Get live info** Processes card — dispatched `processes/list_tree` (PID, parent PID, name, SHA-256) **plus** `network_diag/connections` (joined to the tree by PID), reconstructed into a parent→child tree with per-process network endpoints. **Usage-class behavioral telemetry** (reveals what a person runs + where it connects); a separate verb from `device.live.uptime`. Execute-gated; `target_id` is the `agent_id`. The tree + connection results persist in the response store (PostgreSQL schema `response_store`) like any command result (same DSAR/read-back note as `device.live.processes`). **Currently one emitter** — the dashboard `/run` route, post-dispatch (`result=dispatched`/`no_agents`); a `process_tree` card is dashboard-only until the REST/JSON A1 backfill (#1649), at which point a second pre-dispatch (`result=requested`) emitter will be added to match `device.live.uptime`. |
| `device.live.services` | Agent | **Get live info** Services card — dispatched `services/list` (service run state). Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated; `target_id` is the `agent_id`. |
| `device.live.netconfig` | Agent | **Get live info** Adapters & IP card — dispatched `network_config/ip_addresses`. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.arp` | Agent | **Get live info** ARP card — dispatched `network_config/arp` (host ARP / neighbour table). Available on all three platforms; IPv6 neighbours and incomplete entries are reported on Windows only, Linux covers IPv4 ARP, and macOS omits the interface name and entry type. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.dns_cache` | Agent | **Get live info** DNS-cache card — dispatched `network_config/dns_cache` (resolver cache; Windows and Linux, not available on macOS). DNS-cache is device-level usage-class telemetry (names a person resolved). Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.listening` | Agent | **Get live info** Listening-ports card — dispatched `network_diag/listening`. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.connections` | Agent | **Get live info** Active-connections card — dispatched `network_diag/connections` (established TCP). Usage-class behavioral telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.users` | Agent | **Get live info** Logged-in-users card — dispatched `users/logged_on`. Usage-class behavioral telemetry (who is signed in). Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.capture_sources` | Agent | **Get live info** Capture-sources card — dispatched `tar/status` to show which TAR warehouse sources are capturing locally (read-only; configuration stays on the TAR page). Machine-health/configuration telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.disk` | Agent | **Get live info** Disk-space card — dispatched `disk_space/free` to report free/total bytes for the root volume. Machine-health telemetry (not behavioral — no per-user content). Execute-gated (`Execution:Execute`); `target_id` is the `agent_id`. Emitted post-dispatch by the dashboard `/run` route (`result=dispatched`/`no_agents`). |
| `device.live.hw_disks` | Agent | **Get live info** Disks card (also reachable from the Hardware CI record's **Live** lens) — dispatched `hardware/disks` to report per-disk model, capacity, media type, and interface. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated; `target_id` is the `agent_id`. |
| `device.live.hw_memory` | Agent | **Get live info** Memory card — dispatched `hardware/memory` to report installed memory modules (slot, capacity, type, speed). Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.hw_processors` | Agent | **Get live info** Processors card — dispatched `hardware/processors` to report installed CPU(s) (model, core/thread counts, clock speed). Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.hw_drivers` | Agent | **Get live info** Drivers card — dispatched `hardware/drivers` to report installed device drivers (name, version, date, provider, device class).
