Windows and Linux only — the agent enumerates no equivalent driver registry on macOS. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.battery` | Agent | **Get live info** Battery card — dispatched `power_health/battery` to report presence, charge state, percent, time-to-empty, cycle count, and health percent. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.thermal` | Agent | **Get live info** Thermal card — dispatched `power_health/thermal` to report thermal-zone status and temperature. On a device reporting no thermal zones the result carries status/detail only, with no temperature reading. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.smart` | Agent | **Get live info** Disk health (SMART) card — dispatched `disk_actions/smart` to report per-disk model, bus and media type, plus SMART health, percent used and spare percent for NVMe drives on Windows only (macOS and non-NVMe or virtual disks report health `unknown` and `-` for percent used and spare percent). Windows and macOS only — the `disk_actions` plugin's Linux leg does not back this action. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.volumes` | Agent | **Get live info** Volumes card — dispatched `disk_actions/volumes` to report mounted volumes (mount points, backing device, filesystem type, size). Windows and macOS only — same Linux gap as `device.live.smart`. Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.adapters` | Agent | **Get live info** Network adapters card — dispatched `network_config/adapters` to report adapter name, MAC address, link speed, and status. Distinct from `device.live.netconfig` (`network_config/ip_addresses`, IP-layer configuration). Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `device.live.wifi` | Agent | **Get live info** Wi-Fi card — dispatched `wifi/connected` to report the device's current Wi-Fi association (SSID, signal, security, BSSID/channel). Machine-health telemetry. Execute-gated. |
| `dex.device.perf.query` | Agent | An operator clicked **Load performance** on a DEX device drill-down, dispatching a live read-only `$Perf_Hourly` TAR query to that device. Execute-gated (beyond `GuaranteedState:Read`); `target_id` is the `agent_id`, `detail` records the agent count reached and the `command_id`. Machine-health telemetry — distinct from the behavioral `dex.device.view`. |
| `dex.device.procperf.query` | Agent | An operator clicked **Load applications** on a DEX device drill-down, dispatching a live `$ProcPerf_Hourly` query (per-application image names — **usage-class telemetry**, observes what people run). Deliberately a **separate verb** from `dex.device.perf.query` so usage-class reads stay separately countable for works-council access-audit purposes. Execute-gated; `target_id` is the `agent_id`, `detail` records the agent count and `command_id`. The subsequent result polls require this dispatch's `command_id`, so no usage-class read occurs without a counted dispatch row. |
| `inventory.software.query` | Inventory | A **which-devices-run-X** fleet software query — emitted by the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/inventory/software`) **and** the `/software` page's **devices** tables (`/fragments/inventory/software/devices`); the MCP `query_installed_software` tool writes under the verb `mcp.query_installed_software` with `target_type` `mcp_tool` and `target_id` the tool name, and the REST-style target below travels in its `detail` (the MCP emitter writes the target only, with no `rows=`). Machine-scope installed-software data (no per-user/PII — lower sensitivity than the behavioral device lenses), so **set-and-proceed**. For the REST and MCP emitters the target is `name=<title>` / `agent=<id>` / `fleet` (`name=<title>;agent=<id>` when both are set), followed by the filters that were set in a fixed order — `;version=..;q=..;kind=..;ecosystem=..;source=..`, each value passed through `log_token`; the page's devices tables write the same shared `target_id`. `result=success` carries `rows=<n>` for the visible rows (REST route; an export writes the `inventory.software.export` verb instead, below) and is written on **every** call that serves a page, continuation pages included (tagged `page=continuation` in `detail`): the keyset cursor is opaque but not authenticated, so per-page audit is what keeps a hand-made cursor from walking the fleet unrecorded. On the REST route and the MCP tool the caller's scope is applied in SQL for up to 10 000 visible devices, so nothing is dropped and no `denied` row is written there for the post-filter; only a scope wider than that bound is post-filtered in process, and then a `result=denied` row (recording the distinct dropped-device count, for cross-operator isolation evidence) is written **and** the `result=success` row in the same request. A `result=denied` row is also written, on both emitters and under either scope, when the request names an `agent_id` outside the caller's scope (`detail` `scope: agent_id outside the caller's scope`; the response is an empty page with `devices_omitted` unchanged; when the post-filter also dropped devices the one row carries the filtered-count text instead). The `/software` devices tables use the same scope push-down and bounded refill as the REST route, so the rules above apply to them unchanged — a SIEM rule keying on `denied` must expect a paired `success` on every surface; a store degrade emits `result=failure` (REST returns `503`, the dashboard renders the "unavailable" banner — never a silent empty, ADR-0016 §7). Gate `Inventory:Read`. **As of #3290 Phase 2, the REST and MCP surfaces give a service-scoped API token a real confined result** — `rows`/`devices_omitted` reflect the intersection of the caller's management-group scope and its service-tag scope (`target_id` itself is unaffected by scope, still just the query params echoed back), the same `authz::meet` composition the drop-row above already uses, rather than a blanket denial. **The `/software` page's devices tables gate solely on the fleet-read gate (ADR-0017): a management-group-confined caller is admitted and sees only in-scope devices, and a service-scoped token is denied outright** (`result=denied`, `target_id=fleet`). **A gate-level denial (no grant at all, RBAC-off+service-token, RBAC/management-group/tag-store unavailable, an unrecognized engine/mcp_tier caller class) on REST or MCP is now audited under a DIFFERENT action, `auth.fleet_read_required`** (empty `target_type`/`target_id`, the same shared-primitive convention `require_permission`/`require_list_read` use for their own gate-level denials) — `inventory.software.query`'s own `result=denied` row fires ONLY for the scope-filtered partial-drop case and the explicit out-of-scope `agent_id` case above, never for an outright gate denial. A SIEM rule counting this route's total denial population must sum BOTH actions; keying on `inventory.software.query`'s `denied` result alone under-counts it (consistency-auditor + sre, governance run 2026-08-20). |
| `inventory.software.catalog` | Inventory | A software **title catalogue** read — emitted by the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/inventory/software/catalogue`) **and** the `/software` page (the dashboard emitter); the MCP `query_software_catalogue` tool writes under the verb `mcp.query_software_catalogue` with `target_type` `mcp_tool` and `target_id` the tool name; the target and the `titles=<n> totals=<titles>/<installs>` figures travel in its `detail`. Machine-scope, **set-and-proceed** (`audit_persisted: false` in the REST body, or the page rendering regardless, when the row cannot persist). The REST route writes `result=success` on every call that serves a page, first page **and** continuation pages (an out-of-scope per-device read writes `denied` only) — the cursor is opaque but not authenticated, so per-page audit is what keeps a hand-made cursor from walking the catalogue unrecorded; a continuation page carries `page=continuation` in `detail`. REST `target_id` is `fleet` (fleet view) or `agent=<id>` (per-device view), followed by the filters that were set in a fixed order — `;kind=..;ecosystem=..;source=..;q=..;versions_min=N`, each value passed through `log_token` (so a hostile value cannot forge a field); the page writes the same shared target. `result=success` carries `titles=<n> totals=<titles>/<installs>` on REST (the `/software` page writes the same figures). `result=denied` is written (a) when a management-group-confined caller asks for the **fleet** view — the rollup is fleet-wide and cannot be scoped, so the REST route answers `403` (`permission: Inventory:Read`) — and (b) when the per-device view names a device outside the caller's scope (the response is an empty page, no oracle on whether the device exists). A store degrade (rows, totals or refresh-metadata read, incl. the statement timeout) emits `result=failure` (REST `503` + `retry_after_ms`, never an empty `200`). Service-scoped API tokens are denied (`result=denied`, `403`, no `permission` field) before the query string is parsed, so the row carries `target_id=fleet` and the route's own verb (`inventory.software.catalog`, also for a `?format=csv` request). A gate-level denial (no grant of any kind (global or management-group), RBAC unavailable, or the management-group/tag store unavailable) is audited under `auth.fleet_read_required`, not this verb — a SIEM rule counting this route's denial population must sum both. **The `/software` page gates solely on the fleet-read gate (ADR-0017)**: the fleet aggregate cannot be narrowed to a management group, so a confined caller is refused it (`result=denied`) while the host view is admitted for an in-scope device. Gate `Inventory:Read`. |
| `inventory.software.versions` | Inventory | An **installs-per-version** drill for one title — emitted by the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/inventory/software/catalogue/versions`) **and** the `/software` page (title click); the MCP `get_software_versions` tool writes under the verb `mcp.get_software_versions` with `target_type` `mcp_tool` and `target_id` the tool name (the target travels in its `detail`), and answers the unfiltered read before the first catalogue refresh with the retryable error shape and a `failure` row (`detail` says `building`). Same access, set-and-proceed and `denied` posture as `inventory.software.catalog` (including the pre-parse service-token deny with `target_id=fleet` and the `auth.fleet_read_required` gate-level denials), and `success` on every call that serves a page (an out-of-scope per-device read writes `denied` only). REST `target_id` is `name=<title>` followed by `;agent=..;kind=..;ecosystem=..;source=..` when set, with every value passed through `log_token` (the `/software` page writes the same target); `result=success` carries `versions=<n>`, a degrade emits `result=failure`, and so does the unfiltered read before the first catalogue refresh (`detail` says `building`; REST `503`). Gate `Inventory:Read`. |
| `crypto.libraries.view` | Inventory | A **cryptographic-library family summary** read — emitted by the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/crypto/libraries`) **and** the `/crypto` page; the MCP `get_crypto_libraries` tool writes under the verb `mcp.get_crypto_libraries` with `target_type` `mcp_tool` and `target_id` the tool name (the target travels in its `detail`). Machine-scope technical data (installed package names and versions), **set-and-proceed** (when the row cannot persist: `audit_persisted: false` in the REST body plus a `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` header; on the `/crypto` page the fragment carries the same header and renders an "Audit not recorded." notice above the data or the error banner, as a table row on a Load more page). `target_id` is `fleet` followed by `;family=..;ecosystem=..` when set, each value passed through `log_token`; `result=success` carries `cid=<correlation id> tv=<table_version> family=<id or all> rows=<n families> src=<catalogue|live> scope=<fleet|confined>` (`src` = where the package names came from; `scope=fleet` for an unconfined read, `scope=confined` for any present visible device set, including an empty one; the MCP row carries the same keys without `cid=`, plus ` target=<filters>` appended). A store degrade or an admission refusal (REST `503` + `retry_after_ms`, never an empty `200`) attempts a `result=failure` row with `cid=.. tv=.. scope=<fleet|confined> store degraded` (the inventory read failed) or `cid=.. tv=.. scope=<fleet|confined> capacity in use` (the in-flight bound refused it); the MCP row has the same forms without `cid=` and with the target appended: `tv=<n> scope=.. store degraded target=<filters>` or `tv=<n> scope=.. capacity in use target=<filters>`. The failure row is not guaranteed: under connection-pool exhaustion (`pool_acquire_timeout`) the audit write needs the same pool and can be lost, which increments `yuzu_server_audit_emit_failed_total` (alert `YuzuAuditPersistFailures`). A refusal at the access gate (no `Inventory:Read` grant of any kind, RBAC or the management-group/tag store unavailable) is written by the gate as `auth.fleet_read_required`, not under this verb. **Cadence on the `/crypto` dashboard:** drawing the dashboard reads the summary once for every library card on it and writes ONE row (family `all`) with ` widgets=<n>` appended (the number of library cards that read served); reloading a single card (`/fragments/crypto/widget/{id}`: Retry, or a card just added) writes one row; the REST route and the MCP tool write one per call. A dashboard with no library card writes none. Gate `Inventory:Read`. |
| `crypto.libraries.devices` | Inventory | A **device drill for one cryptographic-library family** — emitted by the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/crypto/libraries/devices`) **and** the `/crypto` page (family or version click, and Load more); the MCP `list_crypto_library_devices` tool writes under the verb `mcp.list_crypto_library_devices` with `target_type` `mcp_tool` and `target_id` the tool name. Same access, set-and-proceed and `failure` posture as `crypto.libraries.view`. Every page served is audited, **continuation pages included** (tagged `page=continuation` in `detail`) — the cursor is opaque but not authenticated, so per-page audit is what keeps a hand-made cursor from walking the device list unrecorded. `target_id` is `family=<id>` followed by `;version=..;ecosystem=..` when set; `result=success` carries `cid=.. tv=<table_version> family=.. rows=<n on this page> src=<catalogue|live> scope=<fleet|confined>[ page=continuation]` (the MCP row: the same keys without `cid=`, plus ` target=<filters>` appended). Gate `Inventory:Read`. |
| `crypto.cbom.export` | Inventory | A **CycloneDX 1.7 CBOM export** of the cryptographic-library inventory — emitted by the REST endpoint (`GET /api/v1/crypto/cbom`); the MCP `export_crypto_cbom` tool writes under the verb `mcp.export_crypto_cbom` with `target_type` `mcp_tool` and `target_id` the tool name (the target travels in its `detail`). Same target and detail key set as `crypto.libraries.view` (`rows` = families) plus, on the success row, ` serial=<urn:uuid:...> [ecosystem=<filter>] components=<n>` (REST and MCP alike; the `ecosystem` key only when that filter was given) so the row names the exact document served; same set-and-proceed posture. A failure minting the BOM serial number happens before any inventory read and writes no row. See [crypto.md](crypto.md#exporting-a-cbom). |
| `crypto.ca.stats.view` | Inventory | The **Yuzu-issued certificate counts** were read — emitted by `GET /api/v1/crypto/ca/stats`, by the `/crypto` dashboard (one row per draw for all its Yuzu-issued cards, ` widgets=<n>` appended), by a single Yuzu-issued card reloaded on its own (`/fragments/crypto/widget/{id}`, one row per request) and by the MCP tool `get_crypto_ca_stats` (same verb, one row per call). Yuzu's own internal-CA inventory only (ADR-0070 D6). `target_id` `yuzu_ca`; `result=success` carries `[cid=<correlation id> ]ws=crypto_widget_windows_v1 as_of=<epoch>[ widgets=<n>]`, `as_of` being the database time the counts were measured against (read at request time on every call, never cached). A caller whose view is confined is refused (ADR-0070 R2) and written as `result=denied` with `[cid=.. ]ws=crypto_widget_windows_v1 withheld=confined`. A degraded read (store unreadable or no CA store, or the capacity bound full) attempts `result=failure` with `... as_of=db_now store degraded` or `capacity in use` (MCP included) and the caller gets `503` / a retryable error (never zeros). **Set-and-proceed**: a lost row sets `audit_persisted: false` (REST/MCP) and `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, and the dashboard shows the "Audit not recorded." notice. A refusal for want of `Inventory:Read` is written by the access gate (`auth.fleet_read_required`), not under this verb. Gate `Inventory:Read`, unconfined. |
| `crypto.ca.certificates.view` | Security | A page of **Yuzu-issued certificates** behind one count was read — emitted by `GET /api/v1/ca/issued` (with or without a filter parameter: `kind`, `days`, `week`, `bucket`, `as_of` or `cursor`), by the `/crypto` drill panel (`/fragments/crypto/ca/certificates`, every **Load more** page included) and by the MCP tool `list_issued_certs` (same verb). For a filtered read `target_id` is `kind=<kind>[ days=<n>][ week=<YYYY-Www>][ bucket=<b>]`; `result=success` carries `[cid=<cid> ]ws=crypto_widget_windows_v1 as_of=<epoch>[ pinned] kind=<kind>[ days=<n>][ week=<w>][ bucket=<b>] rows=<n> page=<first\|continuation> total=<n>` (`pinned` when the page's `as_of` was pinned - passed by the caller, or carried by the `cursor` of a **Load more** page - so a paged walk shows one `as_of` throughout; `page=continuation` on every page read with a `cursor`, since a cursor is not authenticated and per-page audit is what records a hand-made walk). On `GET /api/v1/ca/issued` and `list_issued_certs` a confined caller, or one with no `Inventory:Read` grant at all, is refused and written as `result=denied` (`[cid=<cid> ]ws=crypto_widget_windows_v1 withheld=confined|refused`, the same shape as `crypto.ca.stats.view`; `target_id` `filtered` or `inventory` for the form asked). The `/crypto` drill panel and the Settings certificate panel refuse a confined caller, or one with no `Inventory:Read` grant, too but write **no** `denied` row for it. That is a gap, tracked by #6384: a CC7.2 test that expects a row for a confined user's drill or panel view will find none. A degraded read, or on `GET /api/v1/ca/issued` and `list_issued_certs` an access check that could not be completed (`... as_of=db_now access check unavailable`), attempts `result=failure` as for `crypto.ca.stats.view`; the drill writes no row when its access check is unavailable. Set-and-proceed in the same way; a `400` writes no row. The **unfiltered** `GET /api/v1/ca/issued` and `list_issued_certs` write this verb too: `target_id` `inventory`, and `result=success` carries `[cid=<cid> ]inventory rows=<n> limit=<n> offset=<n> has_more=<true\|false> standing=available`, one row per page; a confined caller, or one with no `Inventory:Read` grant, is refused with the same `result=denied` row, and an unreadable store attempts `result=failure`. On `GET /api/v1/ca/issued` and `list_issued_certs`, filtered or not, every success row ends `standing=available` (the caller holds an unrestricted `Inventory:Read`, or RBAC enforcement is off). A refusal is a `denied` row ending `withheld=confined` (a management-group-confined view) or `withheld=refused` (no `Inventory:Read` grant at all); an access check that could not be completed is a `failure` row and a `503`, never a served page. A second token, `standing=unexpected`, exists only as a defensive value: a refused, confined or degraded standing is answered (`denied` or `failure` row) before any success row is written, so it cannot occur by construction and should never appear; if it does, treat it as a defect. An MCP call writes two rows (this verb and `mcp.list_issued_certs`). The Settings certificate panel, which lists the same rows, is not audited; it withholds only the issued-certificate list from a confined caller or one with no `Inventory:Read` grant, with a note in its place. Gate `Security:Read` and an unconfined `Inventory:Read`. The rows read carry `issued_by`, which is `operator:<username>` for an operator-issued certificate (`agent:<id>` for an enrolment, `system:default-certs` for the server's bootstrap certificates); the audit detail names no row. |
| `crypto.dashboard.layout.update` | CryptoDashboard | An operator replaced their **own `/crypto` widget layout** — `PUT /api/v1/crypto/dashboard/layout` (the dashboard's Add, remove, move and Reset buttons) or the MCP tool `set_crypto_dashboard_layout` (same verb). `target_id` the operator's user name (only ever the caller's own layout). Audit-first: a `result=attempted` row (`detail=[cid=<cid> ]widgets=<n>[ expected_version=<n>]`) is written before the change, and if it cannot be the layout is not changed (`503`, `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`); then `result=success` (` version=<n>` appended, the new stored version) or `result=failure` with ` outcome=<o>` appended: `conflict` (a stale `expected_version`, `409`), `corrupt`, `degraded` (store fault), `invalid` or `unknown`. **`outcome=unknown`** means the database connection dropped after the save was sent, so the server cannot tell whether it was stored; the caller is told `save outcome unknown — reload to check`, and the stored layout (or the next success row) is the evidence of what happened. An invalid layout (`400`) writes no row. A service-scoped token's attempt is written `result=denied` (`service-scoped token refused`) and changes nothing. A cookie-authenticated write naming another site is refused `403` and written as `csrf.denied` (`target_id` `crypto.dashboard.layout.update`). Authentication only: the layout chooses what is drawn, and every widget's data read keeps its own gate. Through the MCP tool, two further generic rows can appear and carry no `crypto.dashboard.layout.update` row: `mcp.set_crypto_dashboard_layout` with `result=failure` when the caller's identity could not be resolved (the layout is not changed), and `mcp.<tool>` with `result=denied` when `arguments` is not a JSON object (null, array or string), which every MCP tool served from the registry now writes before the tool runs. |
| `crypto.dashboard.layout.access_denied` | CryptoDashboard | A **service-scoped API token** tried to read a `/crypto` dashboard layout (`GET /api/v1/crypto/dashboard/layout`). Always `result=denied`, detail `service-scoped token refused`, `target_id` empty: a service-scoped token's layout key would be its minter's user name, shared by every token of that minter, so the route refuses it. The MCP twin refuses the same token at its own gate. The refusal does not move `yuzu_auth_service_scope_default_denied_total`. |
| `inventory.software.export` | Inventory | A software **file export** — emitted by `GET /api/v1/inventory/software/catalogue?format=csv` (the title catalogue) and by `GET /api/v1/inventory/software?format=csv\|json` (the per-install rows; `result=success` carries `installs format=<csv\|json> rows=<n> truncated=<true\|false>`, `target_id` is the raw query's target, and a post-filter drop on a scope wider than the push-down bound writes a paired `denied` row as for `inventory.software.query`). REST-only (an agent pages the same data through the MCP cursor); machine-scope, so **set-and-proceed**: the file is still served and the response carries `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` (csv; the JSON export sets `audit_persisted: false` in its body instead) when this row cannot persist. Written on every export call that serves a file. `target_id` is the query's target (as for `inventory.software.catalog`); `result=success` carries `titles format=csv rows=<n> truncated=<true\|false>` (`rows` is the number of data rows written, `truncated` is true when the walk reached `limit` (ceiling 10,000), so rows may have been left behind). Same paired-row convention as `inventory.software.query`: a per-device titles export for a device outside the caller's scope writes an empty-file `success` row (`rows=0`) and a paired `result=denied` row in the same request, every time; the per-install export writes its paired `result=denied` row only when the post-filter dropped devices (a scope wider than the push-down bound, where `devices_omitted` is non-zero) or the request named an `agent_id` outside the caller's scope; a store degrade emits `result=failure` (`503`), and so does a fleet export requested before the first catalogue refresh (`detail` says `building`; `503` with `retry_after_ms`, no file). `result=denied` is also written for a management-group-confined caller asking for the fleet export (under this verb), and for a service-scoped token on `?format=csv` (under `inventory.software.catalog`, `403`, `target_id=fleet`, written before the query is parsed). A gate-level denial is audited under `auth.fleet_read_required`, as for the catalogue. `Sec-Audit-Failed` is also set when only the `denied` row of a paired out-of-scope export failed to persist. Gate `Inventory:Read`. |
| `inventory.device.software` | Agent | An operator opened the **installed software** lens of a device's Hardware CI record — `GET /fragments/hardware/ci?id=<agent_id>&lens=software`. Every other CI-record lens emits `inventory.device.ci` instead; this verb is the `lens=software` case only. Emitted through `emit_behavioral_audit` on the **behavioural-PII audit tier** (the HTML fragment is set-and-proceed: it renders and carries `Sec-Audit-Failed` if the row cannot persist). `target_type=Agent`, `target_id` is the `agent_id`; `result` is `success`, or `failure` when the device roster read failed; `detail` is `hardware CI lens=software state=found\|absent\|store degraded` (with ` await=<n>` on a sync-now poll), or `state=roster unreadable` (roster read failed and no CI record could be shown) / `state=found identity=roster unreadable` (CI record served, identity degraded); `state` is the state of the device's **CI record** (`device_ci`), not of the installed-software read itself, so `state=found` does not say whether the software read succeeded). Gated **per device** on `Inventory:Read` for that device's management group (`scoped_perm_fn` — the tier + management-group chokepoint), so an operator only reads a device in their scope. |
| `inventory.devices` | Inventory | An operator opened the **Hardware** list (`GET /fragments/hardware/list`) or called `GET /api/v1/hardware` — the device roster (hostnames + agent_ids + OS + online/last-seen) **plus the device-CI columns** (serial, model, CPU, RAM) joined from `DeviceInventoryStore`. Because serial is a device-persistent identifier (GDPR personal data per ADR-0016), the `result=success` row is emitted via the **behavioural-PII audit tier** (`emit_behavioral_audit`): the REST route **fails closed** (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed`, nothing served) when the row cannot persist, the HTML fragment is **set-and-proceed** (renders, carries `Sec-Audit-Failed`). Gate `require_fleet_read` (`Inventory:Read`, admit-then-filter): per-operator management-group confinement of the roster is **effective**; a gate-level denial is audited under `auth.fleet_read_required`, not this verb. `target_type=Inventory`, `target_id=fleet`; `result=success` carries `hardware list[ (REST)]: devices=<n> omitted=<m>` (`omitted` = rows outside the caller's scope); `result=failure` carries `hardware roster provider unwired` (persisted best-effort via `try_persist_audit`, no `Sec-Audit-Failed`; REST answers `503` for the unwired provider itself, not for the audit) or `hardware roster read failed` (the roster store read failed; emitted via `emit_behavioral_audit`, so a persist failure carries `Sec-Audit-Failed`; the REST twin answers `503` with `retry_after_ms`, the fragment renders the "roster unavailable" banner). (Distinct from `inventory.device.ci` / `inventory.device.software`, the per-device record verbs.) **MCP emitter:** the tool `list_hardware_ci` is a third emitter of this verb, behind the same `Inventory:Read` fleet-read gate and with the same `target_type=Inventory`, `target_id=fleet`. `result=success` carries `hardware list (MCP): devices=<n> omitted=<m>` for a global-read caller (`devices` is the number of rows matching the filters before paging, `omitted` as above, always 0 there) and `hardware list (MCP): devices=<n> scope=authority_scoped` for a confined caller, whose hidden-device count is deliberately not recorded; `result=failure` carries `hardware provider unwired` (the tool answers `-32603`) or `hardware roster read failed` (`-32603` with `retry_after_ms`). The MCP emitter writes through `try_persist_audit` (MCP has no response-header channel, so there is no `Sec-Audit-Failed`) and **fails closed**: it attempts BOTH this row and the generic `mcp.list_hardware_ci` row (below), and if either does not persist the data is withheld and the call answers `-32603` `audit subsystem unavailable, request not served`. That is stricter than the set-and-proceed MCP default (`audit_persisted:false`). A refused argument writes only the generic row, never this one. If this row persists but the paired generic `mcp.list_hardware_ci` row cannot be written, the response is withheld yet a lone `success` row remains: the log can over-record a read, never under-record one, and each retry adds another such row, so count served reads from the pair, not from this row alone. |
| `inventory.device.ci` | Agent | An operator opened **one device's Hardware CI record** — `GET /fragments/hardware/ci?id=<agent_id>` for **every lens except `software`** (overview, tags, dex, guardian, live, actions, or any unrecognised value; `lens=software` emits `inventory.device.software`) — or called `GET /api/v1/hardware/{id}`. The record carries device-persistent identifiers (GDPR personal data per ADR-0016: serial, system UUID, MACs), so this uses the **behavioural-PII audit tier** (`emit_behavioral_audit`) — same as `dex.device.view`/`tar.dns.read`/`tar.arp.read`. `target_type=Agent`, `target_id` is the `agent_id`; `result` is `success`, or `failure` when the device roster read failed; `detail` is `hardware CI lens=<lens> state=found\|absent\|store degraded` (` await=<n>` appended on a sync-now poll) for the fragment, `hardware CI record (REST) state=<…>` for REST. When the device roster read failed, `state` is `roster unreadable` (nothing else could be shown, which the two surfaces judge differently. REST: the device was not otherwise found, meaning a CI record that is readable and absent, no software and no tags, and the answer is `503`, not `404`. Dashboard fragment: no CI record could be shown, meaning CI absent or the CI store degraded, whatever software or tags exist) or `<found\|absent\|store degraded> identity=roster unreadable` (a record was served with a degraded identity: REST `200` with `identity_state: degraded`; the dashboard fragment emits only the `found` variant). `result` reflects only the roster read: with a healthy roster these verbs record `success` and `state=store degraded` or `absent` for the CI store. `absent` = the device hasn't completed its first `device_ci` sync (not a failure); `store degraded` = the store could not be read. The fragment is gated **per device** on `Inventory:Read` for that device's management group (`scoped_perm_fn`) and is set-and-proceed; REST is gated by `require_fleet_read` scope (an out-of-scope id is `404`) and **fails closed** (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed`) when the row cannot persist. **MCP emitter:** the tool `get_hardware_ci` is a third emitter of this verb, gated by the same `Inventory:Read` fleet-read scope, with `target_type=Agent` and `target_id` the `agent_id`. Its `detail` is `hardware CI record (MCP) state=<found\|absent\|store degraded>`, with ` identity=roster unreadable` appended (and `result=failure`) when the record is served with a degraded identity, or `hardware CI record (MCP) state=roster unreadable` with `result=failure` when nothing could be shown (the tool answers `-32603` with `retry_after_ms`, never a not-found). The MCP tool also writes `hardware CI record (MCP) state=software or tags unreadable` with `result=failure` when the device is absent from the roster, its CI row answered absent, and the software or tag read could not be completed: that case is a retryable `-32603` too, not a terminal not-found (the REST route answers 404 there). An unwired provider writes `result=failure` `hardware provider unwired` (empty `target_id`). The MCP emitter **fails closed** exactly as the REST route does: it attempts BOTH this row and the generic `mcp.get_hardware_ci` row (below), and if either does not persist the record is withheld and the call answers `-32603` `audit subsystem unavailable, request not served`. A refused argument, an out-of-scope id or a not-found id writes only the generic row, not this one. As for `inventory.devices`, a lone `success` row can accompany a withheld response when the paired generic row cannot be written. |
| `inventory.sync.request` | Agent | An operator clicked **Sync now** on a device's Hardware CI record (or called `POST /api/v1/hardware/{id}/sync` directly) to request an out-of-cycle daily-sync push for one device instead of waiting for the ~24h cadence. Gated on `Execution:Execute` scoped to the device (`scoped_perm_fn`) — a sync-on-demand is a device action, not an inventory read. `target_id` is the `agent_id`; `source` (body field, default `all`) is one of `installed_software` / `app_perf` / `device_ci` / `software_licensing` / `all`. `result` and `detail` vary by outcome: `result=denied` for a non-JSON-object body (`detail=malformed JSON body`, `400`), a malformed `source` (`detail=bad source=<value>`, `400`, or `detail=source field is not a string`, `400`), or an agent whose reported version predates sync-on-demand (`detail=agent_version=<v> predates sync-on-demand`, `409` — needs 0.13.1 or later); `result=failure` (`detail=sync dispatch unwired`, `503`) if the server has no sync-dispatch mechanism wired; `result=no_agents` if the agent isn't currently connected (`detail=agent_offline`, `503`); `result=requested` (`detail=source=<source>`, `202`) once every precondition passes (a device id over 256 bytes or containing a byte below `0x20` is refused with `400` before the permission check and writes no audit row), audited **before** the command is dispatched to the agent — `emit_behavioral_audit` (the same helper `device.live.*` uses) fails the request CLOSED with `503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed` if this row cannot persist, and the command is never dispatched in that case. If the registry then refuses the dispatch itself (agent connected per the roster but unreachable), the response is `503` with no second audit row — the `requested` row is the durable evidence that the operator asked; whether the agent was actually reached is visible only in the HTTP response (`command_id` is minted by dispatch and returned in the response body, never persisted to the audit row), the same one-row-per-request convention `/api/v1/dex/devices/{id}/live` uses. **Fan-out form:** the Software page's **Sync now** (`POST /api/v1/hardware/sync`) is gated on a global `Execution:Execute` grant (an operator limited to management groups is refused before anything is written here) and confines its targets to the caller's visible set. It writes the same verb once per request with `target_type=Scope` and `target_id` of `__all__`, `group:<id>` or `expression` (a single named device is `target_type=Agent` with the device id). `detail` is `scope=<scope, at most 128 bytes; control bytes, spaces, = and , shown as _ so it cannot forge a neighbouring field> arm=<broadcast|group|scope|ids> requested=<n> reached=<n> skipped_offline=<n> skipped_version=<n>`, and the row is written **before** anything is dispatched; if it cannot persist the request fails closed with `503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed` and nothing is sent. `reached` in the row is the PLAN (the devices this run will send to); the `202` body's `agents_reached` is the number of agents that accepted the request afterwards. A run reaches agents attached to the server that handled it. `skipped_offline` counts the devices this server process did not reach that are not too old: it has no live session for them (a disconnected device, or one attached to another server), or the send was refused by the live stream (for example a gateway-attached agent whose gateway lacks the dispatch capability). Which devices are candidates depends on the scope: a group or a single named device lists its disconnected members too, but an `__all__` or scope-expression run lists only this server's own sessions plus the shared presence record, so a device that is offline everywhere is never counted, and if the presence read is degraded an `__all__` run lists fewer devices (those attached to other servers are not listed) while a scope-expression run is refused with 503 (the scope could not be evaluated) and sends nothing. `requested` and `skipped_offline` therefore describe what this server could reach; they are not a census of the fleet. The row is written before dispatch, so its `skipped_offline` does not yet include sends refused afterwards; the `202` body's does. `result=requested` when at least one device will be contacted, `no_agents` when none can be (still a `202`); `result=denied` for a malformed body (`detail=bad request: ...`, `400`), an unparseable scope (`invalid_scope`, `400`), `fleet_wide_required` (`403`, the whole fleet named without an unfiltered grant), `over_cap cap=500` (`413`, raised at the 501st reachable target, so no count is recorded; the attempt spends the 60 s cooldown), `rate_limited cooldown=<s>s` (`429`) and `run_in_flight` (`409`); `result=failure` when the scope could not be evaluated (`scope_unresolved reason=...`, `503`) or the route is missing a required component (`detail=sync fan-out unwired`, `500`, `target_id` empty). `result=failure` with `detail=dispatch_threw ... scope=...` records an unexpected error while sending (answered `503`, the cooldown is not spent). Counts only are returned to the caller, never device ids. The MCP tool `sync_hardware_software` writes this same domain row and also its generic `mcp.sync_hardware_software` row. |
| `csrf.denied` (hardware sync) | Endpoint | A browser (session-cookie) `POST /api/v1/hardware/sync` or `POST /api/v1/hardware/{id}/sync` carried no `Origin`/`Referer`, or one naming another site, and was refused with `403` by this route's own copy of the check (the server-wide check answers a cookie-session caller first), which runs after the permission check and before the request was parsed or audited as a sync. `target_id` is `hardware.sync` (fleet route) or `hardware.device_sync` (per-device route). Token callers (API, MCP, engine) are exempt. |
| `hardware.actions.view` | Agent | An operator opened the **Actions** lens on a device's Hardware CI record (`/fragments/hardware/ci/actions`) — the generic action-runner catalogue: every one of a connected agent's loaded-plugin actions, classified by capability, with parameter-hint forms for the ones this deployment recognises. Gated **per device** on `Inventory:Read` (`scoped_perm_fn`); viewing the catalogue itself additionally needs `Execution:Execute` for the device — an operator with only `Inventory:Read` sees a "needs Execute" placeholder instead, which is **not** audited (only a rendered catalogue is). `target_id` is the `agent_id`; `result=success` carries `detail=actions catalogue: <n> rows`. Actually **running** one of the listed actions is a separate event — dispatch goes through the existing `POST /api/command` route (and its own audit), so this verb records only that the catalogue was viewed. |
| `hardware.action.result` | Agent | A poll of a dispatched Actions-lens command's result (`/fragments/hardware/ci/result`), audited once the poll reaches a **terminal** phase — rendered output, a renderable-but-empty result, a failed command, or a timeout — never on an in-flight "still waiting" tick. Gated the same as `hardware.actions.view` (`Inventory:Read` scoped to the device) plus a per-poll `Execution:Execute` probe; a caller without Execute sees an explanatory placeholder instead, unaudited. `target_id` is the `agent_id`; `result` is always `success` — it records that the result **view was rendered** to the operator, not that the underlying dispatched action itself succeeded — with `detail=<plugin> command_id=<id> phase=<n>` (`n` is the numeric result phase: `1`=rendered, `2`=rendered-empty, `3`=failed, `4`=timed out). The plugin the poll looks for is taken from the `command_id` on the server (a caption parameter that disagrees, or a prefix that names no known plugin, is a `400`); the label a stored reply carries is the server's own dispatch record, not the id. For a Forensics-tier plugin's reply, a caller who does not hold a global `Forensics:Read` gets a permission notice instead of the reply, never a timeout, and a poll that renders such a reply also writes `response.forensics.read` (below). The permission notice, and the notice shown when the caller's permission could not be confirmed, leave no audit row of their own. |
| `csrf.denied` (runtime config) | Endpoint | A browser (session-cookie) `PUT /api/config/{key}` that carried no `Origin`/`Referer`, or one naming another site (and not a `--csrf-trusted-origin` entry). `target_id` is `config`. The call answers `403` and changes nothing; API, MCP and engine token callers are exempt. For a cookie-session caller the `csrf.denied` (central gate) row is written instead. |
| `csrf.denied` (DEX score policy) | Endpoint | A browser (session-cookie) `PUT` or `DELETE /api/v1/dex/score-policy` that carried no `Origin`/`Referer`, or one naming another site (and not a `--csrf-trusted-origin` entry), was refused with `403` by this route's own copy of the check (the server-wide check answers a cookie-session caller first), before the policy document was parsed or applied. `target_id` is `dex_score_policy`. API, MCP and engine token callers are exempt. For a cookie-session caller the `csrf.denied` (central gate) row is written instead, with `target_id` `PUT /api/v1/dex/score-policy` or `DELETE /api/v1/dex/score-policy`. In production the central-gate row is the one written for a cookie session; this row exists only if the central gate is bypassed. |
| `dex.score_policy.update` / `dex.score_policy.reset` / `dex.score_policy.validate` | RuntimeConfig | `PUT` (`update`), `DELETE` (`reset`) and `PUT ?dry_run=true` (`validate`) on `/api/v1/dex/score-policy` (`Infrastructure:Write`); `target_id` is `dex_score_policy`. Results: `denied` for every refusal of the request (a missing or malformed `If-Match`, a bad `dry_run`, an invalid document, a stale revision), with `reason` `if_match_missing`, `if_match_malformed`, `dry_run_unsupported`, `dry_run_invalid`, `invalid` or `stale`; a real write is audit-first: `attempted` is stored BEFORE the store is touched (if it cannot be stored the call is refused `503` with `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` and nothing changes), then `success`, or `failure` (`reason` `stale`, `writer_busy`, `commit_refused` or `outcome_unknown`); a `reset` of a policy that was never written records `attempted` then `no_change`; `validate` records one `success` row (a row the audit store refuses is a `503`). `detail` is a JSON object `{if_match, prior_revision, revision, ...}`: `revision` is the revision of the canonical text that was (or, for `validate`, would be) stored (`default` for a `reset`). The whole canonical `document` is recorded only on the `attempted`, `success` and `validate` rows (`null` for a `reset`); every other row (`denied`, `failure`, `no_change`) carries `document_bytes` instead, the size in bytes of the canonical text proposed (`null` when no document was validated, as for a `reset` or a refusal before the document was checked), so a refusal made before the `attempted` row (a stale `If-Match` included) never repeats the document in the audit store, while a request that fails after it leaves one full `attempted` row plus one lean row. A real `PUT` writes two rows that carry the document (`attempted` and `success`), also when the document is identical to the stored one, and a dry run writes one (`validate`); the document is at most about 185 KB (about 130 KB at the override limit), and rows are kept for the audit retention period (365 days by default), so size the audit store for repeated saves. `reason`, `field` (the offending path of a refused document) and `dry_run` appear where they apply: `dry_run` is `true` on the `validate` row and on the `denied` row of a refused dry run, so a count of refused updates can exclude refused dry runs, and it holds the neutralised offending value when `dry_run` itself is what was refused. An `outcome_unknown` `failure` row records that the store reported an error after the write was sent and the re-read did not show the requested state: it carries `observed_revision` (the revision then stored) or `reread_error` (the re-read failed), and never asserts that the write did not apply. A `success` row whose `detail` carries `outcome` `observed_after_store_error` means the requested state was observed after a store error; it shows the desired state is in force, not that this request's commit made it so. A lone `attempted` row with no outcome row means the result is unknown: `GET /api/v1/dex/score-policy` and compare its revision; the current state does not prove whether this request committed it. To follow the chain of applied changes, use `success` rows of verb `update` or `reset` only: the `prior_revision` of one equals the `revision` of the last earlier such row. `denied`, `failure` and `validate` rows also carry a PROPOSED `revision`, so they must not be used to follow the chain. Every row is stamped from the session the route resolved. `Sec-Audit-Failed: true` on the response means a row did not persist: on a `200` the outcome row is missing and only the `attempted` row stands: for a `PUT`, or a `DELETE` that removed a policy, the change was applied and a retry with the old `If-Match` answers `409`; a `DELETE` of a policy that was never written changed nothing and may be repeated. `GET` to confirm the state, treat the `attempted` row as the sole evidence, and check the health of the audit store; on a refusal the `denied` or `failure` row of that refusal is missing. `GET` is not audited; a missing permission is the standing `auth.permission_required` row. |
| `config.update` | RuntimeConfig | An operator wrote a runtime-config key via `PUT /api/config/:key`, its twin `PUT /api/v1/config/{param}`, or the MCP tool `set_runtime_config` (`Infrastructure:Write`; the three write the same rows, and an MCP row is followed by the usual `mcp.set_runtime_config` row). The write is audit-first: a row with result `attempted` and an empty `detail` is stored before anything changes (if it cannot be stored the call is refused `503` and nothing changes), then a `success` row, or a `failure` row when the store refuses the value (`detail` is the store's refusal text, for example the 30 second floor, never the submitted value; a database error records `store error; outcome unknown`). A lone `attempted` row with no outcome row means the result is unknown: read `GET /api/config` (see the PUT section of the REST reference). An unknown key, or a body or integer that fails validation, is refused before any row is written. A browser call from another site is refused and recorded as `csrf.denied` (`target_type=Endpoint`, `target_id=config`). `target_id` is the key name. For an ordinary key the `success` row's `detail` carries `value=<the new value>` verbatim, so this row is the change-management evidence for what a setting was changed to and by whom. **For a secret-valued key (today `oidc_client_secret`) `detail` never carries the real value** - the write path records `value=<redacted>`, and `AuditStore::query()` additionally redacts the `detail` of any *pre-existing* `config.update` row naming a secret key **on read**, so rows written before that fix shipped no longer disclose the plaintext through `GET /api/v1/audit`, the legacy `GET /api/audit`, or the MCP `query_audit_log` tool (every reader shares the one read path). The stored row on disk is left unchanged - an audit row is evidence, so this redacts what a caller *sees* rather than rewriting history. If you set `oidc_client_secret` before that fix, rotate it: the value was disclosed while it was being recorded in the clear. **Rotating durably takes more than one step, and rotating through Settings alone breaks the next restart** — see [`upgrading.md`](upgrading.md) ("Security: rotate `oidc_client_secret` if it was ever set on this install") for what to remediate, and [`authentication.md`](authentication.md) ("OIDC Single Sign-On") for the durable and non-durable ways to configure it. The note below about Settings is about where the audit evidence lands, not how to rotate. `result=denied` (HTTP 403, nothing stored, no preceding `attempted` row) when the caller rule refuses the write; it is decided before the store is opened and before the body is parsed. For an `oidc_*` key the caller's own role must be `admin`: `detail=elevated_session_oidc_setting_blocked` for an administrator only through a temporary elevation, `detail=admin_role_required_oidc_setting_blocked` for a non-administrator holding `Infrastructure:Write`, `detail=token_session_oidc_setting_blocked` for an MCP-tier token or a service-scoped token (whatever its creator's role). For the seven administrator-only keys (`plugin_signing_required`, `dex_alert_routing`, `dex_blast_min_devices`, `dex_blast_window_seconds`, `dex_blast_cooldown_seconds`, `dex_cohort_export_key`, and `audit_retention_days`, which has no Settings route and is administrator-only by design because it is the stored audit-retention setting, which is stored only today: the enforced window is `--audit-retention-days` plus a restart) the caller must hold the `admin` role as an administrator account (a session or an API token that is not MCP-tier; an active elevation counts, as on the Settings pages; the account's own legacy role must be `admin`, an RBAC Administrator grant alone does not count): `detail=token_session_admin_setting_blocked` for an MCP-tier or service-scoped token, `detail=admin_role_required_setting_blocked` for a session whose effective role is not `admin`. In production a service-scoped token and a `readonly` or `operator` MCP token are refused earlier by the permission gate (`auth.permission_required`; on MCP the generic tier and service-scope gates refuse them and leave only an `mcp.set_runtime_config` denied row), so the callers that produce these rows are a supervised-tier MCP token (even one minted by an administrator) and, with RBAC on, a non-administrator role holding `Infrastructure:Write`. **SIEM note:** the Settings routes audit the equivalent denial as `auth.admin_required`, so a rule keyed on `auth.admin_required` does not see a refusal on this route. To find these refusals, query `GET /api/v1/audit?action=config.update&target_type=RuntimeConfig&target_id=<key>` (`target_id` requires `target_type`) and keep rows whose `result` is `denied`, or drop `target_id` to scan every key. Audit rows record `principal`, `principal_role`, `principal_class`, `session_id`, `source_ip` and `user_agent` but not the MCP tier, so a `config.update` row cannot tell an MCP-tier write from an administrator API-token write after the fact. A write through `set_runtime_config` also leaves an `mcp.set_runtime_config` row (same session, same time), which a REST write does not. Note this verb covers the generic config API only; a change made through Settings emits its own verb instead (`settings.dex_alerts.*`, `plugin_signing.require.changed`, and for OIDC `oidc.configure` on `target_type="OidcConfig"` with the ISSUER as `target_id`). **Consequence for rotation evidence:** a Settings-driven OIDC secret rotation is audited as `oidc.configure` and does NOT name `oidc_client_secret` anywhere, so a query for `config.update` + `target_id=oidc_client_secret` finds only rotations done through the generic API. Since Settings is the only path that applies an OIDC change to the running server, expect key-level rotation evidence to be absent for the path operators are told to use. |
| `settings.dex_alerts.cohort_export` | RuntimeConfig | An admin set or cleared the cohort metrics export tag key via the DEX → Alerts tab. `target_id` is `dex_cohort_export_key`; `detail` carries `export_key=<key>` or `export disabled`. The runtime-config store keeps no history, so this row is the change-management evidence for what cohort labels were exported when. |
| `tar.db.corruption_quarantined` | Agent | (SOC 2 CC7.1/CC7.2) An agent reported, via its heartbeat, that its `tar.db` was quarantined as corrupt and re-initialised. Principal `system`, target type `Agent` (`target_id` = the device's agent id), `detail` = `corruption_total=<n> quarantine=<epoch>:<file>`; the paths are `<agent data_dir>/tar.db` and `<agent data_dir>/<file>` (basename only: the heartbeat tag value cap is 64 bytes and the data dir is agent configuration). One row per (agent, quarantine identity) per server process (another HA node ingesting the same agent can add a second row for the same identity): the server dedups against the audit store itself, so store prunes, server restarts and HA node switches never repeat it. At most one row per agent per 10 minutes per server process (in-memory; a restart or another HA node opens a fresh window), so a rotating identity cannot mint a row per heartbeat; intermediate quarantines inside one window collapse to the newest identity, which is logged on the first heartbeat after the window — for TRUE event frequency, trust the agent's own cumulative `heartbeat.db_corruption_total` count (surfaced fleet-wide via `yuzu_fleet_tar_db_corruption_agents`, see the metrics reference), never a row count, which under-represents by design inside a rate-limit window. Derived from heartbeat tags, so it is agent-asserted, not server-verified: this row corroborates that the agent's own software believed it hit corruption; it does not rule out an actor with agent-level access forging the same signal, and should not be presented as conclusive evidence against tampering. |
| `tar.status.scan` | Infrastructure | An operator clicked **Scan fleet** on the TAR dashboard page (`/tar`). A `tar.status` command was dispatched to the operator's visible-agent set. `detail` carries the count of agents the dispatch reached (e.g. `dispatched to 47 agent(s) in scope`). One row per Scan button press; no row on page-load Refresh. |
| `tar.source.reenable` | Infrastructure | An operator clicked **Re-enable** on a row of the TAR retention-paused list and a `tar.configure` command with `<source>_enabled=true` was dispatched to a single device. `detail` carries `device=<id> source=<process|tcp|service|user>` on success. Failures emit `result=failure` with `detail` set to the real reason (`scope_violation source=<src>` for out-of-scope device IDs, `agent_not_connected source=<src>` for connected-but-zero-sent dispatches) so SIEM rules can distinguish a forged-form attempt from a transient connectivity issue — even though the HTTP response body is identical (`Agent not reachable.` 404) for both, to deny enumeration. |
| `tar.source.purge` | Infrastructure | An operator clicked **Purge data** on a row of the TAR retention-paused list (Phase 15.A) and a destructive `tar.purge_source` command was dispatched to a single device — dropping **all** warehouse rows for that source (`<source>_{live,hourly,daily,monthly}`) while leaving the collector **paused**. Gated on `Infrastructure:Delete` (a higher tier than re-enable) plus the operator's RBAC visibility on the device; the operator must type the device hostname to confirm. `detail` carries `device=<id> source=<process\|tcp\|service\|user>` on success (`rbac_denied` on the permission gate; `scope_violation`/`agent_not_connected` on failure, same 404-collapse as re-enable). The agent refuses (`source_not_paused`) if the source is still enabled, or (`config_unreadable`) if its enable flag cannot be read; nothing is purged in either case. `rows_deleted` is computed agent-side and appears in the command's response record, not this dispatch row. The structured REST equivalent (`POST /api/v1/tar/retention-paused/purge`) writes this verb with `result=requested` **before** dispatch (audit-on-open, fail-closed — no dispatch if the row can't persist), plus `detail cid=<correlation-id>`. **Caveat:** a purge issued through the generic `POST /api/command` escape hatch is currently audited under the generic `command.dispatch` verb (with `detail` `tar:purge_source`), **not** this `tar.source.purge` verb — SIEM rules keyed only on `tar.source.purge` will miss that path (tracked in Tr3kkR/Yuzu#1787). |
| `tar.process_tree.read` | Agent | An operator reconstructed a process tree for one device from its local TAR warehouse via the TAR dashboard (`/tar`, Frame 3). **Usage-class behavioral telemetry** — surfaces process names + PIDs + owning user + per-process remote IP:port, and (on Linux/macOS) command lines. Emitted on **two** points so the access is recorded even on failure (parity with `device.live.*`): a **dispatch** row at the live-query send (`result=dispatched` when ≥1 agent reached, `result=no_agents` when offline; `detail` = `dispatch preset=<p> command_id=<id>`), and a **success** row after the tree renders (`detail` = `preset=<p> from=<epoch> to=<epoch> nodes=<n> anomalies=<n> os=<os> conns=<0|1>` — `os` distinguishes a names-only Windows read from a behavioral Linux/macOS read; `conns=1` means per-process connection data was shown). `target_id` is the `agent_id`. Viewing the frame needs `Infrastructure:Read`; reconstruction is Execute-gated (`Execution:Execute`) and scoped to the device's management group. The reconstruction is cached under a CSPRNG token bound to the originating operator. `preset` is canonicalized to a fixed allowlist and `os` is normalized to `{windows,linux,macos,?}` before interpolation, so neither agent- nor operator-controlled input can forge an audit field (#1290 posture). A **failure** row (`detail = csprng_unavailable`) is emitted if secure token generation fails (entropy exhaustion) — no tree is cached. |
| `tar.process_tree.detail` | Agent | An operator opened the detail panel for one process node from a cached reconstruction (a row click in `/tar` Frame 3). **Usage-class** — surfaces that node's path/command line (Linux/macOS), owning user, anomaly evidence, and full per-PID remote IP:port table. One row per drilldown (intentionally per-click, the works-council "who viewed which process" posture; mirrors `dex.signal.view`). `target_id` is the `agent_id`; `detail` = `node=<id> os=<windows\|linux\|macos\|?>`. Gated identically to a reconstruction — `Infrastructure:Read` + `Execution:Execute` scoped to the device — **and** bound to the originating session: a predicted or leaked cache token can't be replayed by a different operator. |
| `tar.dns.read` | Agent | The DNS-cache panel was rendered for a device in the TAR process-tree pane (`GET /fragments/tar/process-tree/device-net`, ADR-0015). **Usage-class telemetry** — the device DNS resolver cache reveals which domains the host resolved (device-level state; **no per-process attribution**). Emitted at **dispatch** (`result=dispatched` when ≥1 agent reached, `result=no_agents` when offline; `detail` = `command_id=<id>`). `target_id` is the `agent_id`. Requires `Infrastructure:Read` + `Execution:Execute` scoped to the device. **Separately countable from `tar.arp.read`** for works-council access-audit purposes. |
| `tar.arp.read` | Agent | The ARP-table panel was rendered for a device in the TAR process-tree pane (same route as `tar.dns.read`, ADR-0015). Surfaces the host ARP / neighbour table (IP↔MAC bindings per interface) — Layer-2 topology, lower sensitivity than DNS but security-relevant (ARP-spoofing forensics). Dispatch-time emission + gating identical to `tar.dns.read`; kept a distinct verb so DNS PII access stays separately countable. |
| `tar.sources.read` | Agent | An operator opened the TAR **Capture sources** frame for a device (`GET /fragments/tar/capture-sources/load`, ADR-0015), dispatching `tar.status` + `tar.compatibility` to read the device's per-source enable/support state. `detail` = `command_id=<id>`. `target_id` is the `agent_id`. Requires `Infrastructure:Read` + `Execution:Execute` scoped to the device. |
| `tar.sources.configure` | Infrastructure | An operator pushed staged capture-source enable/disable changes via the TAR Capture-sources frame (`POST /fragments/tar/capture-sources/push`, ADR-0015) — **one row per changed source** (each a `tar.configure <source>_enabled` dispatch). `detail` = `<source>_enabled=<true\|false> command_id=<id>`. A change to `dns` (usage-class PII) in either direction is captured here. Requires `Infrastructure:Read` + `Execution:Execute` scoped to the device. |
| `execution.visualization.fetch` | Execution | Chart-ready JSON was rendered for an execution's response set via `GET /api/v1/executions/{id}/visualization` (issue #253 / #587). `target_id` is the `command_id`; `detail` carries `<definition_id> index=<N>` on success, or `<definition_id> reason=<r>` on the failure path where `<r>` is one of `missing_definition_id`, `malformed_definition_id`, `definition_not_found`, `no_visualization`, `index_oor`. **Corrected (PR review, 2026-09-01):** the success row no longer carries `scope_dropped` — a scope drop now emits a SEPARATE `result=denied` row on this same action (`detail=<definition_id> scope_dropped=<N>`), paired with the `result=success` row for the same request, matching the shape `server.cpp`'s legacy responses routes and MCP `query_responses`/`aggregate_responses` already use. A SIEM rule must treat `denied`+`success` as a pair for one call, not look for `scope_dropped` inside the success detail. Permission gate: `require_fleet_read("Response","Read")` (#1634 — replaced the flat `Response:Read` gate). **Management-group scoping is effective for normal operators (#1634, closed)** — the visible-agent set is resolved and pushed into the SQL query before the row cap, then the chart transform runs only over in-scope rows; a management-group-confined caller genuinely sees only their in-scope agents' data, and the paired `denied` row fires under ordinary operation whenever a confined caller's execution spans agents outside their groups, not only on RBAC-store corruption. `rows_capped` now reflects the CALLER's own scoped cap hit. The `command.dispatch` event (above, when emitted from the dashboard) appends `definition_id=<id>` to its `detail` when the dashboard reverse-resolves a chart-bearing definition, so SIEM can join `command.dispatch` to the subsequent `execution.visualization.fetch` by `definition_id`. |
| `response.read` | Execution | A management-group scope filter dropped one or more out-of-scope agents' rows from a legacy response read — `GET /api/responses/{id}` (`surface=get`), `GET /api/responses/{id}/aggregate` (`surface=aggregate`), or `GET /api/responses/{id}/export` (`surface=export`) (#1634) — or from the dashboard `POST /api/dashboard/group-from-results` materialiser dropping out-of-scope agent ids before they enter the new management group (`surface=group_from_results`, #1712). Emitted as `result=denied` when a drop occurred. On `group_from_results` that is the ONLY row (the happy, nothing-dropped path is not separately audited there); all three legacy surfaces (`get`, `aggregate`, `export`) and the three v1 surfaces also write a `result=success` row (below); `target_id` is the `instruction_id` on the legacy surfaces or the `command_id` on `group_from_results`; `detail` carries `scope_dropped=<N> surface=<get\|aggregate\|export\|group_from_results>` where `<N>` is the count of DISTINCT out-of-scope agents filtered out. The MCP `aggregate_responses` tool records the equivalent drop as its own `mcp.aggregate_responses` `result=denied` row; the visualization endpoint emits it as a SEPARATE `result=denied` row on `execution.visualization.fetch` (see that row above), not inline in a success detail. Permission gate: `require_fleet_read("Response","Read")` on the legacy `get`/`aggregate`/`export` surfaces (#1634 — replaced the flat gate) — **but `group_from_results` gates on `ManagementGroup:Write`, with `Response:Read` supplying only the visibility filter**, a different combination from the other surfaces this row covers. **Current trigger scope — same shape on every surface now (#1634, closed):** on `get`/`aggregate`/`export`, per-management-group scoping is effective for normal operators — a confined caller is admitted and this verb fires under ordinary operation whenever their execution spans agents outside their groups, not only on RBAC-store corruption. `group_from_results` (#1712) remains the pre-existing exception in gate SHAPE (its admission gate, `ManagementGroup:Write`, is a different permission from the one supplying its scope, `Response:Read`) but not in reachability — both classes of surface now fire this verb under normal operation, not only on store corruption. **Added (#2146 A2-R2):** the REST v1 twins `GET /api/v1/responses/{id}` (`surface=v1_get`), `.../aggregate` (`surface=v1_aggregate`), and `.../export` (`surface=v1_export`) also emit this verb, extending the `surface=` set above. Like the three legacy surfaces (below), these three v1 surfaces additionally emit a **`result=success`** row on every served read (REST fail-closed on an audit-persist failure - a 503 if the audit write itself fails) - so the "`result=denied` only" rule above applies to `group_from_results` only. The success row's `detail` is a DIFFERENT shape from the denied rows' `scope_dropped=<N> surface=<...>` string - it carries no `surface=` token at all, just `"REST v1 response <query\|aggregate\|export> cid=<correlation_id>"` (the three v1 handlers' own action names, not the `v1_get`/`v1_aggregate`/`v1_export` tokens used on the denied path) - a SIEM rule matching on `surface=` will not see the success rows. A SIEM rule keyed on `response.read` must now expect both `result` values: `success` = a v1 or legacy read was served, `denied` = a scope-drop occurred on any of the seven surfaces. A rule that matches on `surface=` sees only the `denied` rows: select success rows with `action=response.read result=success` and tell the route apart by the `detail` prefix (`REST v1 response <query\|aggregate\|export> cid=` for v1, `legacy response <query\|aggregate\|export> cid=` for the legacy routes, where `query` is the legacy `GET /api/responses/{id}`). A SIEM alert keyed on this verb should expect `result=success` rows routinely: one per served read on every legacy and v1 response route for every caller, confined or not; `result=denied` rows appear only when a scope-drop occurred. A request rejected for a malformed numeric query parameter (`400` on REST, `-32602` on MCP `query_responses`, #4644) is **not** audited. **All three legacy routes now write a `result=success` row (#4644)**: `GET /api/responses/{id}` (`detail="legacy response query cid=<correlation_id>"`), `GET /api/responses/{id}/aggregate` (`detail="legacy response aggregate cid=<correlation_id>"`) and `GET /api/responses/{id}/export` (CSV and JSON, `detail="legacy response export cid=<correlation_id>"`) each emit `response.read` / `result=success` / `target_type=Execution` / `target_id=<instruction_id>` on every served read, including an unconfined (global) caller and a read that returns no rows, after the store read and before any body is built, so each is countable together with its v1 twin's row. Before this change a global caller left NO row on `get` and `aggregate`, and a scoped caller left only the scope-drop `denied` row. All three are **fail-closed like the v1 twins**: if the success row, or the scope-drop `denied` row, cannot be persisted, or the audit pipeline throws, the route answers `503` (A4 envelope, `retry_after_ms: 5000`, `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`) and serves no data; the scope-drop `denied` row used to be set-and-proceed on `get` and `aggregate`. A request rejected with a `400` for a malformed parameter writes no row. A `503` for an unavailable or degraded response store writes no success row, and writes no row at all when it happens before any audit row is written: on legacy `get` and `export` both the scope-drop `denied` row and the `success` row are written only after the store read, so a degraded store leaves nothing. On legacy `aggregate` and on all three v1 routes (`v1_get`, `v1_aggregate`, `v1_export`) the scope-drop `denied` row is written BEFORE the main store read, so a confined caller with a drop whose main read then degrades leaves a `denied` row and a `503`, with no `success` row; a failed scope lookup (`distinct_agent_ids`) precedes that row and leaves nothing. |
| `response.forensics.read` | Execution | A read of stored command responses **served rows written by a Forensics-tier plugin** (`app_usage`, `browser_inventory`, `execution_artifacts`, `privacy_permissions`, `remote_access_audit`; the set is derived from the capability catalogue, every plugin whose actions sit on the `Forensics` securable). Written once per request next to the neighboring audit row (after `response.read` / `execution.detail.fetch`; before the `execution.visualization.fetch` success row on the visualization route), and only when the rows actually returned to the caller include at least one such row (a row labelled `~unverified`, a response for which the server holds no dispatch record, counts as one and appears in `plugins=` as `~unverified`): a caller who does not hold `Forensics:Read` is served none and leaves no `response.forensics.read` row, and a request that returns only ordinary-plugin rows leaves none. A caller using an API token, an MCP token or an engine principal that does not hold a global `Forensics:Read` still leaves one `auth.permission_required` denied row (see the authentication guide) per read attempt, so automation that polls stored responses produces denial rows; an interactive browser sign-in (local, OIDC or SAML) does not. Kept as a separate verb so reads of this class of data stay separately countable for works-council access-audit purposes (the same reason `device.live.*` is split per kind). What is counted is a read request that served such rows, not a viewing and not a row: an executions drawer that stays open on a running execution re-fetches its detail fragment after each state-change event (debounced to at most about twice a second), and each refresh that serves such a row writes a row of its own; an export's `<n>` is taken before the export's byte cap trims the file, so it can exceed the rows the file contains. The token-caller denial rows above are one per read attempt, so a token poller that reads once per second leaves about 86,400 `auth.permission_required` rows a day (arithmetic from the one-row-per-attempt rule, not a measurement); size the audit retention and any SIEM ingest for that before such automation runs against a tier it does not hold. `result=success`; `target_type=Execution`, `target_id` is the `execution_id` or `instruction_id` the request named; `detail` is `forensics_rows=<n> plugins=<comma-separated plugin names> surface=<s>`, where `<n>` is the number of served rows and `<s>` is the transport that served them, one of a closed set chosen by the server (never taken from the request): `rest` (the v1 routes), `legacy` (the unversioned `/api/responses` routes), `mcp`, `dashboard` (the results fragment), `drawer` (the executions drawer), `executions_page` (the Executions page detail), `hardware` (the Hardware result poll) or `bundle` (bundle collation), so a dashboard view of the rows can be told from an API read without parsing paths. **Surfaces:** the legacy `GET /api/responses/{id}` and `/export`; REST v1 `GET /api/v1/executions/{id}/responses`, `/api/v1/responses/{id}`, `/api/v1/responses/{id}/export` and `/api/v1/executions/{id}/visualization`; MCP `query_responses`; the dashboard results fragment (rows on the rendered page), the executions drawer / page detail, the Hardware Actions-lens result poll `GET /fragments/hardware/ci/result`, and bundle collation (`GET /api/v1/bundles/{id}`, MCP `get_bundle_result`). On that poll the row is written alongside `hardware.action.result`, only when the output or failure text it renders came from a Forensics-tier plugin, and its `target_id` is the poll's `command_id`; a poll that is still waiting renders no reply and writes none. Aggregate, facet-option and count reads (including `/aggregate`, `aggregate_responses`, the group agent-count preview and the dashboard filter-bar fragment `GET /fragments/results/filter-bar`) write no row; facet options can list column values of Forensics-tier rows to a holder of `Forensics:Read`, and that read is not audited. The dashboard create-group-from-results action (`POST /api/dashboard/group-from-results`) takes its membership from the agents that match its filters over the rows the caller may read; it writes its own `group.create_from_results` row but no `response.forensics.read` row, even when those rows are Forensics-tier ones (a documented gap in audit coverage). **Bundle collation (`surface=bundle`):** a collation writes the row only when at least one step that responded was run by a Forensics-tier plugin, `<n>` counts those responded steps, and `target_id` is the bundle id; it is written next to the existing `bundle.collate` row (after it, on REST). A collation for a caller without `Forensics:Read` shows such a step as pending, serves none, and writes none; a bundle with no Forensics-tier step writes none. Every poll of a bundle that serves such a step writes the row again. **Persistence by surface:** the REST routes write this row before any body is returned, and fail closed (`503` + `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`, no data served) when it cannot persist, so the neighboring row can already exist for a request that served nothing and a retry writes it again; MCP serves the data and reports `audit_persisted: false` (on `get_bundle_result`, only for this row; the existing `bundle.collate` row keeps its previous behavior); the dashboard fragments, including the Hardware result poll, serve the page and set `Sec-Audit-Failed: true`. |
| `execution.read` | Execution | A management-group-confined caller was refused a legacy `/api/executions/{id}` read — `GET .../{id}` (`surface=detail`), `.../summary` (`surface=summary`), `.../agents` (`surface=agents`), or `.../children` (`surface=children`) (#3789). Emitted **only when the requesting caller's fleet-read scope was engaged** (`require_fleet_read`'s `gate.scope`) — an UNCONFINED caller's plain "this id has never existed" 404 is not a confinement decision and writes no row, so this verb's rate is a measure of confinement actually firing, not of ordinary 404 traffic (a caller-global-grant/admin holder therefore never appears here for a bad id). `GET /api/executions` (list) never emits this verb — its confinement is a silent SQL `WHERE`-clause narrowing with no per-row audit, same as every other pushdown-filtered list read. `target_id` is the execution id; `detail="not found or outside caller's fleet-read scope surface=<detail\|summary\|agents\|children> cid=<correlation_id>"` — the detail is deliberately IDENTICAL whether the execution never existed or exists but is fully out of scope (a distinguishing detail string would be a same-response-shape oracle a confined caller could probe). `result=denied`. **Two more emitters (#2146 A2-R1), same verb, different detail shape:** REST v1's `GET /api/v1/executions/{id}/children` twin reuses the identical `detail="... surface=children cid=<correlation_id>"` string (indistinguishable in the audit trail from the legacy `/children` route's own denial); MCP's `get_execution_children` tool also denies under this SAME verb but with its own detail text, `detail="MCP children query: not found or outside caller's fleet-read scope"` (no `surface=`/`cid=` tokens - MCP has no per-request correlation id to embed here). Permission gate: `require_fleet_read("Execution","Read")` (#3789 — replaced the flat `Execution:Read` gate on these four routes; the two new #2146 A2-R1 emitters use the same gate). |
| `execution.rerun` | execution (lowercase — matches this verb's pre-existing `success`/`failure` rows, NOT the `Execution`-capitalized convention `execution.read` and most other verbs in this table use; a `target_type=Execution` SIEM filter will not match any row this verb ever writes) | `POST /api/executions/{id}/rerun`. `result=success`: `target_id` is the NEW rerun execution's id (not the original). `result=denied` (#3789, confined callers only — an unconfined caller's failed rerun falls through unaudited to `create_rerun`'s own pre-existing 400, unchanged from pre-#3789 behavior): the original execution failed the mutation-admission rule in `execution_scope_rules.hpp` (`admit_confined_mutation` — nonexistent, zero-visible, an incomplete target-response ledger, or any out-of-scope targeted agent all collapse to this SAME outcome, by design, to avoid a hidden-cohort oracle); `target_id` is the ORIGINAL execution id; `detail="not found or outside caller's fleet-read scope cid=<correlation_id>"`. Permission gate: `require_permission("Execution","Execute")` ahead of `require_fleet_read("Execution","Read")` for scope (the fleet gate is structurally Read-only, `authz_gates.cpp`, and cannot replace the Execute check). |
| `execution.cancel` | execution (lowercase — same casing anomaly as `execution.rerun` above) | `POST /api/executions/{id}/cancel`. `result=success`/`result=failure` cover the pre-#3789 `mark_cancelled` outcome, unchanged. `result=denied` (#3789, confined callers only — see `execution.rerun` above for the identical admission rule and no-oracle rationale) uses the same `target_id`/`detail` shape as `execution.rerun`'s denied row. An UNCONFINED caller hitting a genuinely nonexistent id also gets a plain `404` with **no** audit row — the response CODE differs from `execution.rerun`'s unconfined-not-found `400` (see above), but both write no audit row in this case. This closes what `mark_cancelled` alone would otherwise report as a false `success` on a zero-row UPDATE. Permission gate: same as `execution.rerun`. **A second, system-initiated source (#4982):** the clock-guarded `reap_stuck_running_executions()` background sweep (~15-minute cadence, see `docs/clock-guarded-retention.md`) also writes this action when it force-cancels an execution permanently stranded at `status='running'`/`agents_targeted=0` — `principal=system` (no HTTP session/token principal exists for a background writer), `target_id`=the execution id, `result=success`, `detail=reap_stuck_running_executions` (distinguishing it from the operator route's rows, which carry no `detail`). A background actor silently transitioning a command's terminal state would otherwise leave no audit trail at all — see `docs/user-manual/metrics.md`'s `yuzu_exec_tracker_stuck_reap_total` for the paired real-time signal. |
